### Assemblies/CouncilTroopDeliveryTests.cs

_The mod's CouncilTroopDeliveryBehavior persists a List<CouncilTroopDelivery> as byte[] JSON via Newtonsoft.Json, mirroring CompanionMissionBehavior's and CouncilStipendBehavior's own SyncData shape exactly (idempotent load, graceful on missing/corrupt, see CouncilStipendTests.cs's own header for why the mod project itself is integration-only here). What IS unit-testable, and IS the real save contract the behavior depends on, is the DTO's own round trip through the same serializer: a delegation is a real party physically travelling the map with someone else's troops already loaded aboard it (Gabriel's condition B, a PERMANENT gift once it departs) - a save made mid-journey corrupting any one of these fields would either strand the escort forever (lost PartyId) or misattribute/miscount the troops on arrival (lost tier counts or donor id)._

- GIVEN an in flight delivery with an escort leader, WHEN round tripped through the same serializer the behavior uses, THEN every field survives
- GIVEN a delivery with no escort leader recorded, WHEN round tripped, THEN the null survives
- GIVEN no active deliveries, WHEN round tripped, THEN it is an empty list not null
- GIVEN corrupt json, WHEN deserialized, THEN it throws so the behaviors own catch path can fall back

### Assemblies/CouncilTroopLoanPolicyTests.cs

_CouncilTroopLoanPolicy decides HOW MANY troops, split by tier, a lord's party parts with for a council lend_troops resolution. Gabriel's own written CONDITIONS (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md) are authoritative and are what these tests pin: (C) "jamais plus de 25% du party du Lord. Doit avoir une distribution de tiers avec faible, moyen, haut" - a hard 25% ceiling AND a genuine tier mix, never a pure levy dump, whenever the party actually fields higher tiers. Get the cap wrong and a council pledge could gut a lord's real fighting strength (or a battle-deciding windfall for the player); get the mix wrong and the promise reads as "troops" while every soldier delivered is an unarmoured levy, the exact LLM-says/game-does gap this whole project exists to close._

- GIVEN a pure low tier party of various sizes, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN it never exceeds 25 percent
- GIVEN a party too small to spare a quarter, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN it lends nothing
- GIVEN an empty party, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN it lends nothing
- GIVEN a mostly low tier party with a thin sliver of medium, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN at least one medium troop is included
- GIVEN a mostly low tier party with a thin sliver of high tier, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN at least one high tier troop is included
- GIVEN a party with a clean proportional mix, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN the split matches the proportions
- GIVEN a party split evenly across all three tiers, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN the loan is also even
- GIVEN a party dominated by its high tier, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN the dominant tier is represented
- GIVEN a party with only one tier present, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN it lends only from that tier up to the cap
- GIVEN a bucket constructed with a negative count, WHEN read back, THEN it clamps to zero
- GIVEN a wide spread of party shapes, WHEN the loan is computed, THEN it never exceeds the cap or any tiers own count

### Assemblies/CouncilVenuePolicyTests.cs

_Documents the venue pick for a settlement-hosted council: which interior the player actually walks into when convening. Wrong here means the player either gets locked out of a settlement that DOES have a usable room, or gets shoved into a hall they do not own, which would read as a bug in the "reuse the proven duel scene" recipe._

- GIVEN the player owns the settlement and it has a lordshall, WHEN choosing a venue, THEN the hall is chosen
- GIVEN the player is a guest and the settlement has a tavern, WHEN choosing a venue, THEN the tavern is chosen
- GIVEN the player owns the settlement but it has no lordshall, WHEN choosing a venue, THEN the tavern is chosen as fallback
- GIVEN the player is a guest and the settlement has no tavern but has a lordshall, WHEN choosing a venue, THEN the hall is chosen as fallback
- GIVEN the settlement has neither a lordshall nor a tavern, WHEN choosing a venue, THEN none is chosen
- GIVEN each venue, WHEN asked for its location id, THEN the engine ids map correctly

### Assemblies/CouncilWardTests.cs

_The mod's CouncilWardBehavior persists a List<CouncilWard> as byte[] JSON via Newtonsoft.Json, mirroring CouncilStipendBehavior's/CouncilTributeBehavior's/CouncilHostageBehavior's own SyncData shape exactly (idempotent load, graceful on missing/corrupt). The mod project (CampaignBehaviorBase, IDataStore, Hero) is not referenced by this test project, so the behavior's actual SyncData call is integration-only (see project_testing_architecture: mod = engine, untestable here). What IS unit-testable, and IS the real save contract the behavior depends on, is the DTO's own round trip through the same serializer: these tests pin that a bond's mentor/protege ids survive a save/load cycle intact, since a save made mid-bond corrupting either id would silently orphan the mentorship (or worse, misattribute it to the wrong pair of heroes)._

- GIVEN an active bond, WHEN round tripped through the same serializer the behavior uses, THEN every field survives
- GIVEN no active bonds, WHEN round tripped, THEN it is an empty list not null
- GIVEN corrupt json, WHEN deserialized, THEN it throws so the behaviors own catch path can fall back

### Assemblies/DispatchGroundingCodecTests.cs

_How a council's dispatch carries the place of a patrol through the ledger's one grounding slot (jaserms1, 16/10/2026: a war council's patrol order did nothing). Wrong, the patrol rides to the wrong place or nowhere, or every errand pledged before the patrol existed stops resolving after an update._

- GIVEN a patrol of a named place, WHEN recorded and read back, THEN both the mission and the place survive
- GIVEN an errand recorded before patrols existed, WHEN read back, THEN it is the errand alone
- GIVEN a patrol with no place, WHEN read back, THEN the place is missing not guessed
- GIVEN the word patrol, WHEN parsed, THEN it is the patrol mission

### Assemblies/EngagementLedgerTests.cs

_The engagements ledger is the live MIRROR of a council conversation's standing decisions: what the right-hand column shows, and what applies to the world when the council lifts. It has no cancel button by design (Gabriel ruled that an immersion break, 2026-07-30); a decision is walked back purely by the conversation no longer asserting it, so the extraction snapshot drops it and this ledger must drop it too. What breaks if reconcile is wrong: a decision the table rescinded would still be on the panel and would still apply at the lift (the exact dishonesty the "player sees it, then it applies" rule exists to prevent), or a decision restated each turn would pile up as duplicate rows. Gabriel asked specifically for the appears-then-retraction pair pinned here._

- GIVEN a declaration in the snapshot, WHEN reconciling, THEN it appears in the ledger
- GIVEN a standing declaration, WHEN a later snapshot no longer carries it, THEN it is removed
- GIVEN the same declaration across two snapshots, WHEN reconciling twice, THEN it is not duplicated
- GIVEN a discussed declaration, WHEN it becomes agreed in a later snapshot, THEN its status moves in place
- GIVEN a mix of discussed and agreed, WHEN asking what commits, THEN only the agreed ones are returned
- GIVEN snapshot items with no key or no text, WHEN reconciling, THEN they are ignored

### Assemblies/EngagementSealTextTests.cs

_Documents the durable memory wording the Engagements apply-at-End seals onto a hero at council close. Real world-effect executors do not exist yet for a council engagement (only a bandit-clear quest is executable elsewhere in this codebase), so this phrasing IS the applied effect: what breaks if it drifts is either a decision misattributed as the player's own promise when it was collective, or the reverse._

- GIVEN a decision bound to a seated subject, WHEN sealed, THEN it reads in the first person
- GIVEN a decision with no bound subject, WHEN sealed, THEN it reads as a collective settlement
- GIVEN blank text, WHEN sealed, THEN it returns empty
- GIVEN text already ending in a period, WHEN sealed, THEN the period is not doubled

### Assemblies/EngagementSnapshotParserTests.cs

_The parser is the ONLY thing standing between raw LLM output and EngagementLedger.Reconcile: the ledger mirror, and thus what applies to the world at the lift, is only as honest as this parse. A throw here would kill a live council turn; a mis-mapped status would apply a decision the table never actually settled; a lost well-formed head to a malformed tail would make the panel flicker away decisions the player just watched form._

- GIVEN a clean json array of two items, WHEN parsing, THEN both map to snapshot items
- GIVEN a markdown fenced array, WHEN parsing, THEN it still parses
- GIVEN prose surrounding the array, WHEN parsing, THEN the array is still located and parsed
- GIVEN a status string, WHEN parsing, THEN it maps case insensitively and defaults to discussed
- GIVEN an element missing id or with blank decision, WHEN parsing, THEN it is skipped
- GIVEN null or blank input, WHEN parsing, THEN an empty list is returned
- GIVEN input that is not json, WHEN parsing, THEN an empty list is returned
- GIVEN a truncated array missing its closing bracket, WHEN parsing, THEN an empty list is returned
- GIVEN a well formed head followed by a malformed element, WHEN parsing, THEN the head survives and nothing throws
- GIVEN a malformed array with no well formed head, WHEN parsing, THEN an empty list is returned

### Assemblies/EngagementSubjectMatcherTests.cs

_Documents the tolerant subject-to-seat match the Engagements apply-at-End seal depends on. What breaks without it: a wrong match seals a decision onto the wrong hero's durable memory, so an NPC would recall a promise that was never theirs. Returning -1 for an unresolved or collective subject is equally load-bearing, since that is what routes the seal to the presiding anchor instead of guessing a seated member._

- GIVEN a subject naming a single seated member, WHEN matching, THEN that members index is returned
- GIVEN a seated name inside a longer subject, WHEN matching, THEN it still matches
- GIVEN a subject naming nobody seated, WHEN matching, THEN no match is returned
- GIVEN a blank subject or no participants, WHEN matching, THEN no match is returned
- GIVEN a subject that could match two seated members, WHEN matching, THEN the first wins

### Assemblies/EnvoyIdentityPolicyTests.cs

_Documents the wording rule for a synthetic ambassador's identity: the display name and persona line the player and the LLM actually see. The player report this whole convergence traces back to is a council that "felt magic" (a stand-in appearing as if it were the real lord); if the display name or persona ever failed to name the represented faction/leader, or leaked a raw "null"/empty placeholder from a missing name, the seat would either read as the wrong person or as visibly broken. Both DisplayName and Persona are pure string composition, so every boundary (blank, null, both present) is exercised here rather than left to be noticed in a live scene._

- GIVEN a faction name, WHEN building the display name, THEN it reads herald of that faction
- GIVEN a faction name with surrounding whitespace, WHEN building the display name, THEN it is trimmed
- GIVEN a null faction name, WHEN building the display name, THEN it degrades to the generic fallback without emitting null
- GIVEN a blank faction name, WHEN building the display name, THEN it degrades to the generic fallback
- GIVEN a faction and leader name, WHEN building the persona, THEN it names both and states it binds nothing
- GIVEN a faction name but no leader name, WHEN building the persona, THEN it degrades without emitting null or a double space
- GIVEN a leader name but no faction name, WHEN building the persona, THEN it degrades without emitting null
- GIVEN both faction and leader name missing, WHEN building the persona, THEN it degrades to a generic but coherent sentence

### Assemblies/ExpulsionPolicyTests.cs

_Documents expel_from_clan's pure gate: whether the player may cast a seated companion out of their own clan right now. The stake across every test here: expulsion is one of the gravest social costs the table can hand down (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's Kimi review, Partie 8), so getting the AND wrong either lets a non-leader force out a companion that was never theirs to dismiss, exposes a marriage-ending loophole through the wrong channel, or refuses a legal expulsion the lift should have carried out._

- GIVEN a clan leader and a live companion, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is legal
- GIVEN the player does not lead the clan, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the target is not a companion, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the target is the players own spouse, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the target is not alive, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN no condition holds, WHEN asked to expel, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the expulsion tuning constants, WHEN read, THEN they match the documented design
- GIVEN every teaching fact holds, WHEN offering expel, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the player does not lead the clan, WHEN offering expel, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the target is the players own spouse, WHEN offering expel, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the companion is away on an errand, WHEN offering expel, THEN the action is withheld

### Assemblies/FiefSwapGroundingCodecTests.cs

_Documents the composite grounding swap_fiefs uses to carry TWO fief names through the single TargetHint slot every other resolution kind reuses, mirroring ArrangeMarriageGroundingCodecTests' own coverage. The stake throughout: a codec that fails to round-trip could leave CouncilLift.SettleSwapFiefs resolving the wrong pair of fiefs, or worse, transferring one side of the trade while silently dropping the other, exactly the half-a-swap outcome the whole atomic design exists to prevent._

- GIVEN two ordinary fief names, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN both survive unchanged
- GIVEN a null or blank composite, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN a composite with no delimiter, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN either half is blank, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN the target fief name itself contains the delimiter, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN it survives whole
- GIVEN a null half, WHEN composed, THEN it does not throw and still fails to decompose

### Assemblies/FiefTransferPolicyTests.cs

_Documents grant_fief/revoke_fief's pure gate: whether a fief transfer between the crown and a vassal is currently legal. A fief is CLAN-owned in vanilla, never hero-owned, so the stake across every test here is the same one COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's Kimi review pins: only a true kingdom sovereign may move one at all, and only when the named fief is actually where the pledge says it is (the player's own clan for a grant, the spoliated vassal's own clan for a revoke). Getting either AND wrong either lets a non-sovereign clan leader hand out or seize a fief that was never theirs to move, or refuses a legal transfer the lift should have carried out._

- GIVEN a sovereign and a player owned fief, WHEN asked to grant, THEN it is legal
- GIVEN the player is not the kingdom sovereign, WHEN asked to grant, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the recipient is not a vassal of the players kingdom, WHEN asked to grant, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the named fief is not the players own, WHEN asked to grant, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN neither condition holds, WHEN asked to grant, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a sovereign and a vassal owned fief, WHEN asked to revoke, THEN it is legal
- GIVEN the player is not the kingdom sovereign, WHEN asked to revoke, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the target is not a vassal of the players kingdom, WHEN asked to revoke, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the named fief is not the vassals own, WHEN asked to revoke, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN neither condition holds, WHEN asked to revoke, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the revoke tuning constants, WHEN read, THEN they match the documented design
- GIVEN the grant influence cost, WHEN read, THEN it matches the documented design
- GIVEN a sovereign and both fiefs owned as expected, WHEN asked to swap, THEN it is legal
- GIVEN the player is not the kingdom sovereign, WHEN asked to swap, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the named player fief is not the players own, WHEN asked to swap, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the named vassal fief is not the vassals own, WHEN asked to swap, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN any single condition is missing, WHEN asked to swap, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN every grant teaching fact holds, WHEN offering, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the player does not rule a kingdom, WHEN offering a grant, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the npc is not a vassal in the players kingdom, WHEN offering a grant, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN no grantable crown fief, WHEN offering a grant, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN every revoke teaching fact holds, WHEN offering, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the player does not rule a kingdom, WHEN offering a revoke, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the npc is not a vassal in the players kingdom, WHEN offering a revoke, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN no revocable fief, WHEN offering a revoke, THEN the action is withheld

### Assemblies/GiveItemPolicyTests.cs

_Documents give_item's pure policy: the one rule that decides whether an item may ever be given away at all._

- GIVEN an item with a positive value, WHEN checked, THEN it is giveable
- GIVEN an item with a zero value, WHEN checked, THEN it is not giveable
- GIVEN an item with a negative value, WHEN checked, THEN it is not giveable
- GIVEN an item worth exactly one denar, WHEN checked, THEN it is giveable

### Assemblies/GoldPledgePolicyTests.cs

_Documents give_gold's pure policy: the payable-amount clamp mirroring the existing 1:1 give_gold action's own solvency rule (BannerlordGameStateBridge.ExecuteGiveGold). The stake across every test here is the same: a council pledge must never conjure coin the giver does not hold, and a pledge from an insolvent giver must be refused rather than silently applied for zero denars while still reporting success._

- GIVEN a proposed amount the giver can afford, WHEN clamped, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN a proposed amount above the givers gold, WHEN clamped, THEN it is capped to their gold
- GIVEN a proposed amount above the ceiling, WHEN clamped, THEN it is capped to the maximum
- GIVEN a giver with no gold, WHEN clamped, THEN the payable amount is zero
- GIVEN a non positive proposed amount, WHEN clamped, THEN the payable amount is zero
- GIVEN a proposed amount exactly at a boundary, WHEN clamped, THEN it is unchanged

### Assemblies/GovernorAppointmentPolicyTests.cs

_Protects the FIRST governance executor's own refusal rule (CouncilLift.SettleAppointGovernor). Author's condition: appointing a companion or clan lord as governor is a real pledge the lift may carry out, and the prospective governor MAY refuse it - but only MECHANICALLY, from their own standing toward the player, never as a narrated refusal invented after ChangeGovernorAction already ran. That is exactly the phantom-deed class this project eliminates elsewhere (a quest "completed" with no verified deed, a relation gain with no gate): here the failure mode would be a governor appointed in the save data while the story tells the player they were turned down, or the reverse. Getting the boundary wrong in either direction reopens that class of bug._

- GIVEN high standing, WHEN deciding, THEN the honour is accepted
- GIVEN neutral standing, WHEN deciding, THEN the honour is accepted
- GIVEN a mildly negative standing, WHEN deciding, THEN the honour is still accepted
- GIVEN deep resentment, WHEN deciding, THEN the honour is refused
- GIVEN standing one point above the resentment floor, WHEN deciding, THEN the honour is accepted
- GIVEN every teaching fact holds, WHEN offering, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the player does not lead the clan, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the appointee is not of the players clan, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the appointee is unavailable, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN no vacant governable fief, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the appointee already governs another fief, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld
- GIVEN the appointee is away on an errand, WHEN offering, THEN the action is withheld

### Assemblies/HostageEligibilityPolicyTests.cs

_Documents give_hostage's eligibility gate: COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's own five CONDITIONS ("vivant, libre, majeur, pas chef de clan, pas heritier unique"), each pinned by its own test so a future change to the predicate cannot silently drop one of Gabriel's own guards. The STAKE across every test here: an ineligible relative (a minor, an imprisoned kinsman, a clan chief, or the pledger's clan's own sole remaining heir) offered as a hostage would either be mechanically wrong (a chief has no business residing at a foreign court) or would gamble away a clan's own succession, exactly the "enjeu de succession vanilla trop lourd" Gabriel's own condition warns against._

- GIVEN every condition met, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is
- GIVEN the relative is dead, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the relative is not free, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the relative is a minor, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the relative is a clan chief, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the relative is the clans sole heir, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN multiple conditions fail at once, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is still refused

### Assemblies/HostagePolicyTests.cs

_Documents give_hostage's ONLY pure policy figure: the fixed 45-day residency term. Kimi's "v1 invite d'honneur" (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's Partie 8) deliberately fixes the term rather than letting the LLM negotiate a second parameter alongside the relative's own name, mirroring TributePolicy.TributeDays'/StipendPolicy.TrancheDays' own simplification. The STAKE across the single test here: a future tuning pass cannot silently drift this figure out of COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's own 20-90 day range without a test noticing._

- GIVEN the hostage term, WHEN asked, THEN it is a fixed forty five days
- GIVEN the hostage term, WHEN asked, THEN it sits within the documented twenty to ninety day range

### Assemblies/MemberPresenceDescriberTests.cs

_Documents the phrase CouncilRosterResolver attaches to each seated member so PromptBuilder's council block can state the truth of where they stand. STAKE (author's bug report, "bug c"): CouncilRosterPolicy seats a member on availability alone, by design with no distance test (see its own header), so a companion staying behind can be seated without ever being in the player's party; without this phrase and the directive it feeds, that companion could then claim in dialogue to be "already at your side" on the road. Every case here is a real seated-but-not-in-party shape the mod must describe honestly instead of leaving the model to guess._

- GIVEN a member riding in the players party, WHEN describing, THEN at your side in your party
- GIVEN a member leading their own party, WHEN describing, THEN leading their own company
- GIVEN a member staying in a named settlement, WHEN describing, THEN keeping to that place
- GIVEN a member with a blank settlement name, WHEN describing, THEN the generic abroad phrase
- GIVEN a member with none of the above, WHEN describing, THEN abroad not travelling with you
- GIVEN conflicting stale facts, WHEN describing, THEN in main party wins
- GIVEN leading their own party and a stale settlement name, WHEN describing, THEN leading wins

### Assemblies/MissionTypeParserTests.cs

_MissionTypeParser is the only bridge between what the LLM writes in a [RESOLUTION]'s target_mission field (carried through AssemblyResolutionEntry.TargetHint, the same single grounding slot assign_party_role uses for a party role) and the real CompanionMissionBehavior errand CouncilLift.SettleDispatchMission dispatches. The stake: a mission type must be resolved from a STRUCTURED value, never prose-parsed at the lift, and an unrecognised type must fail cleanly (None) rather than guess an errand the model did not actually name, or a companion could be sent on an errand nobody asked for._

- GIVEN a recognised mission name, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to the matching type
- GIVEN mixed case, WHEN parsed, THEN it still resolves
- GIVEN surrounding whitespace, WHEN parsed, THEN it still resolves
- GIVEN a legacy gather news errand word, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to the merged type
- GIVEN blank or unrecognised text, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to none

### Assemblies/NonAggressionPactPolicyTests.cs

_Documents non_aggression_pact's pure policy: the fixed term, the order-independent faction-pair match (CouncilPactBehavior.IsUnderPact's own core), and the breach-fault predicates a real MapEvent's attacker/ defender sides feed into (CouncilPactBehavior.OnMapEventEnded). The STAKE across every test here: this pact is HONEST-LIMITED by design (see NonAggressionPactPolicy's own header) - it binds only the mod's own hostile-quest emission and a detected breach, never vanilla AI, so the fault-direction rule (a grudge ONLY when the player's own side attacks) must never be inverted, or the mod would plant a nonsensical grudge (an NPC resenting the player for the NPC'S OWN faction's attack)._

- GIVEN the pact term, WHEN asked, THEN it is a fixed forty five days
- GIVEN the breach consequence figures, WHEN asked, THEN they match the doc
- GIVEN the same pair in either order, WHEN matched, THEN both orders are recognized
- GIVEN a mismatched pair, WHEN matched, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a null or blank id on either side, WHEN matched, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN factionA attacking factionB, WHEN checking breach, THEN it is a breach
- GIVEN factionB attacking factionA, WHEN checking breach, THEN it is still a breach
- GIVEN the battle does not pit the two pact factions against each other, WHEN checking breach, THEN it is not a breach
- GIVEN factionA is the attacker, WHEN asking who is at fault, THEN the player is at fault
- GIVEN factionA is not the attacker, WHEN asking who is at fault, THEN the player is not at fault

### Assemblies/OathGroundingCodecTests.cs

_Documents the composite grounding swear_oath uses to carry (oath kind, target value) through the single TargetHint slot every other resolution kind reuses, mirroring ArrangeMarriageGroundingCodecTests'/ FiefSwapGroundingCodecTests' own coverage. The ONE deliberate difference from those two: this codec's RIGHT half (the target value) may legitimately be blank (a protect oath names no target beyond the player), so its own round-trip rule differs and needs its own tests, not just a copy of theirs. The stake throughout: a codec that fails to round-trip could leave CouncilLift.SettleSwearOath tracking the WRONG oath kind, or refusing a perfectly valid protect oath because its target half is empty._

- GIVEN an oath kind and a target value, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN both survive unchanged
- GIVEN a blank target value, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN it still succeeds with an empty target
- GIVEN a null target value, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN it still succeeds with an empty target
- GIVEN a blank oath kind, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails even if a target value is present
- GIVEN a null or blank composite, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN a composite with no delimiter, WHEN decomposed, THEN it fails
- GIVEN the target value itself contains the delimiter, WHEN composed and decomposed, THEN it survives whole

### Assemblies/OathOfferPolicyTests.cs

_OathOfferPolicy is the 1:1 conversation TEACHING gate for swear_oath, brought to the 1:1 pipeline alongside the council on 2026-08-05. What it decides: whether the LLM is even SHOWN the swear_oath action this turn, from the plain engine facts the mod's EncounterContextBuilder reads about the ONE conversation NPC. What it feeds: PromptBuilder.AppendSwearOathOffer's gate. What breaks if it is wrong: it guards the empty-promise class the whole conditional-teaching contract exists to prevent. It mirrors the council's own swearer eligibility (ChatViewModel.AnySeatedMemberCanSwearOath): alive, free, not the player, no oath already outstanding (one vow at a time), and plausibly qualifying for at least one whitelisted oath kind. Every fact is re-checked at the bridge (CourtActionResolver.TrySwearOath), so a true answer here only ever OFFERS a teaching, never records an oath. Each fact below removes one way an NPC could otherwise "agree" to an oath the game then silently declines._

- GIVEN every teaching fact holds, WHEN offering an oath, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the npc is dead, WHEN offering an oath, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN the npc is imprisoned, WHEN offering an oath, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN the npc is the player, WHEN offering an oath, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN the npc already carries an oath, WHEN offering an oath, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN the npc qualifies for no oath kind, WHEN offering an oath, THEN it is withheld

### Assemblies/PartyCommandOfferPolicyTests.cs

_PartyCommandOfferPolicy is the 1:1 conversation TEACHING gate for the three party-command verbs (assign_party_role, rejoin_party, dispatch_mission) brought to the 1:1 pipeline alongside the council on 2026-08-05. What it decides: whether the LLM is even SHOWN each action this turn, from the plain engine facts the mod's EncounterContextBuilder reads about the ONE conversation NPC. What it feeds: PromptBuilder's Append*Offer gates. What breaks if it is wrong: it guards the empty-promise class the whole conditional-teaching contract exists to prevent. Each gate mirrors the council's own offering eligibility for its verb (ResolutionOfferingResolver: the TargetInPlayerParty fact for assign/dispatch, the TargetOutOfPlayerParty fact for rejoin), so teach a verb only when the bridge's own re-check (CourtActionResolver.TryAssignPartyRole / TryRejoinParty / TryDispatchMission) could actually honour it, and an NPC can never "agree" to something the game then silently declines. Each fact below removes one way that could happen._

- GIVEN a companion in the party, WHEN offering a role, THEN it is taught
- GIVEN a companion out of the party, WHEN offering a role, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN a non companion in the party, WHEN offering a role, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN an away free companion, WHEN offering rejoin, THEN it is taught
- GIVEN a companion already in the party, WHEN offering rejoin, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN an imprisoned companion, WHEN offering rejoin, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN a companion leading their own company, WHEN offering rejoin, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN a dead companion, WHEN offering rejoin, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN an available companion in the party, WHEN offering a mission, THEN it is taught
- GIVEN a companion not free to ride out, WHEN offering a mission, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN a companion out of the party, WHEN offering a mission, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN someone who cannot ride for the player, WHEN offering a mission, THEN it is withheld

_PartyRoleParser is the only bridge between what the LLM writes in a [RESOLUTION]'s target_role field (carried through AssemblyResolutionEntry.TargetHint, the same single grounding slot appoint_governor uses for a fief name) and the real engine role CouncilLift.SettleAssignPartyRole assigns. The stake: a role must be resolved from a STRUCTURED value, never prose-parsed at the lift, and an unrecognised role must fail cleanly (None) rather than guess a role the model did not actually name, or a companion could be assigned a role nobody asked for._

- GIVEN a recognised role name, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to the matching role
- GIVEN mixed case, WHEN parsed, THEN it still resolves
- GIVEN surrounding whitespace, WHEN parsed, THEN it still resolves
- GIVEN blank or unrecognised text, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to none

_Documents pledge_against's one council-specific caveat: a member must not be allowed to scheme against a rival seated at the very same table (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's Kimi review, "trahison a table"). - GIVEN the rival was not seated at this council, WHEN checked, THEN it is not flagged

- GIVEN a null or blank target id, WHEN checked, THEN it is never flagged

### Assemblies/ResolutionEligibilityPolicyTests.cs

_Documents which catalogue motions a council may actually offer given the world. The rule is AND: every condition a motion requires must hold. The stake is the empty-promise guard, if a motion is offered while a condition is unmet (no vacant fief, no gold), the council could pass a resolution the bridge cannot apply, which the whole verification design exists to prevent._

- GIVEN assign party role, WHEN the target is or is not in the party, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN dispatch mission, WHEN the target is or is not in the party, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN rejoin party, WHEN the target is or is not out of the party, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN appoint governor, WHEN either condition is missing, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN grant stipend, WHEN the player cannot afford it, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN a companions council and partial facts, WHEN offered, THEN only the satisfied motions appear
- GIVEN a vacant fief and an available member, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN appoint governor is included
- GIVEN no vacant fief OR no available member, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN appoint governor is withheld
- GIVEN a seated companion out of the party, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN rejoin party is included
- GIVEN no seated companion out of the party, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN rejoin party is withheld
- GIVEN a seated member in the party, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN dispatch mission is included
- GIVEN no seated member in the party, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN dispatch mission is withheld
- GIVEN the player can afford the floor tranche, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN grant stipend is included
- GIVEN the player cannot afford the floor tranche, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN grant stipend is withheld
- GIVEN any scope and empty facts, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN quest is always included
- GIVEN the player can declare war, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN declare war is included
- GIVEN the player cannot declare war, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN declare war is withheld
- GIVEN the player can make peace, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN make peace is included
- GIVEN the player cannot make peace, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN make peace is withheld
- GIVEN give gold, WHEN a member does or does not hold gold, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated clan lord holds gold, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give gold is included
- GIVEN no seated war council member holds gold, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give gold is withheld
- GIVEN a companions or family council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give gold is never included
- GIVEN give influence, WHEN a member can or cannot grant influence, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated ally can grant influence, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give influence is included
- GIVEN no seated ally can grant influence, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give influence is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give influence is never included
- GIVEN grant fief, WHEN either condition is missing, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN the player can grant a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN grant fief is included
- GIVEN the player cannot grant a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN grant fief is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords companions or family council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN grant fief is never included
- GIVEN revoke fief, WHEN either condition is missing, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN the player can revoke a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN revoke fief is included
- GIVEN the player cannot revoke a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN revoke fief is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords companions or family council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN revoke fief is never included
- GIVEN expel from clan, WHEN no seated companion is expellable, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN a companion is expellable, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN expel from clan is included
- GIVEN no companion is expellable, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN expel from clan is withheld
- GIVEN a family clan lords war council or parley, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN expel from clan is never included
- GIVEN pledge against, WHEN no seated member can pledge, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN a seated member can pledge against a rival, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN pledge against is included
- GIVEN no seated member can pledge against a rival, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN pledge against is withheld
- GIVEN a companions war council or parley council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN pledge against is never included
- GIVEN arrange marriage, WHEN no seated ally offers a marriageable kin, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN a seated ally offers a marriageable kin, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN arrange marriage is included
- GIVEN no seated ally offers a marriageable kin, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN arrange marriage is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords companions or family council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN arrange marriage is never included
- GIVEN swap fiefs, WHEN any condition is missing, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN the player can swap a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN swap fiefs is included
- GIVEN the player cannot swap a fief, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN swap fiefs is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords companions or family council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN swap fiefs is never included
- GIVEN tribute, WHEN no seated counterpart can pay, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN a seated counterpart can pay tribute, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN tribute is included
- GIVEN no seated counterpart can pay tribute, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN tribute is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family clan lords or war council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN tribute is never included
- GIVEN release prisoner, WHEN the player does or does not hold a prisoner of the enemy faction, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN the player holds a prisoner of the enemy faction, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN release prisoner is included
- GIVEN the player holds no prisoner of the enemy faction, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN release prisoner is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family clan lords or war council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN release prisoner is never included
- GIVEN lend troops, WHEN any seated member can or cannot lend troops, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated lord can lend troops, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN lend troops is included
- GIVEN no seated member can lend troops, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN lend troops is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family or parley council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN lend troops is never included
- GIVEN give troops, WHEN any seated lord can or cannot receive troops, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated lord can receive troops, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give troops is included
- GIVEN no seated lord can receive troops, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give troops is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family or parley council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give troops is never included
- GIVEN non aggression pact, WHEN a seated counterpart is or is not available, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated counterpart can pact, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN non aggression pact is included
- GIVEN no seated counterpart can pact, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN non aggression pact is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family clan lords or war council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN non aggression pact is never included
- GIVEN give item, WHEN any seated lord has or has not a giveable item, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated lord has a giveable item, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give item is included
- GIVEN no seated lord has a giveable item, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give item is withheld
- GIVEN a companions family or parley council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN give item is never included
- GIVEN support claimant, WHEN a member can or cannot support the players claim, THEN offer follows
- GIVEN a seated vassal can support the players claim, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN support claimant is included
- GIVEN no seated vassal can support the players claim, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN support claimant is withheld
- GIVEN a clan lords council, WHEN offered keys are asked, THEN support claimant is never included

### Assemblies/ResolutionLifecycleTests.cs

_Documents the assembly-motion transaction model: what is provisional, what can be cancelled, and what actually commits when the assembly is lifted. The cancellation path is the one that MUST be solid: a player who takes back a decision mid-council must leave no trace in the world, because nothing was applied before the lift._

- GIVEN a proposed motion, WHEN asked, THEN it is provisional and can be reversed
- GIVEN an agreed motion, WHEN asked before the lift, THEN it is still provisional and reversible
- GIVEN a reversed motion, WHEN asked, THEN it is terminal and cannot transition anywhere
- GIVEN the lift, WHEN deciding what commits, THEN only agreed motions do
- GIVEN a proposed motion, WHEN it tries to commit directly, THEN the transition is illegal
- GIVEN an agreed motion, WHEN committed at the lift, THEN it ends as applied or failed and stops
- GIVEN an applied or failed motion, WHEN asked to reverse, THEN it refuses

### Assemblies/StipendClosurePolicyTests.cs

_v1.34.2 companion hardening. StipendClosurePolicy decides how CouncilStipendBehavior.Advance ENDS a running stipend each day: keep paying, close a completed tranche, or refund a dead payee's unspent escrow. Before this, the behavior removed a dead payee's stipend with no refund and no notice, silently swallowing up to the whole remaining escrow (~15k denars possible) that had been financed from the PLAYER's own purse up front; and a normal completion ended with only a log line the player never saw. These tests pin the classification and the refund amount, since a wrong answer either strands the player's already-spent gold or ends an escrow unseen._

- GIVEN the payee is gone mid tranche, WHEN classified, THEN it is PayeeGone so the escrow is refunded not swallowed
- GIVEN a live payee with days and escrow left, WHEN classified, THEN it continues
- GIVEN a live payee whose days have elapsed, WHEN classified, THEN it is Completed
- GIVEN a live payee whose escrow is empty, WHEN classified, THEN it is Completed
- GIVEN a payee gone with escrow left, WHEN sizing the refund, THEN it is the whole unpaid remainder
- GIVEN a non positive escrow, WHEN sizing the refund, THEN it is zero never negative

### Assemblies/StipendPolicyTests.cs

_Documents grant_stipend's pure policy: the daily-amount clamp, the escrow total, and the afford gate both the offering (at the floor, before any amount is proposed) and the lift (at the real proposed amount) read. The stake across every test here is the same: a promise that outruns the player's purse must be refused before the lift ever touches the world, never applied partially and never left to fail silently mid-term (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md "grant_stipend", ESCROW model - Kimi review, adopted as-is)._

- GIVEN an amount below the floor, WHEN clamped, THEN it becomes the minimum
- GIVEN an amount above the ceiling, WHEN clamped, THEN it becomes the maximum
- GIVEN an amount within range, WHEN clamped, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN an amount per day, WHEN the total cost is asked, THEN it is the amount times the tranche
- GIVEN the players gold, WHEN afford is asked, THEN it requires covering the whole tranche
- GIVEN the minimum tranche cost, WHEN computed, THEN it matches the floor rate over the full tranche
- GIVEN every teaching fact holds, WHEN offering a stipend, THEN the action is taught
- GIVEN the npc is not a player companion, WHEN offering a stipend, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN a stipend already runs for the npc, WHEN offering a stipend, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN the player cannot afford the floor tranche, WHEN offering a stipend, THEN it is withheld

### Assemblies/TrialVerdictPolicyTests.cs

_Documents how a trial's verdict is read, and what happens when the council rises without one. The stakes: a verdict misread as a conviction jails a player the council meant to free; a trial the player could escape by closing the window would make the whole arrest hollow._

- GIVEN words that convict, WHEN read, THEN it is a conviction
- GIVEN words that acquit, WHEN read, THEN it is no conviction
- GIVEN words that say neither, WHEN read, THEN nobody is convicted
- GIVEN a hostile bench, WHEN the council rose without a verdict, THEN it convicts
- GIVEN a bench not against the accused, WHEN the council rose without a verdict, THEN it acquits

### Assemblies/TributePolicyTests.cs

_Documents tribute's pure policy: the daily-amount clamp, the fixed 30-day term, and the pay-as-you-go afford check read fresh every tick. The stake across every test here: a tribute that outpaces the payer's own purse must degrade gracefully day by day (CanPay says no, the behavior tolerates it up to the grace threshold), it must never be able to clamp the payer's gold negative, and the amount itself must never reach the lift unclamped, however the LLM proposed it (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's Kimi review, "tribute")._

- GIVEN an amount below the floor, WHEN clamped, THEN it becomes the minimum
- GIVEN an amount above the ceiling, WHEN clamped, THEN it becomes the maximum
- GIVEN an amount within range, WHEN clamped, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN the payers gold, WHEN asked if todays tribute is payable, THEN it only needs to cover one day
- GIVEN a payer with no gold, WHEN asked if tribute is payable, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the tribute term, WHEN asked, THEN it is a fixed thirty days
- GIVEN the grace threshold, WHEN asked, THEN it is three consecutive missed days

### Assemblies/WarCouncilTriggerPolicyTests.cs

_Documents the war-council OFFER gate. What breaks without it: the player suggestion is that after an army forms or wins a considerable action, the mod OFFERS to convene the war council. This policy is the single tested authority WarCouncilOfferBehavior defers to before it shows that inquiry. Get it wrong and the offer either never appears (no captains ever take counsel) or spams on every back-to-back battle (the anti-nag cooldown is exactly what the DefaultCooldownDays boundary pins), or surfaces to a lone player who commands no army at all._

- GIVEN in an army and a notable action never offered, WHEN evaluated, THEN it offers
- GIVEN no prior offer, WHEN evaluated, THEN the null days case is allowed
- GIVEN not in an army, WHEN evaluated, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN a non notable action, WHEN evaluated, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN a notable action within the cooldown, WHEN evaluated, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN a notable action after the cooldown, WHEN evaluated, THEN it offers again

### Assemblies/WardEligibilityPolicyTests.cs

_Documents sponsor_ward's eligibility gate: alive, free, within the young-adult age band, and not already the protege of another live bond (COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md's own "un protege n'a qu'un mentor a la fois"). The STAKE across every test here: an ineligible companion (dead, imprisoned, too old, or already sponsored) taken on as a ward would either be mechanically wrong (a dead companion cannot travel with anyone) or would silently double-book a protege between two mentors, exactly the kind of drift the eligibility gate exists to prevent._

- GIVEN every condition met, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is
- GIVEN the companion is dead, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the companion is not free, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the companion is outside the age band, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the companion is already sponsored, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN multiple conditions fail at once, WHEN asked if eligible, THEN it is still refused

### Assemblies/WardPolicyTests.cs

_Documents sponsor_ward's pure tuning: the "young companion" age band and the daily skill-XP trickle. Neither figure comes from COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md itself (unlike HostagePolicy.TermDays, which inherits Kimi's own 20-90 day range) - both are this build's own author's design call (WardPolicy's own header), so the STAKE here is narrower than most policy tests: pinning the CHOSEN values so a future tuning pass changes them deliberately, with a comment, rather than by silent drift._

- GIVEN the minimum ward age, WHEN asked, THEN it matches the vanilla adult floor
- GIVEN an age below the floor, WHEN asked if within the ward age band, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN an age at the floor, WHEN asked if within the ward age band, THEN it is accepted
- GIVEN an age at the ceiling, WHEN asked if within the ward age band, THEN it is accepted
- GIVEN an age above the ceiling, WHEN asked if within the ward age band, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the daily mentorship xp, WHEN asked, THEN it is a small flat trickle

## Battles

### Battles/BattleFeatDistillerTests.cs

_BattleFeatDistiller decides which of a battle's many entries an NPC would actually bring up. It is the guard against the obvious failure of this whole pillar: a battle can produce dozens of entries, and a memory that kept them all would be a casualty list rather than a recollection, bloating every profile in the save and making lords recite statistics at the player. It is equally a guard the other way: the ONE deed worth naming, and the foe who put the player down (the seed of a rivalry or a grudge), must survive the cut, or the feature records nothing worth talking about._

- GIVEN only unremarkable feats, WHEN distilled, THEN nothing is kept
- GIVEN a notable and an exploit, WHEN distilled, THEN the exploit is chosen
- GIVEN many worthy feats, WHEN distilled, THEN only one is kept
- GIVEN equal tiers, WHEN distilled, THEN the first recorded wins
- GIVEN the player was downed, WHEN distilled, THEN that foe is kept
- GIVEN an ordinary toll, WHEN distilled, THEN it is not mentioned
- GIVEN a remarkable toll, WHEN distilled, THEN it is mentioned
- GIVEN nothing of note, WHEN distilled, THEN the digest is empty

### Battles/BattleFeatTextTests.cs

_BattleFeatText writes the deeds a battle leaves in the memory of those who were there, and the version that circulates to those who were not. It guards ONE rule above all (Gabriel, ratified 2026-09-06): a LORD is bested, put down, or taken alive, and is called killed ONLY when the engine really killed them. Bannerlord knocks heroes out far more often than it kills them, so a line saying "you killed Lucon" would have NPCs mourning a man sitting in the player's own dungeon, which is exactly the amnesia-and-nonsense this whole mod exists to avoid. The second thing these pin is the VOICE: a witness speaks first-hand ("I saw you..."), the realm speaks as hearsay ("They say..."), because the recent-matter approach routes those two registers differently._

- GIVEN a bested foe, WHEN written, THEN it never claims a death
- GIVEN a foe taken alive, WHEN written, THEN the capture is stated and no death is claimed
- GIVEN a genuinely slain foe, WHEN written, THEN the death is spoken plainly
- GIVEN a witnessed deed, WHEN written, THEN it is in the first person
- GIVEN a player name, WHEN witnessing a feat, THEN the player is named not you
- GIVEN no player name, WHEN witnessing a feat, THEN it falls back to the player
- GIVEN a remarkable toll, WHEN witnessed, THEN the player is named not you
- GIVEN a siege noun phrase engagement, WHEN witnessing a toll, THEN there is exactly one at
- GIVEN the player was downed, WHEN written, THEN the foe is named
- GIVEN an exploit that travels, WHEN written, THEN it is hearsay naming both parties
- GIVEN no engagement name, WHEN written, THEN the line has no dangling place
- GIVEN a missing foe name, WHEN written, THEN it degrades to a sayable phrase
- GIVEN the bested foe himself, WHEN written, THEN he speaks of his own defeat
- GIVEN the bested foe himself, WHEN written, THEN no death is claimed
- GIVEN the man who downed the player, WHEN written, THEN he owns the deed

### Battles/CombatProwessPolicyTests.cs

_CombatProwessPolicy decides how remarkable it is that one fighter put another down, and therefore how far word of it travels. Gabriel's ruling (2026-09-06): a world event is worth it only for an EXPLOIT, e.g. besting an opponent much stronger than yourself. Two failures matter. If the stomp rule broke, every bandit the player rides over would become realm gossip and the feature would read as spam. If the exploit bar drifted, the one deed actually worth talking about (you beat someone above your weight, or a name the realm knows) would be recorded as an ordinary scuffle and never circulate, which is the whole point of the feature._

- GIVEN two fighters alike but for level, WHEN scored, THEN the veteran rates higher
- GIVEN mobility against weapon skill, WHEN scored, THEN the weapon skill wins
- GIVEN empty or null facts, WHEN scored, THEN it is never below one
- GIVEN a foe well above the winner, WHEN rated, THEN it is an exploit that travels
- GIVEN a foe far beneath the winner, WHEN rated, THEN it is unremarkable and does not travel
- GIVEN a famous foe, WHEN rated, THEN it is an exploit even if the winner was stronger
- GIVEN an even match, WHEN rated, THEN it is notable but does not travel

## Chronicle

### Chronicle/ChronicleResumePolicyTests.cs

_that the chronicle does not lose a chapter when a deed behind it is withdrawn._

- GIVEN a chronicled deed is retracted, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN it still starts after the last one it wrote
- GIVEN a retraction and a new deed, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN the new deed is the one it writes
- GIVEN nothing has been withdrawn, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN it starts after the last deed
- GIVEN an older save with a count and no mark, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN the count is used as before
- GIVEN a count larger than the deeds that remain, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN it is clamped to the end
- GIVEN the marked deed itself was retracted, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN it falls back to the clamped count
- GIVEN a chronicle never illuminated, WHEN it resumes, THEN it starts at the beginning
- GIVEN two identical deeds on one day, WHEN the illuminator resumes, THEN it takes the later one

## Companions

### Companions/CompanionServiceTransferPolicyTests.cs

_take_into_service, built on Gabriel's ruling "un seigneur doit pouvoir recruter" after Ira agreed in prose to take his companion Nabb under her banner and the game had nothing to execute._

- GIVEN a free lord and a free companion, WHEN evaluated, THEN the transfer is allowed
- GIVEN the companion is the players spouse, WHEN evaluated, THEN no lord may take them
- GIVEN the companion governs a fief, WHEN evaluated, THEN the fief is not quietly left empty
- GIVEN the companion is away on an errand, WHEN evaluated, THEN they must be recalled first
- GIVEN the hero is not of the players household, WHEN evaluated, THEN there is nothing to give
- GIVEN a retainer sworn in without the companion door, WHEN evaluated, THEN they can still be offered
- GIVEN the hero is the players own blood, WHEN evaluated, THEN they are not a retainer to place
- GIVEN the hero leads the players house, WHEN evaluated, THEN they are nobodys to give
- GIVEN a lord who is dead captive or the player, WHEN evaluated, THEN nobody changes hands
- GIVEN the lord is of the players own clan, WHEN evaluated, THEN it refuses rather than no op
- GIVEN a lord with no house of their own, WHEN evaluated, THEN there is nowhere to take them
- GIVEN no facts at all, WHEN evaluated, THEN it refuses rather than throwing
- GIVEN one companion and no name spoken, WHEN picking, THEN it is the one standing there
- GIVEN several companions and no name spoken, WHEN picking, THEN it refuses to guess
- GIVEN a short name spoken, WHEN picking, THEN the long named companion is found
- GIVEN the full name spoken, WHEN picking, THEN it is matched exactly
- GIVEN two companions sharing a first name, WHEN only that name is spoken, THEN it refuses to guess
- GIVEN a full name that another shares a first word with, WHEN picking, THEN the exact one wins
- GIVEN a name nobody answers to, WHEN picking, THEN nobody is handed over

## Conversation

### Conversation/BriefingReturnDecisionTests.cs

_the SHAPE of the briefing-to-meeting sequence, which is the only thing that ever went wrong with it._

- GIVEN a map conversation and no mission, WHEN asked, THEN there is still a host
- GIVEN a mission and no map conversation, WHEN asked, THEN there is still a host
- GIVEN neither host, WHEN deciding through the same rule, THEN the world comes back
- GIVEN either host alive, WHEN deciding, THEN the meeting is opened over it
- GIVEN the chat is down and a host is live, WHEN deciding, THEN it opens over that host
- GIVEN no host at all, WHEN deciding, THEN it gives the world back rather than waiting
- GIVEN every possible situation, WHEN deciding, THEN it never asks for a scene to be built
- GIVEN the window is still closing, WHEN deciding, THEN it waits
- GIVEN the wait has run long, WHEN deciding, THEN it stops waiting and settles up
- GIVEN nothing armed, WHEN deciding, THEN it does nothing whatever else is true
- GIVEN a hand off, WHEN planning the close, THEN it gives none of the world back
- GIVEN a tale scene, WHEN planning its close, THEN it never ends the players encounter or a conversation
- GIVEN an ordinary close, WHEN planning, THEN the world comes back in order
- GIVEN either close, WHEN planning, THEN what happened is always recorded and the window always goes
- GIVEN a hand off, WHEN it is not caught, THEN exactly what it skipped is owed
- GIVEN every step that exists, WHEN planning, THEN the ordinary close runs all of them
- GIVEN either plan, WHEN read, THEN no step appears twice
- GIVEN a hand off, WHEN compared to an ordinary close, THEN it only ever does less

### Conversation/ConversationCastTests.cs

_Spotlight pillar foundation. A conversation starts focused on one character (the anchor) with others watching from the edge, and the player report this pillar answers is a witness who was spoken to yet stayed a mute bystander. The cast is the source of truth for who is a full participant and who is not, so the whole feature rests on these transitions being exact: a witness spoken to becomes active AND STAYS active; one told to hold their tongue drops back; and the anchor, the reason the conversation exists, can never be silenced. Get a sign wrong and either a drawn-in character keeps being ignored (the very bug) or the person you came to talk to gets shoved out of their own scene._

- GIVEN a new conversation, WHEN it opens, THEN only the anchor is active and the rest are witnesses
- GIVEN a witness, WHEN promoted, THEN they become active
- GIVEN one promoted witness, WHEN checking, THEN the scene is an ensemble
- GIVEN an already active participant, WHEN promoted again, THEN it is a no op
- GIVEN the anchor or an absent character, WHEN promoted, THEN nothing happens
- GIVEN a promoted participant, WHEN demoted, THEN they return to witness
- GIVEN the anchor, WHEN demotion is attempted, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a witness or absent character, WHEN demoted, THEN it is a no op
- GIVEN a newcomer, WHEN introduced, THEN they join as a witness and can be promoted

### Conversation/ConversationResumePolicyTests.cs

_WHEN a conversation the player walked away from may be picked up again, and when putting it back would be a lie._

- GIVEN the same lord on the same day with words still kept, WHEN the chat reopens, THEN the exchange resumes
- GIVEN the player opens a chat with somebody else, WHEN it is checked, THEN the other lords words are not put in his mouth
- GIVEN a day has passed in the campaign, WHEN the chat reopens, THEN yesterdays words are not recited as if still standing there
- GIVEN a save from an earlier day is loaded, WHEN the chat reopens, THEN it is refused like any other day that is not this one
- GIVEN nothing worth resuming, WHEN a chat opens, THEN the chat starts clean
- GIVEN the chat that opens is a staged scene rather than an ordinary talk, WHEN it is checked, THEN nothing is resumed into it
- GIVEN several reasons to refuse at once, WHEN it is checked, THEN the answer names the most fundamental one
- GIVEN a council sitting, WHEN it closes, THEN nothing is kept for a private word later
- GIVEN an ordinary conversation, WHEN it closes, THEN it is kept
- GIVEN a companion briefing, WHEN it closes, THEN nothing is kept

### Conversation/ConversationResumeSnapshotTests.cs

_that the words of a conversation the player walked away from can be put back exactly as they were, and that a snapshot which cannot be read never costs anyone their save._

- GIVEN the negotiation fkasad walked away from, WHEN it is captured and restored, THEN every word comes back in order
- GIVEN a conversation already folded into a recap, WHEN it is restored, THEN the recap comes back with it
- GIVEN a conversation longer than the cap, WHEN it is captured, THEN it is the most recent words that are kept
- GIVEN folded lines are dropped by the cap, WHEN it is restored, THEN the fold marker still points at the right line
- GIVEN a captured conversation, WHEN it is written to the save and read back, THEN it still knows whose it is
- GIVEN an unreadable capture, WHEN it is read, THEN nothing is resumed and nothing throws
- GIVEN a chat that was opened and closed without a word, WHEN it is captured, THEN there is nothing to keep
- GIVEN a capture with no hero named, WHEN it is taken, THEN it is refused rather than kept for anyone
- GIVEN a scene with a witness and narration, WHEN it is restored, THEN the room comes back whole

### Conversation/EnsembleFloorPolicyTests.cs

_The case these were written from is real and is reproduced verbatim below: Gabriel briefed Nabb to charm Ira, Nabb ended his beat by turning to her and asking "Which ones do you remember?", and the floor came back to the player with the question hanging. He had to spend a turn typing "*I watch and wait to see what happens*" to make Ira answer._

- GIVEN the beat Gabriel played, WHEN it closes, THEN Ira is given the floor to answer
- GIVEN a question followed by closing narration, WHEN read, THEN it still counts as asked
- GIVEN a rhetorical question answered by the same speaker, WHEN read, THEN nothing was asked
- GIVEN a beat that ends on a statement, WHEN deciding, THEN the floor returns to the player
- GIVEN only one voice in the room, WHEN it asks, THEN the question is the players to answer
- GIVEN the asker is in the cast, WHEN choosing, THEN they are never chosen to answer themselves
- GIVEN this beat has already answered once, WHEN asked again, THEN the floor goes back to the player
- GIVEN the cap, WHEN read, THEN one beat can never spend more than a single answer
- GIVEN the last line is narration that asks, WHEN deciding, THEN no one is made to answer it
- GIVEN three voices and a name spoken, WHEN choosing, THEN the one named answers
- GIVEN three voices and no name spoken, WHEN choosing, THEN the anchor answers
- GIVEN two names in one line, WHEN choosing, THEN the one turned to last answers
- GIVEN nothing or junk, WHEN deciding, THEN it declines quietly rather than throwing
- GIVEN the player asked the last question, WHEN deciding, THEN no extra turn is spent on it

### Conversation/SilenceOrderPolicyTests.cs

_Spotlight pillar. A promoted participant can be ordered back to silence, and whether that lands is what keeps the scene believable: a lord's own vassal falling quiet on command reads true, and so does a proud rival refusing to be shushed by an equal. Gabriel's ruling is that STANDING (rank and clan influence) dominates deterministically, and temper only decides between near-equals. These pin exactly that: the liege and the wide-gap cases ignore temper, the extremes never depend on a die of personality, and inside the band an agreeable heart yields while a bold, proud one holds the floor. If standing did not dominate, an influential lord's command would be at the mercy of the target's mood, which is the opposite of what power means here._

- GIVEN the commander is the targets liege, WHEN ordered silent, THEN they obey whatever their temper
- GIVEN the commander far outweighs the target, WHEN ordered silent, THEN they obey whatever their temper
- GIVEN the commander is far beneath the target, WHEN ordering silence, THEN it is defied whatever their temper
- GIVEN a defiant target, WHEN the standing gap is wide, THEN influence wins and they comply
- GIVEN equal standing and a neutral temper, WHEN ordered silent, THEN they hold the floor
- GIVEN equal standing and a deferential temper, WHEN ordered silent, THEN they yield
- GIVEN equal standing and a proud defiant temper, WHEN ordered silent, THEN they refuse
- GIVEN no context, WHEN resolving, THEN the voice is kept

## Diagnostics

### Diagnostics/LogRotationPolicyTests.cs

_The log file is trimmed at every launch to the last couple of sessions so it never balloons (player request)._

- GIVEN more sessions than the budget, WHEN trimming, THEN only the most recent blocks remain
- GIVEN a budget of two, WHEN trimming, THEN the last two blocks remain in order
- GIVEN preamble before the first marker, WHEN trimming, THEN it is dropped with the old blocks
- GIVEN fewer sessions than the budget, WHEN trimming, THEN the content is returned unchanged
- GIVEN no marker in the content, WHEN trimming, THEN it is returned unchanged
- GIVEN a zero budget, WHEN trimming, THEN nothing is kept
- GIVEN null content, WHEN trimming, THEN the result is empty

## Duels

### Duels/CourtshipDuelPolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc A: a courtship duel's romance-axis consequence. The through-line is that fighting for her (win or lose) is what a suitor is FOR, while refusing to fight for her at all is judged worse than losing._

- GIVEN the player won the courtship duel, WHEN resolving, THEN attraction rises by admiration on win
- GIVEN the player lost the courtship duel, WHEN resolving, THEN attraction falls by doubt on loss
- GIVEN the player refused the courtship duel, WHEN resolving, THEN attraction falls by cowardice on refuse
- GIVEN the rival balked, WHEN resolving, THEN attraction rises by admiration on rival balked
- GIVEN an unrecognized resolution, WHEN resolving, THEN attraction does not move
- GIVEN the four resolutions, WHEN compared, THEN winning beats losing beats refusing

### Duels/CourtshipRivalryEligibilityTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc A: is there a real triangle worth a duel? All three facts (a real courtship, a real rival desire, and a rival bold enough to force it) must hold at once. Without the triangle actually existing, a duel over a woman nobody is courting is nonsense; without rival boldness, a timid rival would never force it to steel in the first place._

- GIVEN all three facts hold, WHEN asked, THEN the rival should challenge
- GIVEN the player is not courting her, WHEN asked, THEN the rival should not challenge
- GIVEN the rival does not desire her, WHEN asked, THEN the rival should not challenge
- GIVEN the rival is not bold enough, WHEN asked, THEN the rival should not challenge

### Duels/DuelAcceptancePolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc 0: once a challenge is admissible, does the NPC actually take the field? This is where the feature lives or dies as characterisation. Ratified 2026-08-26 (player report: challenges fizzled): a duel of HONOUR is ANSWERED by default, the more so for an honourable NPC, so refusal now takes a real reason: fear turned to a calculated exit, a timid dishonourable nature, or a challenger far beneath the NPC's station (a nobody who calls out a king is dismissed, not fought). If the default drifted back to "refuse", challenges would fizzle and the feature would feel dead; if a great station gulf could not override honour, a peasant could force a king onto the field. These tests pin the named forces and, at the seams, the exact score so the curve cannot silently drift under a later tweak._

- GIVEN a peer duel of honour, WHEN deciding, THEN the default is to accept
- GIVEN a challenger far beneath the npcs station, WHEN deciding, THEN even an honourable lord refuses
- GIVEN a widening station gap, WHEN scoring, THEN it only leans the npc away from the field
- GIVEN a brave honourable lord challenged before witnesses, WHEN deciding, THEN he accepts
- GIVEN a live grudge drives it, WHEN deciding, THEN even a cautious lord takes the field
- GIVEN a calculating coward who fears the player, WHEN deciding privately, THEN he refuses
- GIVEN a calculating lord with little fear, WHEN deciding, THEN the slip rule does not bite until the threshold
- GIVEN the same honourable lord, WHEN challenged publicly versus privately, THEN the public score is higher
- GIVEN a dishonourable lord, WHEN challenged publicly, THEN the crowd pushes him further from the field
- GIVEN no context, WHEN scoring, THEN it is zero and refuses

### Duels/DuelChallengePolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc 0: the gate that decides whether a duel may even be OFFERED, before willingness is weighed. Its verdict is the bridge's law made pure: the LLM can have an NPC say "let us settle this with steel", but only an able peer, face to face, not bound by captivity or kinship or a recent bout, may be called out. Every wrong "yes" here is a duel the game cannot honour, a chat calling a commoner or a prisoner or someone across the map to a field that will never exist, and the whole feature reads as broken._

- GIVEN an able present peer, WHEN evaluating, THEN the challenge is eligible
- GIVEN a party who cannot fight, WHEN evaluating, THEN incapable outranks every other reason
- GIVEN no context at all, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is treated as incapable
- GIVEN the target is self or family, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is refused as kin
- GIVEN one party holds the other prisoner, WHEN evaluating, THEN captivity blocks it
- GIVEN the target is a commoner, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is refused as not a peer
- GIVEN the two are not in the same place, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is not face to face
- GIVEN they dueled within the cooldown, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is too soon
- GIVEN the cooldown has just elapsed, WHEN evaluating, THEN a rematch is allowed
- GIVEN they have never dueled, WHEN checking the cooldown, THEN it is not blocked

### Duels/DuelGrudgeApplicationTests.cs

_that a duel fought over a grievance actually DOES something to the grievance._

- GIVEN a duel fought over a grievance, WHEN its verdict is applied, THEN the grievance is resolved
- GIVEN a duel ducked over a grievance, WHEN its verdict is applied, THEN the grievance deepens
- GIVEN how a grudge duel ended, WHEN applied, THEN fought resolves and ducked deepens
- GIVEN a vendetta, WHEN a duel verdict is applied, THEN it is untouched
- GIVEN no grudge in play, WHEN applied, THEN nothing happens

### Duels/DuelInterlocutorPolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, bugfix (player report, group conversation): a duel challenge came from a witness present in the room, "speaking on their own turn", and got mechanically armed as that witness against the player, even though the fiction described a quarrel between two OTHER people. challenge_duel carries no target parameter, so ResolveDuelChallenge always used whoever spoke as "opponent". This policy is the guard that stops that: it feeds ChatViewModel.ResolveDuelChallenge, deciding whether the speaker who just emitted the action is a fair player-vs-NPC target at all._

- GIVEN the main speaker, WHEN checking, THEN they are a direct interlocutor
- GIVEN a witness the player has addressed, WHEN checking, THEN they are a direct interlocutor
- GIVEN a witness who is not the addressed speaker, WHEN checking, THEN they are not an interlocutor
- GIVEN nobody is currently addressed, WHEN checking a witness, THEN they are not an interlocutor

### Duels/DuelOutcomePolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc 0: what a duel LEAVES BEHIND, across the four ways it can end. The through-line the whole pillar teaches is that steel honestly crossed SETTLES (respect is earned, a driving grudge is answered whoever wins) while walking away HARDENS (contempt, a grudge that festers because it was never faced). The signs are the entire lesson: if winning a duel cost the NPC's respect, or fleeing one settled a grudge, the feature would train players to do the opposite of what a duel means. Non-lethal is assumed throughout, so these are movements of regard, renown and reputation, never a death._

- GIVEN the player won a fair duel, WHEN resolving, THEN the npc respects and fears the player more and renown rises
- GIVEN the player won, WHEN more witnessed it, THEN the renown gain is larger
- GIVEN the npc won a fair duel, WHEN resolving, THEN the player still earns some respect and the npc fears them less
- GIVEN the npc won, WHEN more witnessed it, THEN the player renown does not scale with the crowd
- GIVEN a grudge drove the duel, WHEN it was fought to a finish, THEN it settles whoever won
- GIVEN a player win, WHEN it settled a grudge versus not, THEN affection ends up higher with the grudge settled
- GIVEN the player forfeited, WHEN resolving, THEN respect fear and renown all fall
- GIVEN a grudge drove it, WHEN the player forfeited, THEN the grudge hardens
- GIVEN the npc forfeited, WHEN resolving, THEN the player gains renown and a driving grudge hardens
- GIVEN an honour only stake, WHEN the player won, THEN no wager changes hands
- GIVEN a material stake, WHEN a side won the fight, THEN that side collects
- GIVEN a material stake, WHEN a side forfeited, THEN no wager changes hands
- GIVEN a fought duel, WHEN a side lost, THEN the loser forfeits influence
- GIVEN a fought duel, WHEN more witnessed the defeat, THEN the influence loss is larger
- GIVEN a fought duel, WHEN the crowd is enormous, THEN the influence loss is capped
- GIVEN a forfeit, WHEN resolving, THEN no influence is lost
- GIVEN no context, WHEN resolving, THEN nothing moves
- GIVEN a friendly bout the player wins, WHEN resolved, THEN it costs no fondness
- GIVEN a friendly bout the player wins, WHEN resolved, THEN the loser house is not shamed
- GIVEN a friendly bout the npc wins, WHEN resolved, THEN it still costs no fondness
- GIVEN a friendly bout nobody goes through with, WHEN resolved, THEN nothing happens
- GIVEN a duel over an affront, WHEN resolved, THEN the beaten pride still costs fondness
- GIVEN no reason supplied, WHEN a context is built, THEN it is an affront

### Duels/DuelReasonTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc A: DuelReason is a bare enum, but its two values must exist and stay distinct, since the host switches consequence policy (DuelOutcomePolicy alone vs DuelOutcomePolicy plus CourtshipDuelPolicy) on which one is carried. A silently merged or renamed value would misroute a courtship duel's consequence onto the wrong hero, or an honour duel into moving a romance no one is pursuing._

- GIVEN the two duel reasons, WHEN compared, THEN they are distinct

### Duels/DuelStagingSupportTests.cs

_Duels pillar: this policy decides whether the VENUE DuelVenuePolicy already chose can actually be OPENED by the game right now. It feeds DuelEligibilityResolver.CanDuelNow (the one shared gate the prompt-time teaching and the commit-time bridge both consult): a true here means challenge_duel is safe to teach or to honour; a false means the game has no mission for it yet. Both real venues are built (Wilderness through DuelMissionLauncher, TownGates through TownDuelBehavior's town-centre staging); get either wrong and a duel path that works stops being taught, or one that does not work gets promised, which is the empty-promise bug this codebase keeps closing._

- GIVEN the venue is wilderness, WHEN checking staging, THEN it can be staged
- GIVEN the venue is town gates, WHEN checking staging, THEN it can be staged
- GIVEN the venue is not now, WHEN checking staging, THEN it cannot be staged
- GIVEN the player is inside a town with a centre, WHEN asked, THEN a town duel can be held
- GIVEN the player is at a town but not inside it, WHEN asked, THEN no town duel can be held
- GIVEN a settlement with no duelling ground, WHEN asked, THEN no town duel can be held
- GIVEN the player inside a castle whose courtyard is its centre, WHEN asked, THEN the duel can be held there
- GIVEN a village whose centre is not the one a duel opens, WHEN asked, THEN no duel can be held there
- GIVEN a town duel is refused, WHEN the open field is chosen instead, THEN it can still be staged
- GIVEN the walkabout gone but the town menu not back, WHEN asked, THEN the square is not opened yet
- GIVEN the town menu just back, WHEN asked, THEN it waits a few frames
- GIVEN a chat just closed or the games conversation still open, WHEN asked, THEN the square waits
- GIVEN a companion following the player, WHEN the duel starts, THEN they stand aside
- GIVEN the two duellists or a townsman, WHEN the duel starts, THEN none is moved

### Duels/DuelStakesPolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc 0: what may be WAGERED on a duel, and what walking away from one costs before a crowd._

- GIVEN an honour only stake, WHEN checking legality, THEN it is always legal
- GIVEN gold the challenger can cover, WHEN checking legality, THEN it is legal
- GIVEN gold beyond the challenger purse, WHEN checking legality, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a non positive gold amount, WHEN checking legality, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a prisoner freedom stake, WHEN the challenger holds one, THEN it is legal else refused
- GIVEN more witnesses to a refusal, WHEN costing the forfeit, THEN the renown hit grows
- GIVEN a vast crowd, WHEN costing the forfeit, THEN it is capped
- GIVEN a negative witness count, WHEN costing the forfeit, THEN it floors to the private baseline

### Duels/DuelVenuePolicyTests.cs

_Duels pillar, Inc A: where a duel is staged, or that it cannot be staged now. At sea there is simply no field to stand on, so that branch must win over every other fact, even a (nonsensical) claim of also being in a settlement: guarding the precedence stops a future bug elsewhere from smuggling a duel onto open water._

- GIVEN the player is in a settlement and not at sea, WHEN choosing, THEN the venue is town gates
- GIVEN the player is on the open land, WHEN choosing, THEN the venue is wilderness
- GIVEN the player is at sea, WHEN choosing, THEN the venue is not now
- GIVEN the player is somehow at sea and in a settlement, WHEN choosing, THEN sea wins and the venue is not now
- GIVEN a settlement with nowhere to fight, WHEN choosing, THEN the duel goes to the open field
- GIVEN a settlement that cannot host one, WHEN choosing, THEN the duel is not silently dropped
