- GIVEN an explicit scope of none, WHEN parsing, THEN the whole preference is None
- GIVEN the marker followed by garbage, WHEN parsing, THEN it degrades to None without throwing

### CorrespondenceRankPolicyTests.cs

_Who deals with the player in person. Gabriel (28/09/2026), landless at the start of a campaign: the Empress Rhagaea wrote to him in her own hand, and had ridden out herself to find him. His ruling: a lord writes in person to equals and superiors, never down, except for love; a greater lord has the letter written under a title with no name; and the same rule keeps her from riding out in person. If this is wrong one way, the great of Calradia keep writing to a nobody; the other way, a lover's letter or a friend's arrives from a clerk._

- GIVEN a ruler and a landless newcomer, WHEN she writes, THEN it is not in her own hand
- GIVEN a player risen to a great house or a throne, WHEN a ruler writes, THEN she writes herself
- GIVEN a private matter, WHEN a ruler writes to a newcomer, THEN she writes it herself
- GIVEN a personal bond, WHEN a ruler writes on any matter, THEN she writes herself
- GIVEN a ruler who holds the player dear, WHEN she writes, THEN she writes herself
- GIVEN two houses, WHEN the greater writes, THEN a gap of two tiers brings a steward
- GIVEN a meeting on the road, WHEN a ruler would ride out to a newcomer, THEN she does not
- GIVEN a letter written on a lords behalf, WHEN signed, THEN it bears a title and no invented name

### CouncilAccordClauseTests.cs

_Council Tier 2: these clauses are injected verbatim into an NPC's 1:1 prompt so the character can reference a still-standing council accord ("the tribute I owe you"). What breaks if a clause is wrong: the NPC contradicts the real ledger to the player's face. The single most dangerous error is DIRECTION, an NPC told it RECEIVES a tribute it actually PAYS, or that it was GRANTED a stipend it in fact grants, so most tests here pin who owes whom. The remaining-term wording is pinned too, since "1 day remains" vs "5 days remain" is the one grammatical seam, and a stray "(0 days remain)" would read as a live accord that has actually lapsed._

- GIVEN a tribute, THEN the clause says the NPC owes the player the daily amount
- GIVEN a tribute with missed days, THEN the clause notes falling behind
- GIVEN a tribute with no missed days, THEN the clause does not note falling behind
- GIVEN a stipend, THEN the clause says the player grants it to the NPC
- GIVEN a pact, THEN the clause frames a non aggression pact between the houses
- GIVEN the NPC is the hostage, THEN the clause says they are held by the player
- GIVEN the NPC pledged a hostage, THEN the clause names who their house gave
- GIVEN an unresolved hostage name, THEN the clause falls back to a readable phrase
- GIVEN a blank player name, THEN the clause falls back to the player
- GIVEN a positive remaining term, THEN the clause states it singular aware
- GIVEN a spent remaining term, THEN the clause carries no days parenthetical

### CouncilAccordPanelTextTests.cs

_Council Tier 3: these lines are what the PLAYER reads on a hero's Encyclopedia page ("Owes you a tribute..."), the third-person mirror of the first-person prompt clauses. What breaks if a line is wrong: the page tells the player the opposite of the truth. As with the prompt describer, DIRECTION is the load-bearing fact (a hero that OWES a tribute must not read as RECEIVING one; a ward's mentor and protege sides must not swap), so most tests pin it. The remaining-term wording is pinned too so a live accord is never shown as "(0 days remain)"._

- GIVEN a tribute, THEN the line reads that the hero owes the player
- GIVEN a stipend, THEN the line reads that the hero receives it from the player
- GIVEN a tribute with missed days, THEN the line notes falling behind
- GIVEN a tribute with no missed days, THEN the line does not note falling behind
- GIVEN a pact, THEN the line frames a non aggression pact
- GIVEN the hero is held hostage, THEN the line reads held by you
- GIVEN the hero gave a hostage, THEN the line names who was given
- GIVEN the hero is a ward protege, THEN the line names their mentor
- GIVEN the hero is a ward mentor, THEN the line names their protege
- GIVEN unresolved names, THEN the lines fall back to readable phrases
- GIVEN a ward or a spent term, THEN no days parenthetical is shown
- GIVEN a positive term, THEN the line states it singular aware

### CouncilAnchorPolicyTests.cs

_Decides which seated member speaks the reply when the player addresses a council. Feeds the council session: the chat renders one primary respondent per player turn (Gabriel's whole-table model is ONE call yielding that spoken reply plus a reaction from each other member who speaks or gestures), and this names them._

- GIVEN members of differing regard, WHEN choosing, THEN the warmest answers
- GIVEN two members of equal regard, WHEN the roster order changes, THEN the same one answers
- GIVEN a single seated member, WHEN choosing, THEN they answer
- GIVEN every member resents the player, WHEN choosing, THEN the least hostile answers
- GIVEN nobody is seated, WHEN choosing, THEN there is no anchor

### CouncilPanelPolicyTests.cs

_Council Tier 1 reconciliation: the Engagements panel and the end-of-council memory seal are both driven from the REAL resolution ledger now, so this policy is the single arbiter of what the player sees on the panel and what gets remembered. What breaks if it is wrong: a withdrawn motion lingers on the panel as if still standing; a committed decision shows the wrong status word; or a seal double-writes a memory for a pledge kind that already recorded its own at the lift (SwearOath via CommitmentBehavior, PledgeAgainst via LaunchPledge), so the NPC ends up with two memories of the same act._

- GIVEN a reversed motion, WHEN deciding panel visibility, THEN it is hidden
- GIVEN any non reversed status, WHEN deciding panel visibility, THEN it is shown
- GIVEN a status, WHEN labelling it for the panel, THEN it reads the expected word
- GIVEN a motion that did not commit, WHEN deciding to seal, THEN it is not sealed
- GIVEN an applied ordinary kind, WHEN deciding to seal, THEN it is sealed
- GIVEN an applied kind that already records its own memory, WHEN deciding to seal, THEN it is not sealed
- GIVEN a self memorising kind in odd casing, WHEN deciding to seal, THEN it is still not sealed

### CouncilSceneImagePolicyTests.cs

_This policy decides which situational SCENE image covers the anchor NPC's portrait on the council's "thinking" placeholder. It feeds straight into the message row's runtime image path (ChatViewModel), so a wrong basename here is a wrong (or missing, file-not-found) image in play. What breaks if it is wrong: the wrong mood art shows during a council turn (e.g. a war council image in a family sitting), or the wrong gendered file is requested and silently fails to load, leaving the ordinary anchor portrait as the only visible fallback._

- GIVEN a generic council outside in desert, THEN the desert image is chosen
- GIVEN a generic council outside in steppe, THEN the steppe image is chosen
- GIVEN a generic council outside on any other terrain, THEN the forest mountains image is chosen
- GIVEN a generic council inside a settlement, THEN the tavern image is chosen
- GIVEN a family council, WHEN the player is female, THEN the female family asset is chosen
- GIVEN a family council, WHEN the player is male, THEN the male family image is chosen
- GIVEN a war council, WHEN male, THEN the male war image is chosen
- GIVEN a war council, WHEN female, THEN the female war image is chosen
- GIVEN a family council but the player is outside, THEN the family image still wins over the outside art
- GIVEN a war council but the player is outside, THEN the war image still wins over the outside art
- GIVEN an assembly, THEN the tavern image is chosen
- GIVEN an assembly but the player is outside, THEN the tavern image still wins

### CourierDispatchPolicyTests.cs

_Courier audit (island report): ONE courier in flight per sender. Without this gate, every queued letter dispatched its own courier, and a slow or stuck courier (a sender on an island, a player at sea) snowballed into a whole pack of couriers riding from the same NPC. These tests pin the dedup rule: any undelivered, unlost letter still bearing a courier party id means the road is occupied, and no second courier may spawn for that sender until it clears._

- GIVEN no letters at all, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is false
- GIVEN an empty letter list, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is false
- GIVEN an undelivered letter with a courier, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is true
- GIVEN a delivered letter with a courier id, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is false
- GIVEN a lost letter with a courier id, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is false
- GIVEN a queued letter without a courier id, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is false
- GIVEN one in flight letter among history, WHEN checking courier in flight, THEN it is true

### CourierHirePlacePolicyTests.cs

_Where the player may hire a courier. raphareish (Nexus, 10/10/2026): "sending letters from castles and villages?" The only door was the town tavern keeper, and castles and villages have no tavern. If this rule is wrong, a castle or a village still offers no courier, or a town shows the service twice (its tavern keeper and its menu)._

- GIVEN a castle or a village, WHEN its menu opens, THEN a courier can be hired there
- GIVEN a town or no settlement, WHEN the menu is read, THEN it offers no courier of its own

### CourierRationPolicyTests.cs

_Documents the courier ration top-up boundary (courier robustness batch, ROADMAP 2026-07-20). The stake: a starved courier party is destroyed by the engine and its letter reported "lost on the road" with no visible cause; the top-up guarantees the pathological waits (the M5 landfall vigil) can never starve a courier._

- GIVEN a courier with any food left, WHEN asked, THEN no top up is granted
- GIVEN a courier whose larder is empty, WHEN asked, THEN the top up is granted

### CourierSecretPolicyTests.cs

_Documents what a lost courier becomes. raphareish (Nexus, 24/09/2026): an intercepted letter simply died, and the sender, asked about it, said he had "been busy", because nothing of the letter was left anywhere. Gabriel's rulings the same day: a lost letter is sent again until it arrives, and an INTERCEPTED one with something to say becomes a secret that travels in the world. The band that took it carries it; whoever defeats that band sends it up to the lord who owns the troop (a Pravend patrol, Pravend's owner); a band that goes home brings it to its hideout; and a secret is only worth something for a while. These tests pin each of those decisions._

- GIVEN bandits destroy a courier, WHEN classified, THEN the letter was intercepted by bandits
- GIVEN a troop at war with the couriers clan destroys it, WHEN classified, THEN the enemy intercepted it
- GIVEN anything else destroys a courier, WHEN classified, THEN the letter is merely lost
- GIVEN any loss, WHEN decided, THEN the letter is sent again
- GIVEN an intercepted letter with substance, WHEN decided, THEN it becomes a secret
- GIVEN an intercepted letter with nothing in it, WHEN decided, THEN it is no secret
- GIVEN a letter merely lost, WHEN decided, THEN it is no secret
- GIVEN a lords party defeats the band, WHEN the secret changes hands, THEN that lord learns it
- GIVEN a town patrol defeats the band, WHEN the secret changes hands, THEN the towns owner learns it
- GIVEN a caravan defeats the band, WHEN the secret changes hands, THEN its owner learns it
- GIVEN a party with both a leader and an owner, WHEN the secret changes hands, THEN the leader reads it
- GIVEN another band defeats the carrier, WHEN the secret changes hands, THEN the winners carry it on
- GIVEN the players side defeats the band, WHEN the secret changes hands, THEN the letter is recovered
- GIVEN the intended recipient ends up with it, WHEN the secret changes hands, THEN it is a delivery not a leak
- GIVEN the sender ends up with his own letter, WHEN the secret changes hands, THEN it goes nowhere
- GIVEN no one answers for the defeating troop, WHEN the secret changes hands, THEN it goes nowhere
- GIVEN a lost courier, WHEN its sender remembers, THEN they know what happened
- GIVEN the letter was sent again, WHEN its sender remembers, THEN they know it went again
- GIVEN a lord comes by an intercepted letter, WHEN they remember it, THEN they know who wrote it to whom and what
- GIVEN a long letter, WHEN its reader remembers it, THEN the quote is cut
- GIVEN a fresh secret, WHEN checked, THEN it still matters
- GIVEN a secret past its shelf life, WHEN checked, THEN it no longer matters

### CourierStuckPolicyTests.cs

_Documents when a live courier's stillness becomes a jam (courier robustness batch, ROADMAP 2026-07-20). The stake: a jammed courier used to burn the whole delivery grace window and then LOSE the letter; these two predicates are what hands the letter back to date-based delivery early enough for it to still arrive._

- GIVEN drift within or at the epsilon, WHEN asked, THEN it does not count as movement
- GIVEN progress beyond the epsilon, WHEN asked, THEN it counts as movement
- GIVEN one day of stillness, WHEN asked, THEN the courier is not yet stuck
- GIVEN stillness reaching the threshold, WHEN asked, THEN the courier is stuck

### CourierWaylayPolicyTests.cs

_Player report (2026-07-09): every courier sent was waylaid, every single time, losing the typed letter for good. Design ruling (2026-07-10): a courier is now IGNORED by default; each bandit party it passes rolls independently (about 1 in 3) to turn hostile and give chase, and even then a fast courier usually outruns the chase. These two independent rolls are what keeps real interception rare and earned, instead of the routine loss the report described._

- GIVEN a roll, WHEN deciding hostility, THEN only the bottom third turns hostile
- GIVEN a negative or out of range roll, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not hostile
- GIVEN the full roll range, WHEN counting hostiles, THEN it is about one in three
- GIVEN a chase, WHEN deciding the catch, THEN the fast courier usually escapes
- GIVEN both filters, WHEN combined, THEN the effective per band waylay is well under the chase rate
- GIVEN no band has rolled yet, WHEN checking the budget, THEN a band may still roll
- GIVEN one band has rolled, WHEN checking the budget, THEN a second band may still roll
- GIVEN the cap of bands has rolled, WHEN checking the budget, THEN no further band may roll

### CourtActionPolicyTests.cs

_This is the "hollow promise" fix (CHANGELOG v1.20.5): before this pillar a lord could agree in chat to lend troops or influence and nothing would actually happen. CourtActionPolicy is the pure math the bridge re-validates at execution time ("the prompt is advice, the bridge is law") for every court verb (give_influence, lend_troops, sway_opinion, pledge_against, release_prisoner, plus the give_troops/ spend_influence reciprocals). A wrong number here either breaks a promise the LLM just made to the player, lets an NPC give away resources (their own troops, their clan's influence) no real lord would part with, or lets a verb reach a target it must never touch (an NPC's own close kin, or a faction leader whose fate is reserved for the leader-only war-decision system, never a conversation)._

- GIVEN zero or negative relation, WHEN computing the influence grant, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN a modest relation, WHEN computing the influence grant, THEN it scales with relation
- GIVEN a very deep relation, WHEN computing the influence grant, THEN it is capped
- GIVEN a clan with far more influence than the grant, WHEN checking affordability, THEN it can afford it
- GIVEN a clan with barely more influence than the grant, WHEN checking affordability, THEN it cannot afford it
- GIVEN exactly the buffer multiple, WHEN checking affordability, THEN the threshold is inclusive
- GIVEN zero or negative relation, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN a generous relation and ample room, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN it scales with relation
- GIVEN an extremely deep relation, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN it is capped
- GIVEN a small party, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN the npc never drops below 60 percent strength
- GIVEN the player party nearly full, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN it never exceeds the players room
- GIVEN no spare troops, WHEN computing the troop loan, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN a party below the weakened fraction of a large enough size limit, WHEN checking if weakened, THEN it is
- GIVEN a party at or above the weakened fraction, WHEN checking if weakened, THEN it is not
- GIVEN a size limit below the minimum worth reinforcing, WHEN checking if weakened, THEN a tiny escort never counts
- GIVEN relation below the charity floor, WHEN computing the troop gift, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN ample spare troops and room, WHEN computing the troop gift, THEN it scales with relation
- GIVEN an extremely deep relation, WHEN computing the troop gift, THEN it is capped
- GIVEN the player has barely more than the floor, WHEN computing the troop gift, THEN it never dips the player below the floor
- GIVEN the player at or under the floor, WHEN computing the troop gift, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN the npc party nearly full, WHEN computing the troop gift, THEN it never exceeds the npcs room
- GIVEN a clan influence below the need threshold, WHEN checking if a clan needs influence, THEN it does
- GIVEN a clan influence at or above the need threshold, WHEN checking if a clan needs influence, THEN it does not
- GIVEN swaying against a target, WHEN computing the delta, THEN it is negative
- GIVEN swaying for a target, WHEN computing the delta, THEN it is positive
- GIVEN a pool with close kin and a stranger, WHEN selecting a sway target, THEN kin is never chosen
- GIVEN only close kin in the pool, WHEN selecting a sway target, THEN none is chosen
- GIVEN several non kin candidates, WHEN selecting a sway target, THEN the most negative relation wins
- GIVEN only positive relation non kin candidates, WHEN selecting a sway target, THEN the least friendly still wins
- GIVEN an empty pool, WHEN selecting a sway target, THEN none is chosen
- GIVEN the worst regarded candidate is a faction leader, WHEN selecting a sway target, THEN the ruler is skipped
- GIVEN a bitterly disliked non kin lord, WHEN selecting a pledge target, THEN they are chosen
- GIVEN close kin held in the same enmity, WHEN selecting a pledge target, THEN kin is never chosen
- GIVEN no candidate meets the animosity threshold, WHEN selecting a pledge target, THEN none is chosen
- GIVEN several eligible rivals, WHEN selecting a pledge target, THEN the deepest enmity wins
- GIVEN an empty pool, WHEN selecting a pledge target, THEN none is chosen
- GIVEN no prior recorded day, WHEN checking the cooldown, THEN it has elapsed
- GIVEN the cooldown window not yet passed, WHEN checking it, THEN it has not elapsed
- GIVEN exactly the cooldown length, WHEN checking it, THEN the threshold is inclusive
- GIVEN well past the cooldown window, WHEN checking it, THEN it has elapsed
- GIVEN a member of the players own clan, WHEN testing the household gate, THEN they are excluded
- GIVEN the player themself, WHEN testing the household gate, THEN they are excluded
- GIVEN an unrelated captured lord, WHEN testing the household gate, THEN they remain tradeable

### CourtMarkerAndSwayGroundingTests.cs

_Maifailun (Nexus, 01/10/2026, two screenshots): the player asked Lady Bolat whether she had enjoyed the tournament and bowed; she "turned against Lady Decantia (-6)", a lady nobody had named, and her Memories panel then read "At the player's word, turned against Lady Decantia. [cr_sway:lord_1_1_2]". Two faults. A sway "at the player's word" fired with no word said about that person, so a third party lost regard and a hidden grudge was queued for nothing. And a cooldown marker, kept in the memory's text since before the structured cooldown store existed, was shown to the player and read by the character. If these rules are wrong, either a bow still costs a stranger her friends, or a real case made by name is refused, or an old save's cooldowns restart._

- GIVEN the player only greeted and bowed, WHEN a sway against someone unnamed arrives, THEN there was no case
- GIVEN the player spoke of her by name, WHEN the sway arrives, THEN the case was made
- GIVEN a name only inside another word or a title alone, WHEN checked, THEN nobody was named
- GIVEN a name in a script without spaces, WHEN the player wrote it, THEN it is found
- GIVEN a path with no conversation, WHEN checked, THEN the rule stands aside
- GIVEN an old memory carrying a sway marker, WHEN scrubbed, THEN the text is clean and the cooldown is kept
- GIVEN a later cooldown already stored, WHEN an older marker is scrubbed, THEN the later day stands
- GIVEN a clean profile, WHEN scrubbed, THEN nothing changes

### CourtshipTrialPolicyTests.cs

_CourtshipTrialBehavior calls CourtshipTrialPolicy.IsComplete on every daily tick to decide whether a marriage prospect who has been riding at the player's side has served the trial she set and should now reach out to accept the match. These tests pin the STAKE the author's story hangs on: the trial is passed ONLY when the prospect is STILL accompanying (never abandoned it part way), is STILL an eligible prospect (no war or captivity has since fallen between them), AND the full term has elapsed. If this gate is wrong, a prospect who walked away, or one a war has since barred, is treated as having accepted, and the marriage opens on a promise she never kept; or a served trial never completes and the whole feature is dead. The term itself must stay at two Calradian seasons so the behavior, the self-test, and this file can never drift onto different clocks._

- GIVEN the trial term, WHEN read, THEN it is two calradian seasons
- GIVEN still accompanying and eligible past the full term, WHEN checked, THEN the trial is complete
- GIVEN exactly the term has elapsed, WHEN checked, THEN the trial is complete
- GIVEN one day short of the term, WHEN checked, THEN the trial is not complete
- GIVEN no longer accompanying, WHEN checked, THEN the trial is not complete however long it ran
- GIVEN no longer an eligible prospect, WHEN checked, THEN the trial is not complete
- GIVEN the trial only just began, WHEN checked, THEN it is not complete

### CraftWeaponPolicyTests.cs

_Lordfadooboo (Nexus, 01/10/2026): "a blacksmithing quest". Gabriel: the kind of weapon is part of the deal. A lord asks the player to forge him a sword, an axe, a spear; only a weapon the PLAYER forged, of that kind, completes it._

- GIVEN a lords word for a weapon, WHEN normalized, THEN it is a kind the smithy makes
- GIVEN something the smithy does not make, WHEN normalized, THEN there is no kind
- GIVEN a weapon the player forged of the kind asked, WHEN handed over, THEN it answers the task
- GIVEN a bought weapon or the wrong kind, WHEN handed over, THEN it does not answer
- GIVEN a forge key, WHEN read back, THEN it gives its kind and no other key does

### CrvidTests.cs

_CR's own video format (.crvid: a zip of JPEG frames and a manifest) and the clock that says which frame shows. Gabriel (11/10/2026): the game's video player stops in conversations, so CR plays its own; "je jouerais le video 1 seule fois et reviendrais au premier frame", with a loop icon to make it start over. If the manifest rules are wrong, a broken file reaches the decoder or a good one is refused; if the clock is wrong, a video runs at the wrong speed, never ends, or ends on its last frame instead of its first._

- GIVEN the measured size at 30 fps, WHEN checked, THEN it plays
- GIVEN a landscape frame of the same area, WHEN checked, THEN it plays
- GIVEN a frame larger than measured, WHEN checked, THEN the author is told
- GIVEN an unknown version a zero rate and no frame, WHEN checked, THEN each is reported
- GIVEN the first frame, WHEN named, THEN it is frames 000001 jpg
- GIVEN a 30 fps clip, WHEN one second has passed, THEN frame 30 shows
- GIVEN a clip played once, WHEN its end has passed, THEN it is finished on its first frame
- GIVEN a looped clip, WHEN its end has passed, THEN it starts over
- GIVEN no frame, WHEN asked the moment, THEN it is finished

### DayMoodFactsPolicyTests.cs

_The mood of the day, increment 2: what the game says about a character's day, read into the facts the draw weighs (ROADMAP, THE MOOD OF THE DAY, Ruling 2: a constrained draw, never a random roll; "a lord who has just lost a fief must not wake up cheerful"). If a reading is wrong here, a wounded lord at a feast draws high spirits, or a captive draws an ordinary day, which is the incoherence the whole feature exists to avoid._

- GIVEN a real wound, WHEN the day is read, THEN he is hurt and a scratch is not
- GIVEN an empty treasury, WHEN read, THEN the coffers are empty
- GIVEN a realm losing its war, WHEN read, THEN it has lately lost and not won
- GIVEN a realm winning its war, WHEN read, THEN it has lately won
- GIVEN an even war or peace, WHEN read, THEN the realm neither lost nor won
- GIVEN a hall not under siege, WHEN read, THEN he is at ease and in the field or a besieged hall he is not
- GIVEN the small hours, WHEN read, THEN it is deep night and the evening is not
- GIVEN a prisoner, WHEN read, THEN he is captive
- GIVEN a recent blow or gladness, WHEN read, THEN the day carries it
- GIVEN his self esteem, WHEN read, THEN low high or neither
- GIVEN no readings, WHEN read, THEN it is an ordinary day

### DeedUnavailabilityPolicyTests.cs

_how the mod tells an NPC, before it speaks, which deeds this conversation cannot deliver._

- GIVEN one shut deed, WHEN composed, THEN the deed the reason and the ban on promising are all there
- GIVEN any shut deed, WHEN composed, THEN the model is told to speak in character and not quote the list
- GIVEN nothing shut, WHEN composed, THEN the prompt carries not one character
- GIVEN half a refusal, WHEN composed, THEN it is dropped rather than half rendered
- GIVEN a broken refusal beside a sound one, WHEN composed, THEN only the broken one is lost
- GIVEN more shut deeds than the cap, WHEN composed, THEN the block stays bounded and never repeats a deed
- GIVEN the same deed in different case, WHEN cleaned, THEN it is still one deed
- GIVEN untidy reasons, WHEN cleaned, THEN they are tidied rather than lost
- GIVEN several shut deeds, WHEN composed, THEN each is one line and no verb id is ever shown
- GIVEN several shut deeds, WHEN cleaned, THEN they keep the order the verbs gave them

### DeliveryQuestMatcherTests.cs

_that the lord who asked for a prisoner, and receives one, has had his bargain kept._

- GIVEN a bargain for any lord of a realm, WHEN one of its lords is handed over, THEN it is settled
- GIVEN a bargain for one realm, WHEN a lord of another is handed over, THEN nothing is settled
- GIVEN a bargain naming one lord, WHEN a different lord of his realm is handed over, THEN nothing is settled
- GIVEN a bargain already settled, WHEN another captive is handed over, THEN it is not settled again
- GIVEN an outstanding bargain of another kind, WHEN a matching captive is handed over, THEN nothing is settled
- GIVEN two captives who would both settle it, WHEN the player named one, THEN that one is delivered
- GIVEN no usable preference, WHEN a delivery is sought, THEN the first captive who settles it is chosen
- GIVEN no captive settles anything, WHEN a delivery is sought, THEN there is none

### DiagnosticSubjectPickerTests.cs

_tashmetu (Nexus, 02/10/2026, testing Bridges to Remember on 2.6.10): "cr.prompt_dump <name> picks the first match when names repeat. Could it also accept a hero id?" A diagnostic that silently dumps the WRONG namesake's prompt is worse than none: the modder reads a stranger's prompt and draws conclusions about the character they meant. The picker now takes an exact hero id first, prefers an exact name over a fragment, and says who else answered to the name, with their ids, so the next call can be exact._

- GIVEN a hero id, WHEN picking, THEN that exact hero is dumped
- GIVEN namesakes, WHEN picking by name, THEN the others are named with their ids
- GIVEN an exact name and a longer name containing it, WHEN picking, THEN the exact name wins alone
- GIVEN only a fragment, WHEN picking, THEN the hero whose name contains it is chosen
- GIVEN no match, WHEN picking, THEN nobody is chosen

### DialogueEntryTracePolicyTests.cs

_Pins the one diagnostic line the dialogue collector writes at the end of every hero conversation._

- GIVEN entry was offered, WHEN composing the line, THEN the verdict reads YES
- GIVEN entry was not offered and the trail ends on a foreign hub, WHEN composing the line, THEN it names the foreign token
- GIVEN no tokens were captured, WHEN composing the line, THEN the trail reads as none captured
- GIVEN a trail longer than the cap, WHEN composing the line, THEN it is truncated with a count of the rest
- GIVEN entry was offered but the trail shows no registered hub, WHEN composing the line, THEN it notes the disagreement
- GIVEN entry was not offered but the trail reached a registered hub, WHEN composing the line, THEN it notes the disagreement
- GIVEN no kind string is available, WHEN composing the line, THEN it omits the kind rather than failing

### DialogueHeuristicsTests.cs

_Documents the pure chat heuristics extracted from ChatViewModel: refusal detection, the "NPC asked a question" cue, witness-name role stripping, and response action lookup._

- GIVEN an out of character refusal, WHEN checked, THEN it is detected case insensitively
- GIVEN ordinary in character speech, WHEN checked, THEN it is not a refusal
- GIVEN the safety refusal a scene narrator wrote in play, WHEN checked, THEN it is detected
- GIVEN a character or narrator who cannot do something in the story, WHEN checked, THEN it is not a refusal
- GIVEN blank text, WHEN checked, THEN it is not a refusal
- GIVEN dialogue ending in a question, WHEN checked, THEN true
- GIVEN a question followed by a stage direction, WHEN checked, THEN still true
- GIVEN a statement, WHEN checked, THEN false
- GIVEN a name with a parenthetical role, WHEN stripped, THEN only the name remains
- GIVEN a plain name, WHEN stripped, THEN it is only trimmed
- GIVEN a response carrying the action, WHEN checked, THEN true case insensitively
- GIVEN a response without the action or a null response, WHEN checked, THEN false

### DivorceAnnouncePolicyTests.cs

_DIVORCE, announce phase (romance audit C1/C2). This state machine decides, each tick, what to do with a spouse demand whose refusal clock has not started because its announcing letter has not (yet) reached the player. It exists to kill two real soft-locks: a letter that never arrives (LLM failure or intercepted courier) used to freeze the arc forever with no refusal, no escalation, no re-announce; and a stale delivered letter from a prior cycle used to falsely start the clock, stamping a refusal for a demand the player never saw. Every step below guards one of those failures, so get a boundary wrong and either the arc dead-locks or the player is blindsided, both of which this pillar's CHANGELOG line ("you are never blindsided") explicitly forbids._

- GIVEN the current letter is delivered, WHEN resolving, THEN the clock starts
- GIVEN an undelivered letter still within its window, WHEN resolving, THEN it waits
- GIVEN the window lapsed undelivered and retries remain, WHEN resolving, THEN it reannounces
- GIVEN the retry budget is exhausted, WHEN resolving, THEN it forces a notice
- GIVEN exactly the window has elapsed, WHEN resolving, THEN it acts rather than waiting

### DivorceBlockPolicyTests.cs

_DIVORCE: classifying why a dissolution was refused at its deadline (romance audit M-D2/M-D6). The host used to treat EVERY refusal as the household resisting: a false "closed ranks" record plus a relation penalty on the player, and for the player's own walk-out an infinite silent retry. This policy splits the two real causes so the host reacts honestly. Get it wrong and either a pregnant couple's divorce punishes the player for biology (transient misread as resistance) or the "hand your spouse a party to freeze the divorce" exploit reopens (structural misread as none). Precedence matters: a pregnancy must win over a role, because while either party is pregnant the marriage cannot dissolve AT ALL, so releasing a party would be premature and pointless._

- GIVEN no pregnancy captivity or role, WHEN classifying, THEN no mechanical block
- GIVEN a pregnancy, WHEN classifying, THEN it is transient
- GIVEN a captivity, WHEN classifying, THEN it is transient
- GIVEN only a party leadership, WHEN classifying, THEN it is structural
- GIVEN only a clan leadership, WHEN classifying, THEN it is structural
- GIVEN only a governorship, WHEN classifying, THEN it is structural
- GIVEN both a pregnancy and a role, WHEN classifying, THEN transient wins

### DivorceConsequencePolicyTests.cs

_Divorce, the tiered CONSEQUENCE ENGINE: given already-resolved facts (MutualConsent, sourced from PlayerDivorceConsentPolicy.SpouseConsents in Phase 2a, or DivorcePolicy.IsMutualConsent in Phase 2b; MarriageWasBlessed; the spouse's prior regard), this policy decides who reacts and how, across three stakes: the spouse, their immediate family, and their three best friends. DivorceProcessBehavior (ApplyReactionTo / ApplySpouseReaction) consumes the verdict almost blindly: PlantGrudge plants a real, persisted grudge at the returned magnitude; Relief/Gratitude only fires when RelationNudge is nonzero (the caller checks the NUDGE, not the Kind), so a Kind/field mismatch here silently drops an intended reaction on a live save._

- GIVEN a mutual consent spouse, WHEN asking whether the spouse grudges, THEN it is false
- GIVEN a mutual divorce from a blessed marriage, WHEN resolving, THEN the spouse is relieved and family and friends feel nothing
- GIVEN a mutual divorce from an unblessed marriage, WHEN resolving, THEN the family is grateful and nobody grudges
- GIVEN a none reaction, WHEN inspecting it, THEN both the magnitude and the relation nudge are zero
- GIVEN a plant grudge reaction, WHEN inspecting it, THEN the relation nudge is zero and the magnitude is positive
- GIVEN a relief or gratitude reaction, WHEN inspecting it, THEN the relation nudge is positive and the grudge magnitude is zero
- GIVEN a repudiated spouse from a blessed marriage, WHEN resolving, THEN the spouse the family and the friends all grudge
- GIVEN a repudiated spouse from an unblessed marriage, WHEN resolving, THEN the spouse and friends grudge but the family is grateful
- GIVEN a repudiated spouse, WHEN asking whether the spouse grudges, THEN it is true
- GIVEN a repudiated spouse, WHEN resolving, THEN the spouse grudge is known to the player
- GIVEN a repudiated spouse, WHEN resolving, THEN the family grudge is known but the friend grudge is hidden
- GIVEN the repudiated grid, WHEN reading every grudge magnitude, THEN all clear the formation floor
- GIVEN a repudiated spouse who already disliked the player, WHEN resolving, THEN the grudge is larger
- GIVEN null facts, WHEN asking whether the spouse grudges, THEN it is false
- GIVEN null facts, WHEN resolving, THEN it throws

### DivorceDemandTimeoutPolicyTests.cs

_Documents that the divorce-demand refusal countdown only starts once the letter has actually been delivered: a demand not yet delivered can never time out into a refusal, however long it waits._

- GIVEN the clock has not started, WHEN checking ClockStarted, THEN it is false
- GIVEN a real start day, WHEN checking ClockStarted, THEN it is true
- GIVEN the clock has not started, WHEN checking refusal due, THEN it is never due even far in the future
- GIVEN the clock started today, WHEN the grace period is twelve days, THEN it is not yet due
- GIVEN the clock started eleven days ago, WHEN the grace period is twelve days, THEN it is not yet due
- GIVEN the clock started exactly twelve days ago, WHEN the grace period is twelve days, THEN it is due
- GIVEN the clock started twenty days ago, WHEN the grace period is twelve days, THEN it is due

### DivorcePolicyTests.cs

_Divorce, Phase 1: an NPC can be PERSUADED to end their OWN marriage to another NPC. This file is the whole rulebook: eligibility gates, the sliding trust requirement, the daily spousal-resistance roll, process timers, culture-scaled scandal, and the signed consequence magnitudes (also reused by the player's own Phase 2a/2b divorce)._

- GIVEN a barely unhappy marriage, WHEN asking the required bond, THEN the full trust is needed
- GIVEN a default culture, WHEN scaling the scandal magnitude, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN a hero still in their origin clan, WHEN checking for a clan move, THEN it is false
- GIVEN a hero whose clan differs from their origin, WHEN checking for a clan move, THEN it is true
- GIVEN a mildly unhappy stranger, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN the bond gate still refuses
- GIVEN a missing clan id, WHEN checking for a clan move, THEN it is false
- GIVEN a null culture, WHEN pricing the scandal, THEN it uses the default multiplier
- GIVEN a plain spouse and a high roll, WHEN resisting, THEN the process continues
- GIVEN a plain spouse and a low roll, WHEN resisting, THEN the process aborts
- GIVEN a plain spouse and a mid roll, WHEN resisting, THEN the process is delayed
- GIVEN a process already active, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN a proud calculating spouse, WHEN resisting, THEN the abort chance is wider
- GIVEN a repudiated spouse, WHEN measuring the hits, THEN the full penalties apply
- GIVEN a spouse who fought the estrangement, WHEN reading consent, THEN they were repudiated
- GIVEN a spouse who never resisted and bears no grudge, WHEN reading consent, THEN the divorce is mutual
- GIVEN a hateful spouse who happened not to resist, WHEN reading consent, THEN it is not mutual
- GIVEN a start day, WHEN computing the end day, THEN it adds the process duration
- GIVEN a sturgian culture id in mixed case, WHEN pricing the scandal, THEN it still matches
- GIVEN a sturgian culture, WHEN pricing the scandal, THEN it is halved
- GIVEN a sturgian culture, WHEN scaling the scandal magnitude, THEN it is halved
- GIVEN a wretched marriage, WHEN asking the required bond, THEN no bond is needed at all
- GIVEN an end day, WHEN extending it for a delay, THEN it adds the delay days
- GIVEN an npc courting the player, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN the bond gate is waived
- GIVEN an unlisted culture, WHEN pricing the scandal, THEN it uses the default multiplier
- GIVEN either party is a prisoner, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN either party is pregnant, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN every gate holds, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it is eligible
- GIVEN misery between the anchors, WHEN asking the required bond, THEN it slides down linearly
- GIVEN mutual consent, WHEN measuring the ex spouses hits, THEN relief stings far less than repudiation
- GIVEN negative traits, WHEN resisting, THEN they never shrink the base chance
- GIVEN null facts, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the bond with the player falls short, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the cumulative delay cap is reached, WHEN resisting, THEN an abort can still land
- GIVEN the cumulative delay cap is reached, WHEN resisting, THEN no further delay is offered
- GIVEN the leaving spouse governs a settlement, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the leaving spouse heads their own clan, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the leaving spouse leads a party, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the marriage is not unhappy enough, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the npc is married to the player, WHEN checking eligibility, THEN phase one refuses
- GIVEN the npc is not married, WHEN evaluating eligibility, THEN it refuses

### DivorceProcessTests.cs

_Divorce, Phase 1: the PAIR lock (romance audit M-D3). A divorce process must be locked by the couple, not the requester alone. Persuading spouse A then spouse B of the SAME marriage on one day means two DIFFERENT requesters, so a requester-only lock lets the second through; that opened two crossed processes, the second resolving on a false "household closed ranks" and docking the player's relation for nothing. PairAlreadyEngaged is the pure rule the host now gates Create on; these tests pin that either party, in either slot, is caught._

- GIVEN a process where A estranges B, WHEN asking about B as the new requester, THEN the pair is engaged
- GIVEN a process where A estranges B, WHEN asking about A again, THEN the pair is engaged
- GIVEN a process for A and B, WHEN asking about an unrelated pair, THEN it is free
- GIVEN only a terminal process, WHEN asking about its parties, THEN they are free
- GIVEN no active processes, WHEN asking, THEN it is free and does not throw

### DuelPublicityPolicyTests.cs

_fkasad (Nexus, 01/10/2026): a duel won against a faction's head in his own keep logged "0 witness(es)" with fifteen lords inside, and its news weighed 6 where any battle with a lord weighs 40. If these rules are wrong, a duel before a whole court counts as fought in an empty field, beating a ruler is smaller news than beating a nobody's levy, or a crowded keep makes one duel worth a war in renown and influence._

- GIVEN no conversation witnesses but a full hall, WHEN the audience is counted, THEN the hall counts
- GIVEN a crowded keep, WHEN the audience is counted, THEN it is capped
- GIVEN who was fought and before whom, WHEN the news is weighed, THEN station and audience carry it
- GIVEN a duel refused, WHEN the news is weighed, THEN it travels half as far

### EarnRegardPolicyTests.cs

_Lordfadooboo (Nexus, 01/10/2026): "a quest to earn the good graces of another lord (earn 10 regard with Mesui of the Khuzites) but issued by a different lord". These rules decide who may be named, how far the player must go, and when the deed is done. If they are wrong, a lord sends the player to charm a corpse or a child, a task completes before the player has done anything, or a task is set that can never complete because there is nothing left to win._

- GIVEN a living adult third person, WHEN named, THEN the task may be given
- GIVEN someone dead or a child, WHEN named, THEN no such task exists
- GIVEN the giver or the player, WHEN named, THEN no such task exists
- GIVEN the regard when the task is given, WHEN the goal is set, THEN it lies ten beyond it
- GIVEN a target who already holds the player dear, WHEN the goal is set, THEN there is no task
- GIVEN a short or absent deadline, WHEN the task is given, THEN it runs at least a month or stays open
- GIVEN the targets regard, WHEN checked against the goal, THEN only reaching it completes the task

### EnemyMeetingOfferTests.cs

_Whether a lord who rode up to the player on the road asks first, by a popup, or opens the conversation at once._

- GIVEN a lord of a realm at war with the player, WHEN he reaches them, THEN the word is offered first
- GIVEN a territorial patrol, WHEN he reaches the player, THEN the word is offered first
- GIVEN a lord at peace with the player, WHEN he reaches them, THEN the meeting opens at once

### EnvoyMandatePolicyTests.cs

_Documents how an envoy is held to his lord's mandate (prisoner exchange, increment 3; fkasad, Nexus, 25/09/2026; Gabriel: always an anonymous envoy, never the lord). The envoy is a model, and a model can write any deal. Whatever it writes is rebound here before the bridge runs it in the lord's name, so a stray action is the difference between an envoy who negotiates and one who spends his lord's gold on a captive nobody sent him for._

- GIVEN a lord, WHEN his envoy is named, THEN he has no name of his own
- GIVEN a ransom within the ceiling, WHEN bound, THEN it runs for the wanted captive at the agreed price
- GIVEN a ransom above the ceiling, WHEN bound, THEN it is held to the ceiling
- GIVEN a ransom with no usable price, WHEN bound, THEN it is offered at the ceiling
- GIVEN a captive given for nothing, WHEN bound, THEN it is a gift and no gold moves
- GIVEN a plain acceptance of a final offer, WHEN the envoy writes zero, THEN it closes at that offer not as a gift
- GIVEN the player named a price, WHEN the envoy writes free, THEN it is not a gift
- GIVEN no usable price and an offer on the table, WHEN bound, THEN it closes at that offer
- GIVEN a paid ransom, WHEN bound, THEN it is not a gift
- GIVEN a ceiling and an honest floor, WHEN the envoy opens, THEN he offers half the ceiling within both
- GIVEN a ransom naming another captive, WHEN bound, THEN it is for the wanted captive all the same
- GIVEN an authorised exchange, WHEN bound, THEN it trades the wanted captive for the one offered
- GIVEN no one to offer in exchange, WHEN an exchange is written, THEN nothing runs
- GIVEN any other action, WHEN bound, THEN nothing runs
- GIVEN a regard band, WHEN worded for the envoy, THEN it reads after regards

### EscapedTargetPolicyTests.cs

_fkasad (Nexus, 01/10/2026): since Bannerlord 1.3 a beaten lord often escapes and stays out of reach for weeks, so a "capture that lord" task cannot be finished in time through no fault of the player. "Returning the mission with a 'I didn't catch the one you wanted but I've got those two instead' kind of discussion would fix this. Or taking the [CR] memory into account. After all you've beaten him and won the battle." Ruled by Gabriel the same day: the victory counts (a reduced reward, at the giver's word), a lord of the same realm may stand in for him (if the giver takes him), and a deadline that runs out while the target is a fugitive, or after the player beat him, costs the player nothing. If these rules are wrong, the player is punished for the game's own escape mechanic, or paid in full for a capture that never happened, or a giver is forced to take a substitute he never agreed to._

- GIVEN the player beat the target, WHEN the battle is read, THEN capture and delivery tasks record it and attack needs none
- GIVEN a capture task, WHEN the target was or was not beaten, THEN only a recorded victory may settle it
- GIVEN a victory accepted in place of the capture, WHEN paid, THEN it is half the reward
- GIVEN the target beaten, WHEN a captive of his realm is handed over, THEN he may stand in
- GIVEN another realm or no victory, WHEN a captive is handed over, THEN he does not stand in
- GIVEN a deadline running out, WHEN the target is a fugitive or was beaten, THEN it lapses without fault
- GIVEN a target free and never beaten or another kind of task, WHEN the deadline runs out, THEN the old rule stands

### EscortCostPolicyTests.cs

_EscortCostPolicy prices the per-correspondent ARMED ESCORT: a one-off fee that makes a whole correspondence thread's couriers immune to bandit interception. It exists for the player report (2026-08-05) that letters to a far correspondent were lost almost every time; the escort is the premium guarantee of delivery over that long road, and its price is what these tests pin. The mod charges exactly what EscortPremium returns and, on payment, sets NpcProfile.EscortedChannel, so a wrong price here is real coin wrongly taken (or the feature made trivially cheap). The distance comes from the engine's MapDistanceModel, so the clamps also guard the price against an unexpectedly large or malformed distance value._

- GIVEN a zero distance, WHEN pricing, THEN it charges the base premium
- GIVEN a longer road, WHEN pricing, THEN it costs more than a short one
- GIVEN a mid range distance, WHEN pricing, THEN it is the base plus the scaled rate
- GIVEN a very large distance, WHEN pricing, THEN it is capped at the maximum
- GIVEN a negative or NaN distance, WHEN pricing, THEN it falls back to the base premium
- GIVEN any distance, WHEN pricing, THEN it stays within the clamp band

### EstrangementProgressPolicyTests.cs

_Stolen Love pillar, Increment 2: paces how an affair unravels a married NPC's marriage. The behavior reads Phase each day to decide what the NPC voices, and hands off to Increment 3's divorce at ReadyToDivorce; it reads ProgressPercent for the fraying note. If the day spans drift, a marriage of years either collapses in a week (absurd) or never reaches the payoff and the pillar stalls at "they seem troubled" forever, and the difficulty scaling is what makes a hard-won heart break its vows slower than an already-dead marriage._

- GIVEN negative elapsed, WHEN reading the phase, THEN it is none
- GIVEN within the opening grace, WHEN reading the phase, THEN it has not frayed yet
- GIVEN the ready boundary, WHEN reading the phase, THEN it flips to ready to divorce
- GIVEN a harder seduction, WHEN the same days pass, THEN it unravels slower
- GIVEN elapsed days, WHEN reading the progress percent, THEN it stays within zero to one hundred
- GIVEN a declaration, WHEN checking the divorce due date, THEN it waits the delay

### ExCompanionDivorcePolicyTests.cs

_ExCompanionDivorcePolicy is the divorce half of "marry your own companion". A wanderer companion promoted to Lord to wed the player has NO birth house: the engine stamped the player's OWN clan as their OriginClan at promotion. CR's divorce relocates a leaving spouse by their OriginClan, so without this rule the ex-companion would either linger forever as a Lord of the player's house or be scattered into a random clan. These tests pin exactly which divorcing spouse is returned to the free-wanderer pool: only the player's own promoted ex-companion (origin is the player's clan or none, no structural post), never a born-noble spouse and never an NPC-NPC divorce. If any clause flipped, a real lord could be dissolved into a wanderer, or an ex-companion left to scatter, the two failure modes the guard exists to prevent._

- GIVEN a promoted ex companion spouse with no house and no post, WHEN divorcing, THEN returned to the wanderer pool
- GIVEN a born noble spouse with a real birth house, WHEN divorcing, THEN not returned to the pool
- GIVEN the candidate is the player, WHEN divorcing, THEN not returned to the pool
- GIVEN no player in the marriage, WHEN divorcing, THEN not returned to the pool
- GIVEN an ex companion holding a structural post, WHEN divorcing, THEN left in place not pooled

### ExternalEventRulesTests.cs

_Documents the ingestion-door rules: magnitude is clamped to the carryable range, a summary is bounded to the blob-safe length (and an empty one is rejected), and a source is held to a daily quota._

- GIVEN a negative magnitude, WHEN clamping, THEN it floors at zero
- GIVEN an overlarge magnitude, WHEN clamping, THEN it caps at the max
- GIVEN a magnitude in range, WHEN clamping, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN an empty or whitespace summary, WHEN bounding, THEN it is rejected as null
- GIVEN an overlong summary, WHEN bounding, THEN it is truncated to the blob guard
- GIVEN a padded summary, WHEN bounding, THEN it is trimmed
- GIVEN a source at its quota, WHEN checking, THEN it is throttled
- GIVEN a siege of the players town by a lord, WHEN fed, THEN it is news not the players deed
- GIVEN an execution by the players decree, WHEN fed, THEN it is the players own deed
- GIVEN a fact with no actors, WHEN fed, THEN it is news

### ExternalMissionRulesTests.cs

_Documents the mission-registration-door rules: title/description bounded to the blob-safe length (and an empty one rejected), reward gold/relation clamped to CR's own payable caps (the external mod cannot promise a fortune or a flood of regard), an optional deadline normalised, and the id+source identity required._

- GIVEN a negative gold reward, WHEN clamping, THEN it floors at zero
- GIVEN an overlarge gold reward, WHEN clamping, THEN it caps at the max
- GIVEN a relation reward above the gated cap, WHEN clamping, THEN it caps at the quest reward limit
- GIVEN an empty or whitespace title, WHEN bounding, THEN it is rejected as null
- GIVEN an overlong description, WHEN bounding, THEN it is truncated to the blob guard
- GIVEN a padded title, WHEN bounding, THEN it is trimmed
- GIVEN no deadline, WHEN normalising, THEN it stays open ended
- GIVEN an overlong deadline, WHEN normalising, THEN it caps at a year
- GIVEN a deadline in range, WHEN normalising, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN a missing id or source, WHEN checking identity, THEN it is not registerable

### ExternalVerbContractTests.cs

_ExternalVerbContract is the exact shape a future public Actions.Register(...) API will hand back and forth with a third-party mod, wrapped mod-side by ExternalVerbAdapter into the SAME VerbRegistry CR's own conversation verbs run through. If Name routing, or any of the three Func slots (IsEligible/TeachingText/ Execute), misbehaves, or an unset callback throws instead of simply reading as "absent", an external mod's verb could silently vanish from what the LLM is taught, or crash the conversation it is plugged into. These tests pin the plain-Func plumbing before any engine coupling is added on top of it._

- GIVEN a contract with an eligibility callback, WHEN invoking it with facts, THEN the callback runs with those facts
- GIVEN a contract with a teaching callback, WHEN invoking it, THEN it returns that callbacks text
- GIVEN a contract with an execute callback, WHEN invoking it with a request, THEN it returns that callbacks result
- GIVEN an execute callback setting a recap, WHEN invoking it, THEN the recap round trips on success and refusal
- GIVEN an execute callback that sets no recap, WHEN invoking it, THEN the recap is null
- GIVEN a contract with no callbacks set, WHEN reading them, THEN they are null rather than throwing

### EyeColorBandsTests.cs

_The words an NPC uses for someone's eye colour, banded from Bannerlord's 0..1 slider offset._

- GIVEN Rhagaeas measured offset, WHEN describing, THEN her eyes are amber not green
- GIVEN the cool end of the slider, WHEN describing, THEN it reads blue
- GIVEN the greying blues, WHEN describing, THEN they are not called deep blue
- GIVEN the middle of the slider, WHEN describing, THEN it reads grey
- GIVEN the green stretch of the slider, WHEN describing, THEN it reads green
- GIVEN the warm end of the slider, WHEN describing, THEN it reads amber then brown
- GIVEN the darkest samples, WHEN describing, THEN they read dark brown
- GIVEN any offset past the warm turn, WHEN describing, THEN it never says green
- GIVEN no offset from the engine, WHEN describing, THEN nothing is said
- GIVEN an offset past the end, WHEN describing, THEN it still yields a word

### FactionGuidelinesPolicyTests.cs

_that a character is told about their own people and nobody else's._

- GIVEN a sturgian, WHEN narrowed, THEN only the sturgian paragraph survives
- GIVEN any character, WHEN narrowed, THEN the other sections are left alone
- GIVEN a southern imperial lord, WHEN narrowed, THEN the northern paragraph is not kept
- GIVEN a western imperial lord, WHEN narrowed, THEN the shared heading still matches him
- GIVEN an imperial with no realm, WHEN narrowed, THEN both imperial paragraphs are kept and nothing else
- GIVEN a battanian bandit, WHEN narrowed, THEN his culture decides it not his band
- GIVEN a nord, WHEN narrowed, THEN the plural heading still matches him
- GIVEN a culture no heading names, WHEN narrowed, THEN the section is dropped whole
- GIVEN a text without the section, WHEN narrowed, THEN it is returned unchanged
- GIVEN no culture and no faction, WHEN narrowed, THEN everything is kept
- GIVEN a realm renamed at runtime, WHEN narrowed, THEN the heading uses the live name
- GIVEN a realm renamed at runtime, WHEN narrowed, THEN the guidance itself is untouched
- GIVEN an imperial realm renamed at runtime, WHEN narrowed, THEN both headings stay as written
- GIVEN a realm that was never renamed, WHEN narrowed, THEN the heading is left alone
- GIVEN a live name the heading already carries, WHEN narrowed, THEN it is not retitled
- GIVEN a character with no realm, WHEN narrowed, THEN the heading survives as written
- GIVEN a battanian serving a realm named for another people, WHEN narrowed, THEN only his own people are kept
- GIVEN a realm of another culture, WHEN asking whose realm it is, THEN it is not the characters own
- GIVEN a vlandian in a realm not of his people, WHEN narrowed, THEN the heading keeps his peoples name

### FedEventReachTests.cs

_Documents the "Reach:" line cr.feed_test and cr.world_event print after feeding news. cr.feed_test used to feed magnitude 30 untagged and tell the tester it "reaches fewer people"; it reached nobody, and a modder comparing their bridge against it would have concluded CR was broken. The line must state what the awareness model will actually do, per situation._

- GIVEN an untagged magnitude 30 fact, WHEN its reach is described, THEN it says nobody hears it
- GIVEN a realm tagged magnitude 60 fact, WHEN its reach is described, THEN it gives the realms window
- GIVEN a placed magnitude 50 fact, WHEN its reach is described, THEN the place carries it longer than magnitude alone

### FeudRelationPolicyTests.cs

_FeudRelationPolicy decides what a resolved lord-vs-lord scheme does to REAL vanilla relations: how hard the plotter/target bond sours, and how much the target's close allies cool toward the plotter. That relation drop is the WHOLE lever of the Living World pillar's Inc 1: vanilla DefaultDiplomacyModel.GetScoreOfDeclaringWar includes a relationScore term and the kingdom AI proposes/votes war when the score clears its threshold, so a feud that HOLDS lets war EMERGE on its own. The mod never decrees war; it only worsens relations here. These tests pin the delta DECISIONS only. They deliberately assert NOTHING about war being declared: that is vanilla's call, downstream of the number this policy returns. Wrong magnitudes either make feuds inert noise (repeated plots never build toward the war threshold) or let one plot detonate a war this feature has no business forcing; a wrong "which outcome is a feud" boundary punishes a plot nobody ever knew about, or double-counts one that did._

- GIVEN a slander that landed, WHEN resolving, THEN the bond sours hardest
- GIVEN a sabotage that landed, WHEN resolving, THEN the bond sours by the base without the slander bonus
- GIVEN a caught plot, WHEN resolving, THEN the rupture is smaller than a landed one
- GIVEN the same outcome, WHEN comparing kinds, THEN slander bites the bond harder than sabotage
- GIVEN a landed feud, WHEN reading ally cooling, THEN it is a bounded fraction of the principal rupture
- GIVEN the smallest feud, WHEN reading ally cooling, THEN it never falls below the floor
- GIVEN a landed versus a caught feud, WHEN reading fan out, THEN the landed one reaches wider
- GIVEN a plot that fizzled unseen, WHEN resolving, THEN there is no feud and nothing moves
- GIVEN a scheme still in motion, WHEN resolving, THEN no feud is created yet

### FiefAudiencePolicyTests.cs

_Player wish (Fakade, roadmap): everyone with no field party (ladies, notables, a garrisoned lord standing around in a fief) never got a turn under road-only InterceptionBehavior. This file protects the two rules that make the fief audience safe to ship: WHICH of InterceptionPolicy's triggers may fire off the road (get it wrong and a hostile-territory "you crossed my land" line comes out of a lord who never moved), and the per-NPC cooldown that keeps a busy town from opening a queue of "may I have a word" popups._

- GIVEN a child standing in the town, WHEN asked whether they may seek an audience, THEN they may not
- GIVEN a prisoner a party leader or someone elsewhere, WHEN asked, THEN none seeks an audience here
- GIVEN facts that would choose TerritorialPatrol, WHEN narrowed to fief, THEN it is excluded
- GIVEN facts that would choose HeardOfDeed, WHEN narrowed to fief, THEN it survives
- GIVEN both triggers would qualify, WHEN narrowed to fief, THEN the road only one never wins
- GIVEN nothing applies, WHEN narrowed to fief, THEN the answer is none
- GIVEN facts that would choose Grudge, WHEN narrowed to fief, THEN it survives
- GIVEN facts that would choose AppreciationOwed, WHEN narrowed to fief, THEN it survives
- GIVEN facts that would choose NemesisBounty, WHEN narrowed to fief, THEN it survives
- GIVEN a candidate never offered, WHEN checking cooldown, THEN it is not on cooldown
- GIVEN offered yesterday, WHEN checking cooldown under the default pacing, THEN it is on cooldown
- GIVEN the cooldown window has fully elapsed, WHEN checking cooldown, THEN it is no longer on cooldown
- GIVEN the Never pacing, WHEN checking cooldown, THEN it mirrors InterceptionPacingRules exactly
- GIVEN a candidate with no fief valid trigger, WHEN selecting, THEN it is never chosen
- GIVEN no candidate qualifies, WHEN selecting, THEN the answer is null
- GIVEN several qualifying candidates, WHEN selecting, THEN exactly one winner comes back deterministically
- GIVEN a null candidate list, WHEN selecting, THEN it is a safe null
- GIVEN a winning candidate with a detail, WHEN selecting, THEN the detail survives

### FirstImpressionDescriberTests.cs

_Pins how a resolved FirstImpressionDisposition is rendered into the prompt's "first impression" guidance line, consumed by FirstImpressionResolver.Resolve and injected as EncounterContext.OpeningDisposition._

- GIVEN a neutral or unknown disposition, WHEN describing, THEN nothing is injected
- GIVEN a flavoured disposition, WHEN describing, THEN a non empty line is produced
- GIVEN the loyal opening, WHEN describing, THEN it speaks of the players own clan and never a tone caveat
- GIVEN a prejudiced opening, WHEN describing, THEN it carries the tone only caveat

### FirstImpressionPolicyTests.cs

_Player report (ProudSeal 2026-07-10): NPCs opened too neutral and too reasonable, so a rivalry required the player to offend someone first. This policy decides the OPENING TONE a barely-known lord takes toward the player, from the lord's traits and the player's standing: Cordial, Neutral, Cool, Arrogant, Suspicious, or Demeaning. The answer only colours prompt tone (EncounterContext.OpeningDisposition); it must NEVER touch relation numbers, or deeds would stop being what governs regard. Two hard exemptions matter as much as the roll itself: a lord in the player's OWN clan never opens prejudiced (Loyal), and an already-known lord keeps their earned stance instead of a cold first impression. Get either exemption wrong and a loyal companion or an established friend would suddenly read as hostile for no in-fiction reason._

- GIVEN a null input, WHEN resolving, THEN it is neutral
- GIVEN an own clan member with hostile traits, WHEN resolving, THEN they open loyal
- GIVEN an already known noble with hostile traits, WHEN resolving, THEN no first impression colour
- GIVEN all neutral inputs, WHEN resolving, THEN the opening is neutral
- GIVEN a warm noble meeting a stranger, WHEN resolving, THEN they open cordial
- GIVEN a cruel low honor noble vs a landless upstart, WHEN the roll fires, THEN they are demeaning
- GIVEN the same cruel noble but a high roll, WHEN resolving, THEN they do not show it
- GIVEN an ambitious faction rival, WHEN the roll fires, THEN they are arrogant
- GIVEN a low honor noble only, WHEN the roll fires, THEN they are suspicious
- GIVEN high player standing, WHEN resolving, THEN it disarms a cruel noble
- GIVEN the same seed twice, WHEN taking a stable roll, THEN the value is identical
- GIVEN any seed, WHEN taking a stable roll, THEN it stays in the zero to ninety nine range
- GIVEN a known seed, WHEN taking a stable roll, THEN it yields the precomputed value

### ForcedMarriagePolicyTests.cs

_Ratified 2026-07-23 (player report): a family that has given its blessing can bind its daughter for political ends, so a marriage below the regard bar now PROCEEDS instead of being silently refused, and what the unwon heart buys is a ForcedMarriage grudge scaled to the shortfall. This policy is the ONE place that scaling lives; BannerlordGameStateBridge.ExecuteMarry reads it right after sealing the union, and the grudge it plants is what flips the spouse's consent rules to the unwilling branch (the prompt's "no thresholds apply between spouses" exemption must NOT apply to a bride who never consented). Get the numbers wrong and either a near-miss bride resents like a kidnapped one, or the mechanic becomes a consent bypass with no cost at all._

- GIVEN regard at the marriage bar, WHEN judging the union, THEN it is not forced
- GIVEN a love match, WHEN computing the resentment, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN a colder bride, WHEN computing the resentment, THEN it grows with the shortfall
- GIVEN a bride one point short, WHEN computing the resentment, THEN it is only a faint grievance
- GIVEN a bride who hates the player, WHEN computing the resentment, THEN it stops at the winnable cap
- GIVEN a lowered marriage bar, WHEN regard clears it, THEN the union is not forced
- GIVEN a lowered bar, WHEN a bride is wed one point short, THEN the resentment tracks the lowered bar

### ForeignDivorceDetectionTests.cs

_tashmetu (Nexus, 02/10/2026, Bridges to Remember on 2.6.10): "CR records marriages as world news, but not divorces made by other mods (Hero.Spouse set to null). Could CR notice them?" The game raises no event when a mod clears a spouse, so CR compares the marriages it saw yesterday with today's. If this rule is wrong, a divorce another mod made goes unremembered (the former spouses speak as though still wed), a death is mistaken for a divorce, or a divorce CR itself made is recorded twice._

- GIVEN a couple yesterday and both unwed today, WHEN compared, THEN their divorce is found
- GIVEN one spouse gone from the world, WHEN compared, THEN no divorce is found
- GIVEN a divorce CR made itself, WHEN compared, THEN it is left to CR
- GIVEN one couple unchanged and one parted, WHEN compared, THEN only the parted one is found once
- GIVEN a divorce found, WHEN a former spouse remembers, THEN she knows the marriage ended
- GIVEN a spouse now wed to another, WHEN compared, THEN the first marriage is found ended

### GameRelationReadingTests.cs

_What the game's relation with the player means for one character: their clan's standing, and, when a mod gives them one, a relation of their own. tashmetu (Nexus, 04/10/2026), author of Personal Relations (each noble gets a relation of their own with the player instead of sharing their clan leader's): the prompt's "Your clan's standing" was read from Hero.GetRelationWithPlayer(), which in the base game IS the leader's relation, but with that mod is the character's own. Players saw a lord at +100 "clan standing" and 0 personal regard, and the model spoke as if the house loved them while the lord did not know them. The clan's standing is the leader's; the character's own is said apart, and only when it truly differs, so a game without such a mod sees exactly what it saw before._

- GIVEN a clan member whose own relation differs from her leaders, WHEN read, THEN the clan standing is the leaders and hers is said apart
- GIVEN the base game where a clan shares its leaders relation, WHEN read, THEN nothing more is said
- GIVEN no other leader to read, WHEN read, THEN the characters own relation is the standing

### GarrisonReinforcementTests.cs

_fkasad (Nexus, 01/10/2026), on a governor's supply task: "Works for garrison reinforcement too." A governor or lord whose garrison is thin may ask the player to bring men; handed over in conversation, they go straight onto the walls. The strength bands are the ones the seat-standing pack already describes a garrison by, moved into one home so what a lord says of his walls and what he asks for them cannot disagree. If these rules are wrong, a lord with a full garrison begs for men, a skeleton garrison never asks, a lord asks for more men than his walls can hold, or a short delivery completes the task._

- GIVEN healthy men against the limit, WHEN banded, THEN the seat packs own bands hold
- GIVEN the garrison band, WHEN a lord considers asking, THEN only a thin garrison asks
- GIVEN a proposed number, WHEN the ask is set, THEN it stays within the band and the room left
- GIVEN the players healthy men, WHEN handing over, THEN only the full number counts

### GeographyDescriberTests.cs

_Grounds what an NPC says about where a place lies in the engine's own map data, not a hand-authored lorebook: built after a player report that NPCs invented false geography (a Vlandian border castle described as leading toward Empire territory when its pass actually leads to Battania). A lorebook would only reproduce that same drift as settlements are renamed or change hands mid-campaign, so this reads live positions and distances instead. Feeds EncounterContext.GeographyNote (via SettlementContextResolver), rendered verbatim under "THE LAY OF THE LAND, treat as fact" in the prompt, so the phrases pinned here are exactly what the LLM is told and repeats to the player._

- GIVEN a cardinal delta, WHEN asking the direction, THEN it names that cardinal
- GIVEN a diagonal delta, WHEN asking the direction, THEN it names that ordinal
- GIVEN a near coincident point, WHEN asking the direction, THEN it is empty
- GIVEN a map distance, WHEN banding it, THEN it reads as the expected phrase
- GIVEN a distance exactly on a boundary, WHEN banding it, THEN it falls into the next band
- GIVEN the same spot, WHEN asking the bearing, THEN it is right here
- GIVEN a mid range north east target, WHEN asking the bearing, THEN it combines band and direction

### GiftEquipPolicyTests.cs

_This is the pure core of the "gift equipment in chat" feature (2026-07-25): when the player gifts an equipment item to an NPC, the mod's give_item bridge step reads GiftEquipPolicy.Decide's verdict and, when ShouldEquip is true, actually equips the item on the NPC (the engine half, done separately), then narrates the reaction either way. If this policy is wrong in one direction, an NPC's gear silently DOWNGRADES from a random trinket the player handed over out of roleplay, which reads as a bug the moment the player notices their companion is suddenly worse-armed than before. If it is wrong in the other direction, a wife never wears the dress the player gifted her, or a companion who would clearly like a sword keeps fighting barehanded, which is the whole feature failing to deliver on its promise. Every test below is anchored to that real request: gift equipment in chat, let it actually be worn when it makes sense (robe to the wife, a sword a companion likes), a rival keeps his own gear, and nobody is silently downgraded._

- GIVEN a non equippable gift, WHEN deciding, THEN it is never equipped even into an empty slot for a beloved spouse
- GIVEN an empty target slot, WHEN deciding, THEN the gift is always equipped regardless of relation
- GIVEN a strict upgrade, WHEN deciding, THEN it is equipped even for a low relation npc
- GIVEN a non upgrade gift to a sentimental npc, WHEN deciding, THEN it is worn out of affection even at low relation
- GIVEN a non upgrade gift at or above the affection threshold, WHEN deciding, THEN it is worn without being sentimental
- GIVEN a non upgrade gift to a cold non sentimental npc, WHEN deciding, THEN their own gear is kept
- GIVEN relation exactly one below the affection threshold, WHEN deciding, THEN it does not equip
- GIVEN a gift worth exactly the same as the current item, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not treated as an upgrade
- GIVEN a captor dressing a held prisoner, WHEN deciding, THEN the garment is imposed even on a hostile prisoner over finer armour
- GIVEN a non equippable gift to a held prisoner, WHEN deciding, THEN it is still never equipped
- GIVEN a civilian garment, WHEN choosing the set, THEN a held captive uses the battle set but a free npc uses civilian

### GiftQuantityPolicyTests.cs

_GiftQuantityPolicy backs give_item's transfer count. A player reported giving "10 ingots" and losing only one, because the transfer was hard-coded to a single unit. Get this rule wrong and either the count is ignored again (the report returns) or the player gives MORE than they hold (a dupe/negative-stock bug). These tests pin the clamp: the asked-for count, never above the stack, never below one, and zero only when the player holds none._

- GIVEN a count within the stack, WHEN resolving, THEN that many move
- GIVEN a count above the stack, WHEN resolving, THEN it gives the whole stack
- GIVEN no or invalid count, WHEN resolving, THEN it defaults to one
- GIVEN an empty stack, WHEN resolving, THEN it is zero

### GoldTransferLimitTests.cs

_Whether a gold transfer line says that the ceiling held part of the sum back. tashmetu (Nexus, 16/10/2026): a player asked to give 20,000, gave 10,000, and thought the rest had gone missing. Wrong one way, the player believes coin vanished; wrong the other way, a line blames the ceiling when it was the purse, or a whole payment, that set the sum._

- GIVEN a sum above the ceiling, WHEN the ceiling is given, THEN the line says it is the most at once
- GIVEN a sum within the ceiling, WHEN it is all given, THEN the line adds nothing
- GIVEN a sum above the ceiling and a purse below it, WHEN the purse is emptied, THEN the line adds nothing

### GreetingDeferencePolicyTests.cs

_Whether a character may open with a greeting that offers the player service. tashmetu (Nexus, 16/10/2026): a princess greeted a landless player with no troops with "I am at your disposal", then sized him up as a landless stranger._

- GIVEN a great house speaker and a landless player, WHEN the greeting is chosen, THEN no line offers service
- GIVEN a ruler speaking to a player who does not rule, WHEN asked, THEN the ruler does not defer
- GIVEN a speaker of the players rank or below, WHEN asked, THEN they may defer
- GIVEN a personal bond or a dear friend, WHEN asked, THEN rank does not take the warm lines
- GIVEN a player who rules a realm, WHEN a ruler greets them, THEN the ruler may defer
- GIVEN the lines named in the report, WHEN listed, THEN each is deferential

### GuardEvaluatorTests.cs

_Pins the guard contract: AND-combined, empty passes, short-circuits on the first failure. The resolver is injected (the engine knows what each keyword means against real game state), so these tests stub it._

- GIVEN no guards, WHEN evaluating, THEN it passes
- GIVEN all guards resolve true, WHEN evaluating, THEN it passes
- GIVEN one guard resolves false, WHEN evaluating, THEN it fails
- GIVEN a failing guard first, WHEN evaluating, THEN it short circuits and skips the rest

### HardshipPolicyTests.cs

_Guards HardshipPolicy, the gate deciding whether a town's distress (a food shortage and/or collapsing prosperity) is worth recording as a Hardship WorldEvent (which then spreads as rumour, "what you've heard", and the chronicle). Its answer feeds the weekly world-conditions scan directly: a wrong "yes" fills the realm with phantom famines, a wrong "no" leaves a genuinely starving town's plight unspoken. Each boundary below pins one of those failures. Rationale derived from the policy's own header/docs._

- GIVEN no food shortage and healthy prosperity, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not recorded
- GIVEN a food shortage despite high prosperity, WHEN deciding, THEN it is recorded
- GIVEN collapsed prosperity despite adequate food, WHEN deciding, THEN it is recorded
- GIVEN prosperity exactly at the floor and no shortage, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not recorded
- GIVEN the same town was recorded too recently, WHEN deciding, THEN it is not recorded
- GIVEN the cooldown has exactly elapsed and still distressed, WHEN deciding, THEN it is recorded
- GIVEN the town was never recorded before, WHEN distressed, THEN it is recorded

### HarmPrisonerPolicyTests.cs

_v1.34.2 resilience hardening (prisoner/gold audit, finding 1). This policy is the single gate between an LLM's harm_prisoner [ACTION] and a REAL loss of the player's hit points. Before it, the executor deducted health with only a per-scene cap and a floor, never re-checking that the player was genuinely a captor's prisoner inside a live captive scene, and it fired regardless of the Hardcore content floor the feature is advertised behind._

- GIVEN the player is not the captors prisoner, WHEN the other guards hold, THEN harm is refused
- GIVEN no captive scene is active, WHEN the other guards hold, THEN harm is refused
- GIVEN content level is below Hardcore, WHEN the other guards hold, THEN harm is refused
- GIVEN every guard holds, THEN harm is permitted
- GIVEN every combination of the three guards, THEN only all true permits harm

### HeroLabelTests.cs

_Protects the one rule that tells a hero apart from a namesake: a Battanian lord "Luichan" who took a castle, per a world rumor, was conflated by an NPC with the player's own six-year-old son of the same bare first name, who was then spoken of as the one who took the castle. HeroLabel is the fix reused at every naming site (rumor, kin list, mentioned-hero hint); a wrong sentinel here would print something as absurd as "Luichan of No clan"._

- GIVEN a name alone, WHEN describing, THEN the label is the bare name
- GIVEN a name and clan, WHEN describing, THEN the clan is appended
- GIVEN a name clan and culture, WHEN describing, THEN both are appended in order
- GIVEN a name and culture but no clan, WHEN describing, THEN only the culture is appended
- GIVEN the no clan sentinel, WHEN describing, THEN it is omitted
- GIVEN the unaligned sentinel, WHEN describing, THEN it is omitted
- GIVEN a null clan, WHEN describing, THEN it is omitted without throwing
- GIVEN a blank clan, WHEN describing, THEN it is omitted
- GIVEN a null name, WHEN describing, THEN an empty string is returned without throwing
- GIVEN a blank name, WHEN describing, THEN it is returned unchanged

### HireAgreementPolicyTests.cs

_what a hired companion actually costs once a price has been agreed in conversation._

- GIVEN a price was agreed, WHEN charging, THEN exactly that price is charged
- GIVEN the misread figure that started this, WHEN charging, THEN it is no longer quietly rewritten
- GIVEN no price was ever named, WHEN charging, THEN the asking price stands in
- GIVEN neither a price nor an asking figure, WHEN charging, THEN it never falls to nothing
- GIVEN the purse cannot cover the bargain, WHEN asked, THEN the deal cannot close

### HistoryRelationLedgerTests.cs

_Starting vanilla relations from an authored history (Gabriel, 17/10/2026: "it can also be able to set a starting vanilla"; docs/design/AUTHORED_HISTORY.md section 6). The file is read at every load and edited between loads, so HistoryRelationLedger plans only the difference between what the file asks and what it already did. Wrong, and each reload adds the betrayal's -40 again until every pair in the history sits at -100, or an event the author deleted leaves its hatred behind for ever._

- GIVEN a new change, WHEN synced, THEN it is applied and recorded
- GIVEN a change already applied, WHEN synced again, THEN nothing moves
- GIVEN an edited change, WHEN synced, THEN only the difference is applied
- GIVEN an event the author removed, WHEN synced, THEN its relation is undone
- GIVEN a set on a pair the game made friends, WHEN synced, THEN the pair starts at the value
- GIVEN a set already applied and the game moved the pair since, WHEN synced again, THEN nothing moves
- GIVEN an edited set, WHEN synced, THEN the pair moves by the edit
