- GIVEN prior recaptures, WHEN computing relation, THEN each one adds its own share
- GIVEN a great many recaptures, WHEN computing relation, THEN it is capped at the ceiling
- GIVEN a negative recapture count, WHEN computing relation, THEN it is treated as zero

### NemesisDossierBuilderTests.cs

_NemesisDossierBuilder turns a nemesis's raw facts (name, culture, region, times encountered, recaptures, extra history sentences) into the two strings the encyclopedia hero page actually renders: the "Nemesis:" header and the gold bullet list. The whole point of this piece is the player report (SuperGraham339): captured/tortured by a nemesis, escaped, killed the party, ran into him again, then could not find any record of him on his encyclopedia page. So the two things these tests must pin are (1) an ENCOUNTERED nemesis (timesEncountered > 0) shows a dossier and a LATENT one (timesEncountered == 0, never met) shows nothing at all (leaking a latent nemesis's existence would spoil the "hidden until materialised" design the whole pillar was ratified around), and (2) the wording reads naturally for one encounter/recapture vs several, since a raw "1 times" would look like a bug on the page._

- GIVEN a nemesis encountered once, WHEN building the label, THEN the header is shown
- GIVEN a latent nemesis never encountered, WHEN building the label, THEN it is empty so the widget autohides
- GIVEN a latent nemesis, WHEN building the items, THEN it is empty even with other data supplied
- GIVEN a missing display name, WHEN building the items, THEN it is empty
- GIVEN exactly one encounter, WHEN building the items, THEN the wording reads once not 1 times
- GIVEN several encounters, WHEN building the items, THEN the wording shows the count
- GIVEN zero recaptures, WHEN building the items, THEN no recapture line appears
- GIVEN exactly one recapture, WHEN building the items, THEN the wording reads once
- GIVEN several recaptures, WHEN building the items, THEN the wording shows the count
- GIVEN culture and region are both known, WHEN building the items, THEN both appear in the identity line
- GIVEN the region is unresolved, WHEN building the items, THEN the identity line still reads naturally
- GIVEN neither culture nor region is known, WHEN building the items, THEN a generic identity line is used
- GIVEN history sentences are supplied, WHEN building the items, THEN each appears as its own bullet line
- GIVEN a blank history sentence, WHEN building the items, THEN it is skipped
- GIVEN no history sentences supplied, WHEN building the items, THEN it still builds the identity and encounter lines

### NemesisEscapeChancePolicyTests.cs

_Nemesis pillar: the escaping-a-nemesis-captor arc. Gabriel's explicit ruling ("je laisserais une chance quand meme", ROADMAP) was that a nemesis is NOT a perfect prison: the player always keeps some chance to slip away, but that chance decays with every recapture as he learns to guard the player more carefully._

- GIVEN a first captivity with no prior recapture, WHEN resolving the chance, THEN it is the base chance
- GIVEN one prior recapture, WHEN resolving the chance, THEN it has dropped by one step
- GIVEN growing recapture counts, WHEN resolving the chance, THEN it never increases
- GIVEN many recaptures, WHEN resolving the chance, THEN it settles on the floor but never zero
- GIVEN a negative recapture count, WHEN resolving the chance, THEN it is treated as a first captivity
- GIVEN a first captivity, WHEN resolving the vanilla drip suppress, THEN it is the base and leaves a real chance
- GIVEN one prior recapture, WHEN resolving the vanilla drip suppress, THEN it has tightened by one step
- GIVEN growing recapture counts, WHEN resolving the vanilla drip suppress, THEN it never decreases and never fully cages
- GIVEN a negative recapture count, WHEN resolving the vanilla drip suppress, THEN it is treated as a first captivity

### NemesisIdentityTests.cs

_Nemesis pillar: NemesisIdentity is a plain carrier with no behaviour — this just protects that every property round-trips, so a typo in a property name fails loudly instead of silently at engine-wiring time._

- GIVEN a freshly minted nemesis, WHEN setting every property, THEN all values round trip
- GIVEN a slain nemesis, WHEN setting DeathDay, THEN it carries a value and IsAlive can be false

### NemesisMaterializePolicyTests.cs

_Nemesis pillar, ACTIVE HUNT (map-presence mechanic): the nemesis keeps NO standing map party (nothing for the AI to attrit); he only materializes when the player engages a bandit party of his culture close enough to his anchored region. This is the pure proximity gate that decides "close enough"._

- GIVEN a party inside the region radius, WHEN checking, THEN it can be his
- GIVEN a party beyond the region radius, WHEN checking, THEN it is not his
- GIVEN a non positive radius, WHEN checking, THEN it never matches
- GIVEN a party exactly on the anchor, WHEN checking, THEN it matches

### NemesisNameGeneratorTests.cs

_Nemesis pillar: deterministic per-culture name/epithet generation. Same seed must always mint the same boss (so re-deriving from persisted state never drifts), while different seeds and different band cultures should generally diverge — this is first-pass flavour Gabriel can expand later._

- GIVEN the same culture and seed, WHEN generating twice, THEN the result is identical
- GIVEN the plain result is already taken, WHEN generating distinct, THEN a different pair is returned
- GIVEN several taken pairs, WHEN generating distinct, THEN the result avoids all of them
- GIVEN no taken predicate, WHEN generating distinct, THEN it matches the plain result
- GIVEN an impossibly saturated pool, WHEN generating distinct, THEN it returns without throwing
- GIVEN an unknown band culture, WHEN generating, THEN it falls back to the generic pool without throwing
- GIVEN two different seeds, WHEN generating for the same culture, THEN at least one of them differs across a spread of seeds
- GIVEN two different seeds, WHEN generating, THEN the epithet can also vary independently of the name
- GIVEN each known band culture, WHEN generating, THEN a non empty name and epithet are produced
- GIVEN a culture, WHEN generating across many seeds, THEN every name comes from that cultures own pool not another cultures
- GIVEN a negative seed, WHEN generating, THEN it still resolves to a valid name without throwing
- GIVEN a result, WHEN reading the full display name, THEN it joins name and epithet with a space

### NemesisResolutionPolicyTests.cs

_Nemesis pillar increment 2 (resolution layer): the pure wording/classification for how a tracked nemesis's vendetta closes, whether cut down in battle, deliberately executed, or spared and turned._

- GIVEN the kill was a deliberate execution, WHEN resolving the kind, THEN it is Executed
- GIVEN the kill was not an execution, WHEN resolving the kind, THEN it is KilledInBattle
- GIVEN an executed nemesis, WHEN building the closure summary, THEN it names the player and the nemesis
- GIVEN an executed nemesis and a battle killed nemesis, WHEN building the closure summary, THEN the two flavours read differently
- GIVEN an executed nemesis and a battle killed nemesis, WHEN building the chronicle line, THEN the two flavours read differently
- GIVEN a nemesis spared and turned, WHEN building the turned summary, THEN it names both and reads as a turning not a death
- GIVEN a nemesis spared and turned, WHEN building the turned chronicle line, THEN it names the nemesis

### NemesisRevealPolicyTests.cs

_Nemesis pillar: the pity-timer pacing that decides whether a qualifying bandit-party encounter reveals the regional nemesis as its leader. Rising chance, guaranteed by a fixed count of qualifying encounters so the hunt never goes on forever, but he is never trivially common either._

- GIVEN zero encounters since reveal, WHEN computing the chance, THEN it is exactly the base chance
- GIVEN a negative encounter count, WHEN computing the chance, THEN it is clamped to the base chance
- GIVEN the chance curve, WHEN stepping through every encounter count, THEN it never decreases
- GIVEN encounters at the guarantee count, WHEN computing the chance, THEN it is a certainty
- GIVEN encounters past the guarantee count, WHEN computing the chance, THEN it stays a certainty
- GIVEN an encounter count partway to the guarantee, WHEN computing the chance, THEN it sits strictly between base and certainty
- GIVEN a roll below the chance, WHEN asking ShouldReveal, THEN it reveals
- GIVEN a roll exactly equal to the chance, WHEN asking ShouldReveal, THEN it does not reveal
- GIVEN a roll above the chance, WHEN asking ShouldReveal, THEN it does not reveal
- GIVEN the guarantee is reached, WHEN asking ShouldReveal with any roll short of one, THEN it always reveals

### NemesisRosterPolicyTests.cs

_Nemesis pillar: the regional recurring roster. On any qualifying capture/encounter, reuse the living nemesis of that band culture in that region if one exists (you recross the SAME boss); otherwise the caller mints a fresh one. This is the pure lookup only — minting is out of scope here._

- GIVEN a living nemesis of the same band and region, WHEN finding a recurring nemesis, THEN he is returned
- GIVEN an empty roster, WHEN finding a recurring nemesis, THEN null signals mint a new one
- GIVEN a null roster, WHEN finding a recurring nemesis, THEN null is returned without throwing
- GIVEN the only matching identity is dead, WHEN finding a recurring nemesis, THEN he is ignored and null is returned
- GIVEN the only matching identity has been turned to the players service, WHEN finding a recurring nemesis, THEN he is ignored
- GIVEN a living nemesis in a different region, WHEN finding a recurring nemesis, THEN he is ignored
- GIVEN a living nemesis of a different band culture, WHEN finding a recurring nemesis, THEN he is ignored
- GIVEN two qualifying identities, WHEN one has been encountered more, THEN the more encountered one wins
- GIVEN two qualifying identities tied on encounters, WHEN finding a recurring nemesis, THEN the lowest id wins deterministically
- GIVEN a recently killed nemesis still in cooldown, WHEN checking the culture, THEN a fresh mint is blocked
- GIVEN the cooldown has elapsed, WHEN checking the culture, THEN a fresh mint is allowed
- GIVEN the cooldown is for a DIFFERENT culture, WHEN checking, THEN this culture is not blocked
- GIVEN a living nemesis with no death cooldown, WHEN checking, THEN nothing is blocked

### NemesisRumorLineTests.cs

_NemesisRumorLine composes the tavern-keeper's nemesis rumour reply: the last open bit of the nemesis-discoverability pillar (ROADMAP: "a standalone tavern-keeper rumour variant remains an optional minor follow-up" to the dossier + NemesisBounty interception trigger shipped in v2.1.2). The design constraint that matters most here: this is DISCOVERABILITY ONLY. It names the nemesis and his region and points the player at the existing bounty channel, but must never itself state a reward or imply the bounty is already settled, since NemesisBountyDialogFlow alone owns issuing/settling it (no duplication of that machinery). These tests pin the wording so a future edit cannot accidentally leak bounty specifics into this line._

- GIVEN a nemesis label and region, WHEN composing, THEN both appear in the line
- GIVEN a composed line, WHEN inspecting it, THEN it never states a reward figure
- GIVEN no region is known, WHEN composing, THEN it falls back to a generic region phrase
- GIVEN no nemesis label, WHEN composing, THEN the line is empty

### NemesisTurnPolicyTests.cs

_Nemesis pillar increment 4 (SPARE + TURN): the pure gate deciding whether a beaten, held nemesis accepts being spared and taken into the player's service instead of being killed. Four conditions must ALL hold (fears you enough, respects you enough, his nature allows bending, his grudge is not an unbreakable vendetta), each with an escape valve described in the production file's header._

- GIVEN a cowed respectful pragmatic nemesis, WHEN resolving, THEN he turns
- GIVEN a nemesis who does not fear the player, WHEN resolving, THEN he refuses for too little fear
- GIVEN a nemesis who does not respect the player, WHEN resolving, THEN he refuses for too little respect
- GIVEN a brave nemesis who is no calculator, WHEN resolving, THEN he is too defiant to serve
- GIVEN an honorbound nemesis who is no calculator, WHEN resolving, THEN he is too defiant to serve
- GIVEN a brave but coldly calculating nemesis, WHEN resolving, THEN pragmatism overrides defiance and he turns
- GIVEN a deep grudge with only ordinary dominance, WHEN resolving, THEN his hatred blocks the turn
- GIVEN a deep grudge but overwhelming dominance, WHEN resolving, THEN dominance overcomes his hatred and he turns
- GIVEN facts exactly at both stance floors, WHEN resolving, THEN the floors are inclusive

### NewsDestinationSelectorTests.cs

_Chooses which town a companion's "go gather news about X" errand rides to (CompanionMissionBehavior.ResolveDestination): a named settlement beats a faction (kingdom) beats a culture, and -1 tells the host to fall back to the nearest town. Get the precedence or the tie-break wrong and the companion rides to a town that has nothing to do with what the player actually asked for, or two equally-ranked candidates resolve unpredictably instead of to the intended nearest one._

- GIVEN a culture topic, WHEN no faction matches, THEN it falls to a culture match
- GIVEN a faction topic, WHEN choosing, THEN it picks a town of that faction
- GIVEN a named settlement, WHEN it also partly matches a faction, THEN the settlement wins
- GIVEN a topic that matches nothing, WHEN choosing, THEN it returns no match
- GIVEN no topic, WHEN choosing, THEN it defers to the host nearest fallback
- GIVEN two towns of the same faction, WHEN choosing, THEN the first host ordered one wins

### NewsReachTests.cs

_that the two things this mod writes into the world are actually HEARD by the right people, and not heard by the wrong ones._

- GIVEN a blight a lord spoke of, WHEN his own neighbours are asked, THEN word has reached them
- GIVEN the same blight, WHEN a stranger across the map is asked, THEN it never reached him
- GIVEN a thing merely wondered aloud, WHEN it travels, THEN it needs more help to arrive
- GIVEN a season that changed a realm, WHEN its own people are asked, THEN they know how it fared
- GIVEN the same season, WHEN a distant foreigner is asked, THEN he has not been following it
- GIVEN a season in the next realm, WHEN a border neighbour is asked, THEN word carries that far
- GIVEN an invention and a real event, WHEN compared, THEN the invention never travels further
- GIVEN news a week old, WHEN a countryman is asked, THEN he has still heard of it
- GIVEN news a month old, WHEN anyone is asked, THEN it has passed out of talk

### NotableRecruitmentPolicyTests.cs

_recruit_notable: the pure gate deciding whether a settlement NOTABLE (gang leader, headman, merchant, artisan, rural notable, preacher) can be persuaded in conversation to leave their post and swear into the player's clan as a COMPANION. It closes the same class of broken promise recruit_prisoner closed: the LLM narrating a recruitment the game never carried out._

- GIVEN a free notable with a successor at the regard floor, WHEN evaluating, THEN they can be recruited
- GIVEN regard exactly at the floor, WHEN evaluating, THEN it passes
- GIVEN regard just below the floor, WHEN evaluating, THEN regard is too low
- GIVEN a hero who is not a notable, WHEN evaluating, THEN they are not a notable even at full regard
- GIVEN a dead notable, WHEN evaluating, THEN they are not alive
- GIVEN a notable who is a prisoner, WHEN evaluating, THEN it refuses as a prisoner
- GIVEN a notable already in the player clan, WHEN evaluating, THEN it refuses as already player clan
- GIVEN no eligible successor, WHEN evaluating, THEN it refuses to leave the post vacant
- GIVEN a non notable who also lacks a successor with low regard, WHEN evaluating, THEN identity is reported first
- GIVEN a beloved notable with no successor, WHEN evaluating, THEN the missing successor is reported over regard
- GIVEN the starting relation bonus, WHEN read, THEN it is a modest positive below the deep bond mark

### NpcActionGateTests.cs

_The one door every character's initiative toward the player goes through (Gabriel, 30/09/2026, after Nexusrrr's baby son asked to discuss warfare). An inventory found about a dozen paths, each with its own filter, two of them checking age, several trusting Hero.IsLord (an occupation, not an age). If this gate lets a child through, a baby writes threats or sends killers; if it lets a prisoner through, a captive mails the player from his cell; if it lets the dead or the player through, the world acts in the name of someone who cannot act._

- GIVEN a free living adult, WHEN they would take the initiative, THEN they may
- GIVEN a child, WHEN they would take the initiative, THEN it does not happen
- GIVEN the story modes tutorial restricts interaction, WHEN anyone would take the initiative, THEN it does not happen
- GIVEN a prisoner, WHEN they would take the initiative, THEN it does not happen
- GIVEN the dead the player or no one, WHEN checked, THEN none takes the initiative
- GIVEN a hero taken out of the world, WHEN they would act, THEN it does not happen
- GIVEN a dead lord of the house, WHEN the player convenes a council, THEN he does not take a seat
- GIVEN a teen of the house below the games majority, WHEN a council is convened, THEN they may sit
- GIVEN a young child, WHEN a council is convened, THEN they do not sit
- GIVEN a teen below the games majority, WHEN they would write first, THEN it does not happen
- GIVEN the player writes to someone, WHEN they would receive it, THEN a child may a captive or a hero out of the world may not
- GIVEN a ruler who is a child or a captive, WHEN a warrant would be issued in their name, THEN none is
- GIVEN a child riding with the player, WHEN a conversation gathers its witnesses, THEN the child may be present
- GIVEN a scene held for landfall, WHEN its partner was taken out of the world meanwhile, THEN it is not received
- GIVEN a marriage prospect, WHEN offered, THEN captivity and age are left to marriage s own rules
- GIVEN a young ward, WHEN placed, THEN age is left to the ward s own band
- GIVEN a child or captive relative, WHEN pledged as a hostage, THEN they are not
- GIVEN a duel, WHEN the opponent is a child or a captive, THEN the child is refused and captivity left to the duel
- GIVEN a child in the party, WHEN the player would send them on a mission, THEN they are not sent
- GIVEN a household member, WHEN offered into a lords service, THEN no child captive or absent hero is
- GIVEN a lord asked to ride with the player, WHEN they are a captive or a child, THEN they do not
- GIVEN a captor resolved from days ago, WHEN the scene opens, THEN one since taken or gone does not appear
- GIVEN a conversation with a prisoner held for landfall, WHEN received, THEN the captive is not refused
- GIVEN a captive in the room, WHEN the witnesses are gathered, THEN the captive is not refused
- GIVEN several reasons at once, WHEN checked, THEN the most absolute is named

### NpcMemoryInjectionTests.cs

_Pins the door rules for a memory injected through the public CrNpcMemory API (Extension Surface). The facade resolves the hero and writes to the store; THIS is the pure normalisation a third-party mod's input passes through first, so it is where the contract's guarantees live._

- GIVEN a valid injection, WHEN built, THEN it carries the bounded summary kind and dated day
- GIVEN a blank summary, WHEN built, THEN it is rejected
- GIVEN an over long summary, WHEN built, THEN it is capped to the blob safe bound
- GIVEN a known or unknown kind, WHEN normalised, THEN it maps or falls back to Other
- GIVEN an engine owned kind, WHEN normalised, THEN it is coerced to Other
- GIVEN various days ago, WHEN resolving the day, THEN it stays in bounds
- GIVEN a memory injected as private, WHEN built, THEN it is kept to private talk
- GIVEN a memory injected without the flag, WHEN built, THEN it is public as before
- GIVEN a mod filing news a noble heard, WHEN injected as hearsay, THEN it is kept as hearsay

### OathKindParserTests.cs

_OathKindParser is the ONE bridge between what the LLM writes in a swear_oath [RESOLUTION]'s oath_kind field and the real CommitmentKind CouncilLift.SettleSwearOath tracks. The stake: an oath kind must be resolved from a STRUCTURED whitelist, never a guess, and "move_against" must NEVER resolve here, since that is pledge_against's own separate resolution with its own scheme-launching executor, a different mechanism the model must not be able to invoke under swear_oath's name._

- GIVEN a whitelisted oath kind, WHEN parsed, THEN it resolves to the matching kind
- GIVEN mixed case, WHEN parsed, THEN it still resolves
- GIVEN surrounding whitespace, WHEN parsed, THEN it still resolves
- GIVEN move against, WHEN parsed, THEN it is refused because that is pledge against own kind
- GIVEN blank or unrecognised text, WHEN parsed, THEN it fails

### OfferFlowGateTests.cs

_Covers the pure routing decision behind the offer ("give") button (OfferFlowGate), which the mod's OfferController composes. OfferController itself and its picker/inquiry/bridge glue live in the net472 mod project (engine-bound: ItemPickerService, Verbs.BuyPrisonerVerb, NpcMemoryCampaignBehavior, MBInformationManager, RunLlmExchangeAsync), which this net10 test project cannot reference, so that glue is exercised in game rather than here. Only the precedence table is asserted below._

- GIVEN an outstanding bargain, WHEN routing, THEN it settles the bargain
- GIVEN an outstanding bargain alongside a supply and captives, WHEN routing, THEN the bargain still wins
- GIVEN no bargain but an outstanding supply, WHEN routing, THEN it settles the supply
- GIVEN a supply alongside held captives but no bargain, WHEN routing, THEN the supply wins over the captives
- GIVEN no bargain or supply but held captives, WHEN routing, THEN the category choice is offered
- GIVEN no bargain or supply and no captives, WHEN routing, THEN the inventory picker opens directly
- GIVEN a negative prisoner count, WHEN routing with nothing outstanding, THEN it still goes straight to the picker

### OpenAiApiKeyPolicyTests.cs

_Player request: "The ability to have separate endpoints for the NSFW and Interpret models is VERY helpful, but I also need the ability to have separate API keys for those endpoints, too, otherwise I can't use them." This policy decides which OpenAI-Compatible key a role sends: its own override, or the shared one. If it drifted, a role split onto its own server would keep sending the WRONG key (or none) and fail to authenticate, so these pin the fallback and the whitespace/trim handling the live client resolution relies on. Twin of OpenAiEndpointPolicyTests, same shape, one field later._

- GIVEN a role override, WHEN resolving, THEN the override wins
- GIVEN no role override, WHEN resolving, THEN the shared key is used
- GIVEN surrounding whitespace, WHEN resolving, THEN the result is trimmed
- GIVEN nothing configured, WHEN resolving, THEN it is empty

### OpenAiEndpointPolicyTests.cs

_Player request: run two local servers (one model each) and point different roles at different endpoints. This policy decides which OpenAI-Compatible endpoint a role calls: its own override, or the shared one. If it drifted, a role would silently hit the wrong server (or none), so these pin the fallback and the whitespace/trim handling the live client resolution relies on._

- GIVEN a role override, WHEN resolving, THEN the override wins
- GIVEN no role override, WHEN resolving, THEN the shared endpoint is used
- GIVEN surrounding whitespace, WHEN resolving, THEN the result is trimmed
- GIVEN nothing configured, WHEN resolving, THEN it is empty

### OpenPartnerLoverPolicyTests.cs

_Guards OpenPartnerLoverPolicy (Inc 3a): whether an OPEN partner takes a lover of her own this week, and whether a scanned candidate is a plausible one. This is the symmetry of the Inc 2 jealousy suppression: an open partner who accepts the player's other loves eventually exercises the same freedom herself, the likelier the more she is neglected. The weekly-chance curve feeds the mod's autonomy roll; the eligibility predicate pins WHO she may take. Get the curve wrong and a well-attended open partner strays constantly (drama the open pact was meant to defuse) or a neglected one never does (the feature is dead); get the predicate wrong and she takes the player, a married lord, a child, or a stranger, seeding cascade drama this increment deliberately keeps out._

- GIVEN more neglect, WHEN computing the weekly chance, THEN it rises
- GIVEN extreme neglect, WHEN computing the weekly chance, THEN it clamps to the max
- GIVEN an open partner with no lover, WHEN the roll lands under the chance, THEN she takes one
- GIVEN a partner who already has a lover, WHEN asked, THEN she takes no other
- GIVEN a partner not on open terms, WHEN asked, THEN she never strays
- GIVEN an unwed acquainted compatible adult, WHEN judged, THEN he is a plausible lover
- GIVEN the candidate is the player, WHEN judged, THEN he is rejected
- GIVEN a married candidate, WHEN judged, THEN he is rejected
- GIVEN a child candidate, WHEN judged, THEN he is rejected
- GIVEN a stranger, WHEN judged, THEN he is rejected
- GIVEN a member of the players household, WHEN judged, THEN he is rejected
- GIVEN an orientation incompatible candidate, WHEN judged, THEN he is rejected
- GIVEN an egalitarian partner with no open arrangement, WHEN asked, THEN she may not take a lover
- GIVEN an egalitarian partner with an open arrangement, WHEN asked, THEN she may take an open lover
- GIVEN a harem partner with an open arrangement, WHEN asked, THEN she takes a secret lover
- GIVEN a harem partner with no pact and the toggle on, WHEN asked, THEN she takes a secret affair
- GIVEN a harem partner with no pact and the toggle off, WHEN asked, THEN she stays faithful

### OpenRelationshipPolicyTests.cs

_Guards OpenRelationshipPolicy.SuppressesJealousy, the single pure rule both jealousy paths consult before letting a partner react: the daily competition loop (JealousyBehavior.EvaluateCompetition, which excludes an open partner from the aggrieved selection) and the event-triggered reaction (JealousyBehavior.FireReaction, which never fires for an open partner). Player report: a player whose several wives agreed, in conversation, to a harmonious no-jealousy arrangement still got jealousy pop-ups. The fix models an OPEN RELATIONSHIP: a partner who holds reciprocal open terms with the player is not jealous of the player's other loves. Get this wrong and either the open pact is ignored (the reported bug returns) or an ordinary partner's genuine grievance is silently swallowed._

- GIVEN an open arrangement, WHEN asking if jealousy is suppressed, THEN it is
- GIVEN no open arrangement, WHEN asking if jealousy is suppressed, THEN it is not

### OrientationParserTests.cs

_Guards the mapping from a hand-typed orientation word (character_overrides.json or the cr.orientation console command) to a SexualOrientation. Player request: let a player override an NPC's rolled orientation, e.g. to enable a female-female romance the roll had blocked (v1.30.0 CHANGELOG). The parsed value feeds CharacterOverride.ParsedOrientation, synced onto the NPC's RomanticProfile before every prompt, and the jealousy system's plausibility check reads it too (a same-sex pair is only plausible courtship once the orientation allows it). A word that fails to parse must return null quietly, never throw: a typo in a hand-edited file must never crash the campaign._

- GIVEN a known word, WHEN parsing, THEN it maps to the orientation
- GIVEN an unknown or empty word, WHEN parsing, THEN it returns null

### OutlawPolicyTests.cs

_Documents outlawry, bounties and amnesty (arrests Inc 5; Gabriel's rulings, 26/09/2026). The stakes: a bounty from the wrong realm would set the whole map on a player who robbed one caravan; a sovereign branded a brigand breaks the line Gabriel drew; a bounty that never ended, or ended too easily, would make outlawry either a life sentence or nothing at all._

- GIVEN a merchant attacked, WHEN the bounty is placed, THEN the sender and the receiver want the player
- GIVEN a fishing boat attacked, WHEN the bounty is placed, THEN only its owner wants the player
- GIVEN a merchant bound for its own realm, WHEN the bounty is placed, THEN one realm wants the player
- GIVEN who attacks whom, WHEN judged, THEN only a non ruler at peace is a brigand
- GIVEN the players standing, WHEN hunted, THEN an independent clan is warred on and a vassal gets hunters
- GIVEN a weekly roll, WHEN an amnesty is asked, THEN it comes only on a rare roll
- GIVEN the players number, WHEN a realm sends hunters, THEN they ride close to it within bounds
- GIVEN a lost band, WHEN new hunters are asked for, THEN they come only after the cooldown
- GIVEN a bounty, WHEN its captive is tried, THEN its cause is the charge
- GIVEN a realm with a bounty already, WHEN another is placed, THEN it keeps one
- GIVEN an outlaw, WHEN caught on a realms bounty, THEN that sovereign tries them on its charges
- GIVEN an outlaw, WHEN their realm pardons them, THEN the case is closed

### PanelTickSuppressionCounterTests.cs

_WHY IT MATTERS: this counter decides, for every frame, whether the mod skips the vanilla settlement-visual tick (SettlementVisualManager.OnTick) while a CR panel owns the screen. That tick's siege-bombardment branch calls GameEntity.SetGlobalFrame on projectiles in flight and access-violates natively under a pushed panel (four confirmed crashes, 0xC0000005). Too little suppression and the crash returns; suppression stuck on and the settlement/siege visuals freeze forever after the panel closes. The two subtle failure modes both live here and are why this is pure and tested rather than only watched in game: the count MUST stay above zero across a panel reload's close-then-reopen gap (the repeated-cr.reload_ui crash is exactly a tick slipping through that gap), and it MUST never underflow (teardown reaches the count by two paths, an explicit close and the engine's finalize)._

- GIVEN a fresh counter, WHEN nothing has opened, THEN it does not suppress
- GIVEN a panel opened, WHEN it holds the screen, THEN the tick is suppressed
- GIVEN a panel opened and closed, WHEN balanced, THEN suppression clears
- GIVEN an open panel reloaded, WHEN it closes then reopens, THEN suppression never drops in the gap
- GIVEN more exits than enters, WHEN it would underflow, THEN it stays at zero
- GIVEN two panels open, WHEN only one closes, THEN suppression holds until both close
- GIVEN residual suppression, WHEN reset for a new session, THEN it clears

### PastOnMentionPolicyTests.cs

_PastOnMentionPolicy decides which past events a speaker brings to a turn in which the player named a person, a place or a realm, and how each is worded. Gabriel (17/10/2026): the long-term memory "pourrait parfois etre utilise pour des conversations entre joueur et NPC sur le passe", and the authored history file is there to "mettre en scene des evenements du passe". If the pick is wrong, a lord asked about his own father's execution draws a blank, or a passing word drags up a battle of ten years ago. If the wording is wrong, he tells a betrayal of before the campaign as fresh news, or as hearsay when he was the betrayer._

- GIVEN the player names a lord, WHEN the past is weighed, THEN an event naming him is brought up
- GIVEN the player names no one in the event, WHEN the past is weighed, THEN nothing is brought up
- GIVEN the player names the place of an event, WHEN the past is weighed, THEN the event is brought up
- GIVEN many events of a named realm, WHEN the past is weighed, THEN at most three are brought up
- GIVEN matches of different weight, WHEN the past is weighed, THEN the greatest then the nearest comes first
- GIVEN an event already shown this turn, WHEN the past is weighed, THEN it is not repeated
- GIVEN an event nine years old, WHEN it is worded, THEN the line says nine years ago
- GIVEN the speaker was in the event, WHEN it is worded, THEN the line says he was part of it
- GIVEN an event older than the speaker, WHEN it is worded, THEN the line says he knows it as told
- GIVEN a word shared by many place names, WHEN the message uses it, THEN it names none of them
- GIVEN a secret event, WHEN it is worded, THEN the line carries the secret tag

### PatrolLetterTextTests.cs

_The patrolling companion's monthly letter and the player's stop order by letter (Gabriel, 16/10/2026). Wrong, the companion reports a fight that never happened, a "come back" goes unheard, a "keep on" brings the men home, or the control line shows up in the letter the player reads._

- GIVEN a month of patrol, WHEN the letter context is built, THEN it holds the real record
- GIVEN a month without a fight, WHEN the letter context is built, THEN it says no fight came
- GIVEN an answer with the stop line, WHEN read, THEN the patrol is ordered home
- GIVEN an answer without a stop, WHEN read, THEN the patrol goes on
- GIVEN an answer with the stop line, WHEN the letter is kept, THEN the line is gone

### PeaceMediationPolicyTests.cs

_mediate_peace (third-party war mediation): the pure gate deciding whether the player, as a MEDIATOR talking to a FACTION LEADER, may broker peace between that leader's faction and a NAMED enemy faction. This is the NEW third-party diplomacy verb; before it, make_peace existed only as a council Parley motion for the player's OWN faction, so there was no way to broker peace between OTHER realms in conversation._

- GIVEN a leader at war and a trusted high standing player, WHEN evaluating, THEN they can mediate
- GIVEN regard and tier exactly at their floors, WHEN evaluating, THEN it passes
- GIVEN the npc is not a faction leader, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is refused even at full regard and stature
- GIVEN the leaders faction is not at war with the target, WHEN evaluating, THEN there is no war to mediate
- GIVEN a trusted player below the stature floor, WHEN evaluating, THEN stature is too low
- GIVEN a high standing player the leader does not deeply trust, WHEN evaluating, THEN regard is too low
- GIVEN a non leader with no war and a low stature low regard player, WHEN evaluating, THEN the leader gate is reported first
- GIVEN a leader with no war and a low stature low regard player, WHEN evaluating, THEN the no war gate is reported first

### PendingMatchBookTests.cs

_The door another mod passes to hand the player a match to broker, and the book that keeps it. tashmetu (Nexus, 16/10/2026): a head of house agreed in the chat to give his kinswoman to a lord of the player's house, as Regard and Standing's matchmaker had proposed, and nothing happened, because arrange_match is taught only for a match asked of the player by letter. Gabriel approved the door the same day. Wrong one way, a head of house is taught a match the law then refutes (CR declares before the model speaks, never refutes after); wrong the other way, a lawful match is refused at the door, or a match already settled is taught again._

- GIVEN a lawful couple and the head of her house, WHEN registered, THEN it is taken
- GIVEN a decider with no say over her, WHEN registered, THEN it is refused with that reason
- GIVEN a couple the game would not wed, WHEN registered, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN an unknown id or one person twice, WHEN registered, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the player as suitor or bride, WHEN registered, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a registered match, WHEN its decider is asked, THEN it is found and another decider finds nothing
- GIVEN the same couple registered twice, WHEN counted, THEN one match stands with the latest decider
- GIVEN a match that no longer stands, WHEN pruned, THEN it is dropped
- GIVEN a withdrawn match, WHEN its decider is asked, THEN nothing is found

### PerceivedImagePolicyTests.cs

_The perceived image (ROADMAP, SELF-ESTEEM, P1; Gabriel 30/09/2026: "la combinaison des deux devrait permettre de generer une image percue du NPC qui serait affichee dans le panneau d'info sur le NPC dans le chat"). One numberless sentence: how he carries himself (self-esteem) and how his day shows (the mood of the day). Ruling 2: it shows what shows, so a man who keeps face hides a shaken self-regard from a stranger, and those who know him see through it._

- GIVEN a proud man on a grim day, WHEN seen, THEN both his bearing and his day show
- GIVEN a woman, WHEN seen, THEN she is described as she
- GIVEN a shaken man who keeps face, WHEN a stranger looks, THEN he seems at ease
- GIVEN a shaken man who keeps face, WHEN someone who knows him well looks, THEN they see the strain
- GIVEN a shaken man who does not keep face, WHEN a stranger looks, THEN it shows
- GIVEN a steady man on an ordinary day, WHEN seen, THEN a plain sentence is given
- GIVEN nothing known, WHEN seen, THEN nothing is said
- GIVEN any reading, WHEN seen, THEN no number or band name appears

### PersonaHarshnessTests.cs

_tashmetu (Nexus, 02/10/2026): "A tester saw a companion's sister come out very cruel, beyond what her traits suggest. Is that the model, or something in how the persona is built?" It was the persona. HeroProfileMapper picks an archetype from the COMBINATION of a hero's traits, and three ordinary -1 traits (Generosity, Honor, Mercy) drew "Sadistic. Takes pleasure in the suffering of others... A predator wearing a lord's title", which the prompt then tells the model not to soften. A -1 trait is a leaning: on its own, Mercy -1 already reads "Not needlessly cruel; simply unmoved". If these guards fail, an ordinary hard character is played as a monster again, or a save keeps the old wording for good._

- GIVEN the persona catalog, WHEN read, THEN no archetype says the character enjoys suffering
- GIVEN the retired archetypes, WHEN the catalog is read, THEN none of them is still handed out
- GIVEN a stored persona with a retired archetype, WHEN checked, THEN it is due for the new wording
- GIVEN a current persona, WHEN checked, THEN it is left alone

### PersonaTraitIntensityTests.cs

_Found answering tashmetu (02/10/2026), fixed at Gabriel's word ("corrige aussi les traits a +-2"): the persona catalog matched a trait only at exactly -1 or +1, so a hero whose trait sat at -2 or +2, the game's strongest, met no archetype at all and got the generic "figure of contradictions" line. The most marked characters were the least described. The catalog now reads a trait's DIRECTION, and what is extreme is said in a sentence of its own. If these rules are wrong, the strongest natures fall back to a shrug again, or a mere leaning is described as an extreme._

- GIVEN a trait level, WHEN the archetype is looked up, THEN it is read by its direction
- GIVEN only leanings, WHEN described, THEN nothing is called extreme
- GIVEN mercy at its lowest, WHEN described, THEN the want of mercy is called extreme
- GIVEN every trait at its lowest, WHEN described, THEN nothing speaks of pleasure in suffering
- GIVEN two extremes, WHEN described, THEN both are named

### PlayerCompanyPolicyTests.cs

_nerethi (Nexus) asked to invite someone into a conversation. Gabriel's design (03/10/2026): "Il devrait y avoir request private et invite someone toujours disponibles; invite someone permet de selectionner un compagnon, un prisonnier ou un Lord qui voyage avec le joueur. Alors que request private repropose au NPC principal de la conversation de revenir seul. Si les temoins sont tous des temoins accompagnant le joueur, alors le request private est accepte automatiquement." If these rules are wrong: a stranger is offered as "yours", the person you are talking to is invited into her own conversation, your own people refuse to step out, or a lord's guards leave without the lord having a say._

- GIVEN the players company, WHEN inviting, THEN companions prisoners and travelling lords are offered
- GIVEN one already present and one being spoken to, WHEN inviting, THEN neither is offered
- GIVEN only the players own people in the room, WHEN privacy is asked, THEN it is granted at once
- GIVEN one witness who is not the players, WHEN privacy is asked, THEN the npc decides
- GIVEN the players prisoner, WHEN privacy is asked, THEN it is granted at once
- GIVEN a request still waiting or an empty room, WHEN privacy is asked, THEN nothing is asked

### PlayerDeedValenceTests.cs

_Protects the influence-loop valence — how a deed the player did lands on an NPC who only HEARD of it (a wound to one's own side, a boon to one's champion), the colour behind "what is said of you"._

- GIVEN a deed that struck the observers own faction, WHEN classified, THEN it is a wound
- GIVEN a deed that struck the observers kin but not their faction, WHEN classified, THEN it is a wound
- GIVEN a deed by the observers own faction, WHEN classified, THEN it is a boon
- GIVEN a distant deed touching neither side, WHEN classified, THEN it is neutral
- GIVEN a deed that both served the players side AND struck the observers kin, WHEN classified, THEN the wound wins

### PlayerDivorceConsentPolicyTests.cs

- GIVEN a deeply miserable spouse, WHEN the player ends it, THEN the split is mutual
- GIVEN a happy spouse, WHEN the player ends it, THEN the split is repudiated not mutual
- GIVEN null facts, WHEN evaluating, THEN the split is treated as repudiated
- GIVEN the spouse already has a pending demand, WHEN the player ends it, THEN the split is mutual
- GIVEN the spouse relation sits at the unhappy threshold, WHEN the player ends it, THEN the split is mutual
- GIVEN the spouse relation sits one above the unhappy threshold, WHEN the player ends it, THEN the split is repudiated

### PlayerDressPolicyTests.cs

_Perception layer: pins the four-band classification NPCs will use to notice how the player is dressed._

- GIVEN nothing covers the body or legs, WHEN classifying, THEN the band is Naked
- GIVEN isBodyOrLegCovered is false, WHEN the armor flag contradictorily says true, THEN the band is still Naked
- GIVEN battle armor is worn, WHEN classifying, THEN the band is Armoured regardless of garment tier
- GIVEN civilian clothing at the lowest tier, WHEN classifying, THEN the band is Rags
- GIVEN civilian clothing one tier above the rags ceiling, WHEN classifying, THEN the band is Civilian
- GIVEN a negative garment tier, WHEN classifying, THEN it resolves without throwing

### PlayerExecutionPolicyTests.cs

_This policy is the single gate standing between an LLM's [ACTION] and a REAL, IRREVERSIBLE kill of the player's character (game over, or heir succession where a clan heir exists). It exists because a player asked for exactly this (parity with the "AI Executes" mod, COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md Partie 10.6), but the stakes demand every guard hold at once: opt-in MCM (default OFF), genuine captivity, the same Hardcore/Adult gate every other captive-scene act requires, and Bannerlord's own campaign death setting. What breaks if this is wrong: get it wrong toward "true" and a stray or premature emission permanently ends a player's campaign; get it wrong toward "false" and the feature a player explicitly asked for silently never fires._

- GIVEN the MCM toggle is off, WHEN every other guard holds, THEN execution is refused
- GIVEN the player is not captive, WHEN every other guard holds, THEN execution is refused
- GIVEN content level is below Hardcore, WHEN every other guard holds, THEN execution is refused
- GIVEN the campaign death setting disallows death, WHEN every other guard holds, THEN execution is refused
- GIVEN every guard holds, THEN execution is permitted
- GIVEN every combination of the four guards, THEN only all true permits execution

### PlayerForcePolicyTests.cs

_This policy answers one question for EVERY face-to-face interlocutor: how many men does this person see standing behind the player? The answer becomes EncounterContext.PlayerPartyTroopCount, which the SDK renders as a coarse band ("a warband of well over a hundred soldiers rides at their back") plus a directive forbidding the NPC to call the player alone or an easy mark. It exists because of a player report: an NPC treated a player escorted by two thousand Nords as though they had come alone. Get the count wrong and the prompt lies about a fact the NPC can SEE, which is the one class of error the whole grounding effort is meant to prevent. These tests pin which force counts, and when none does._

- GIVEN the player leads an army, WHEN resolving, THEN the whole army is what is seen
- GIVEN the player only rides inside anothers army, WHEN resolving, THEN only their own party is seen
- GIVEN a captive player, WHEN resolving, THEN no force is seen however large the party
- GIVEN a captive player who led an army, WHEN resolving, THEN captivity still wins
- GIVEN a player travelling alone, WHEN resolving, THEN nothing is seen
- GIVEN a nonsensical negative count, WHEN resolving, THEN it is floored at nothing

### PlayerNameRevealPolicyTests.cs

_A brigand never knows a noble's name from a register (Gabriel's rule), so the only way a bandit captor learns it is the prisoner giving it up in the conversation. This detects that moment, so the mod can write it onto the captor's memory and a recurring nemesis keeps knowing it. Whole-word, case-insensitive._

- GIVEN the player states their name, WHEN checking, THEN it is a reveal
- GIVEN the name in any case, WHEN checking, THEN it is a reveal
- GIVEN the name only inside a longer word, WHEN checking, THEN it is not a reveal
- GIVEN a message without the name, WHEN checking, THEN it is not a reveal
- GIVEN only a short connector matches, WHEN checking, THEN it is not a reveal
- GIVEN a family name part, WHEN checking, THEN it is a reveal
- GIVEN blank input, WHEN checking, THEN it is not a reveal

### PlayerNameTextTests.cs

_What the screen shows where a stored or written text says "the player" (Gabriel, 17/10/2026: never "the player" in the game, the character by name). Wrong one way, a grievance or a memory breaks the fourth wall; wrong the other, a word that only looks like it ("players", "the player's" possessive) comes out mangled._

- GIVEN a stored cause naming the player, WHEN it is shown, THEN it names the character
- GIVEN the role word capitalised or as player character, WHEN it is shown, THEN both read the name
- GIVEN a plural or no role word, WHEN it is shown, THEN it is unchanged
- GIVEN no name known, WHEN a text is shown, THEN it is unchanged

### PlayerStandingPolicyTests.cs

_Guards a bug that SHIPPED and reached a player (JoshGray700, Nexus 2026-07-18, v1.30.16, Grok low reasoning). After joining the Aserai as a vassal, the Sultan Unqid was told by the prompt "The player is your liege lord.", and answered in character: "I am no Sultan, only Unqid of Banu Hulyan, sworn to the realm you now lead... What do you require of me this day, my liege?" He denied his own throne, invented a third-party Sultan to reconcile the contradiction with his rank line, and called his new vassal his liege._

- GIVEN the npc rules the realm the player swore to, WHEN describing, THEN the player is the vassal
- GIVEN the player rules the realm the npc serves, WHEN describing, THEN the player is the liege
- GIVEN the realm is known, WHEN describing, THEN the note says who rules it
- GIVEN the realm is unknown, WHEN describing, THEN the bare standing is still stated
- GIVEN the player is held by a ruler they serve, WHEN describing, THEN captivity wins
- GIVEN the npc is of the players own clan, WHEN describing, THEN kinship wins
- GIVEN the player is hired by the realm the npc rules, WHEN describing, THEN they are not called a vassal
- GIVEN a mercenary contract, WHEN describing, THEN the note says it is pay and not fealty
- GIVEN both serve the same crown and neither rules, WHEN describing, THEN they are named fellow vassals
- GIVEN fellow vassals, WHEN describing, THEN neither is placed above the other
- GIVEN the players realm hires the npcs clan, WHEN describing, THEN the player is paymaster not liege
- GIVEN a hired band in the realm the player serves, WHEN describing, THEN they are not called fellow vassals
- GIVEN no oath binds them, WHEN describing, THEN no standing is claimed
- GIVEN no facts at all, WHEN resolving, THEN the standing is unknown

### PleadCasePolicyTests.cs

_Lordfadooboo (Nexus, 01/10/2026): "a lord asking you to argue on their behalf to their family leader or faction leader". The plea is done when the person named is truly won over toward the giver, in their own conversation. If these rules are wrong, a plea completes when the player turned someone AGAINST the giver, or when he persuaded the wrong person, or about the wrong lord; or a lord sends the player to plead before a child or a dead man._

- GIVEN a living adult who holds a court, WHEN named, THEN the plea may be asked
- GIVEN someone who holds no court, WHEN named, THEN no such plea exists
- GIVEN the dead a child or the giver, WHEN named, THEN no such plea exists
- GIVEN the named person won over toward the giver, WHEN the sway lands, THEN the plea is answered
- GIVEN the named person turned against the giver, WHEN the sway lands, THEN the plea is not answered
- GIVEN the wrong person or the wrong subject, WHEN the sway lands, THEN the plea is not answered

### PoliticalMapPolicyTests.cs

_WHY IT MATTERS: this is the whole logic of the World Almanac's political minimap. It turns the kingdoms' settlements into a coloured territory grid by nearest-settlement assignment (a Voronoi map), with the sea masked out; the engine side only gathers the anchors + a water predicate and rasterises the returned grid into the panel image. Every way the map can be wrong lives here: a cell painted the wrong kingdom's colour (Voronoi boundary off), the ocean bleeding faction colour (water not masked), a kingdom clipped at the image edge (bounds not padded), or the map flickering between redraws (a tie resolved non-deterministically). None of that is visible to a dotnet test once it is meshes and pixels, so it is pinned here._

- GIVEN no anchors, WHEN building, THEN the grid is all transparent
- GIVEN one anchor and no water, WHEN building, THEN every cell takes its colour
- GIVEN two anchors, WHEN building, THEN each cell takes the nearest colour
- GIVEN a water predicate, WHEN building, THEN water cells stay transparent
- GIVEN anchors, WHEN computing bounds, THEN the extent is padded on every side
- GIVEN a cell equidistant from two anchors, WHEN building, THEN the first anchor wins
- GIVEN a non positive dimension, WHEN building, THEN it returns an empty grid without throwing
- GIVEN bounds, WHEN mapping cells to world, THEN the corners and centre land where expected

### PortraitCapPolicyTests.cs

_WHY IT MATTERS: every chat line's portrait is a real GPU render-target (a character tableau), created per line with no engine-side pooling. Left unmanaged, a long scene accumulates dozens of live render-targets and exhausts VRAM (the "create_render_texture: Not enough memory resources" crash, CHANGELOG v1.23.0). This policy is what keeps live portraits bounded to the participant count instead of the transcript length: too little released and VRAM still climbs; one released too many leaves a speaker's line blank. Consumed by PortraitTextureGuard.EnforceOnePerParticipant / ReleaseAll, called on every new chat line and on window close._

- GIVEN one line per speaker, WHEN capping, THEN nothing is released
- GIVEN a speaker who spoke three times, WHEN capping, THEN only their newest line keeps its portrait
- GIVEN several participants each repeating, WHEN capping, THEN exactly one live portrait remains per participant
- GIVEN portraitless system lines interleaved, WHEN capping, THEN they are ignored
- GIVEN a capped transcript, WHEN capping again, THEN nothing more is released
- GIVEN a long scene from a bounded cast, WHEN capping after each line, THEN live portraits never exceed the participant count
- GIVEN a window closing, WHEN releasing all, THEN every live portrait is freed
- GIVEN a null list, WHEN capping or releasing, THEN it is a no op

### PostHistoryInstructionsTests.cs

_Documents the modder-editable "post-history instructions" hook (post_history_instructions.txt): free-form text injected at the VERY END of the system prompt — the highest-recency position, the analog of a SillyTavern post-history / jailbreak field. The flagship guarantee is that it is read LAST, so it carries the strongest steer._

- GIVEN post history text, WHEN building the prompt, THEN it appears at the very end
- GIVEN no post history text, WHEN building the prompt, THEN nothing is added and the marker is absent
- GIVEN post history text, WHEN building the commoner prompt, THEN it also ends with it

### PowerBalanceTests.cs

_PowerBalance.DescribeFor turns the raw military/political facts (fief counts, war state, captivity, a recent battlefield defeat) into the POWER BALANCE prompt note that colours a lord's candor - how deferential, wary, or defiant they sound. Per the CHANGELOG this is meant to read as "calculated, wary deference rather than blind defiance or empty submission": too strong a note and every lord becomes a doormat regardless of standing; too weak (or silent when it shouldn't be) and a captive, or a lord losing a war, keeps talking as if nothing were at stake. Captivity always overrides territory (rank stops mattering the moment you are the player's prisoner); the thresholds below pin exactly how big an asymmetry has to be before a lord finds it worth remarking on at all._

- GIVEN a captive lord of a strong kingdom, WHEN describing, THEN captivity overrides territory
- GIVEN a captive who also lost recently, WHEN describing, THEN only captivity is spoken
- GIVEN a lord with no realm standing who lost to the player recently, WHEN describing, THEN the defeat is spoken
- GIVEN a precarious position compounded by a recent defeat, WHEN describing, THEN both are voiced
- GIVEN at peace and the npc is markedly stronger, WHEN describing, THEN they hold the stronger position
- GIVEN at peace and the player is markedly stronger, WHEN describing, THEN the fief disparity is named
- GIVEN at peace but below the fief floor, WHEN describing, THEN the thin asymmetry is left unspoken
- GIVEN at war against a fiefless kingdom, WHEN describing, THEN the rout reads as precarious
- GIVEN at war and the npc holds twice the territory, WHEN describing, THEN they hold a clear advantage
- GIVEN at war and the player holds twice the territory, WHEN describing, THEN the position is precarious
- GIVEN at war with evenly matched realms, WHEN describing, THEN the outcome is uncertain
- GIVEN evenly matched realms and both clans hold land, WHEN describing, THEN nothing is remarked
- GIVEN evenly matched realms but the npc clan holds no fiefs, WHEN describing, THEN the clan gap is noted
- GIVEN neither side is in a kingdom and nothing else weighs, WHEN describing, THEN no note
- GIVEN the npc is not a lord, WHEN describing, THEN no note is issued
- GIVEN the npc is the players prisoner, WHEN describing, THEN captivity is named

### PregnancyNotePolicyTests.cs

_Protects the fix for a player report: a pregnant NPC woman has no idea she is pregnant in chat and denies it when asked, because her pregnancy state was never injected into the LLM prompt. Describe's whole purpose is the ACKNOWLEDGEMENT directive ("you are aware of this and never deny being with child"), every test here either pins that the directive always fires while pregnant, or that the stage/father wording built around it never contradicts the game's own facts._

- GIVEN a woman who is not pregnant, WHEN describing, THEN returns empty string
- GIVEN an unknown total duration, WHEN describing, THEN returns empty string
- GIVEN a pregnancy barely begun, WHEN describing, THEN early weeks stage
- GIVEN elapsed fraction just under one third, WHEN describing, THEN still early weeks stage
- GIVEN elapsed fraction at one third, WHEN describing, THEN visibly pregnant stage
- GIVEN a pregnancy at the halfway point, WHEN describing, THEN visibly pregnant stage
- GIVEN elapsed fraction just under two thirds, WHEN describing, THEN still visibly pregnant stage
- GIVEN elapsed fraction at two thirds, WHEN describing, THEN heavily pregnant stage
- GIVEN a pregnancy almost due, WHEN describing, THEN heavily pregnant stage
- GIVEN a namable lord father, WHEN describing, THEN names him as the father
- GIVEN a named father who is not namable, WHEN describing, THEN no father clause but still acknowledges
- GIVEN the player is the namable father, WHEN describing, THEN names the player as the father
- GIVEN the player is the father but not namable, WHEN describing, THEN no father clause but still acknowledges
- GIVEN any pregnant state, WHEN describing, THEN the never deny directive is always present

### PregnancyPolicyTests.cs

_Protects the fix for a player report: a companion romance conceived while the campaign's Life/Death Cycle option was disabled (no aging, no birth), and the pregnancy never delivered - vanilla's own PregnancyCampaignBehavior.DailyTickHero gates ITS ENTIRE offspring-delivery check behind the same `IsLifeDeathCycleDisabled` flag (confirmed by decompiling the engine), so she stayed "pregnant" for game-years and the NPC would not stop mentioning it in chat. MayConceive stops a NEW pregnancy from ever being created in that state; ShouldEndStuckPregnancy decides when an EXISTING tracked pregnancy has gone stuck long enough that PregnancyBirthBehavior's daily cleanup should end it childless. Get either wrong toward "false" and a pregnancy lingers forever again; get ShouldEndStuckPregnancy wrong toward "true" and a pregnancy in a NORMAL campaign (births enabled) could be cut short before vanilla ever gets a chance to deliver it._

- GIVEN births are disabled, WHEN deciding whether to conceive, THEN conception is refused
- GIVEN births are enabled, WHEN deciding whether to conceive, THEN conception is permitted
- GIVEN births are enabled, WHEN a pregnancy is far past its due date, THEN cleanup never fires
- GIVEN births are disabled, WHEN the pregnancy is not yet due, THEN cleanup does not fire
- GIVEN births are disabled, WHEN overdue exactly at the margin, THEN cleanup does not fire yet
- GIVEN births are disabled, WHEN overdue one day past the margin, THEN cleanup fires
- GIVEN births are disabled, WHEN the pregnancy has been stuck for game years, THEN cleanup fires

### PrisonerBuyerPolicyTests.cs

_Documents who may send an envoy about a captive the player holds (prisoner exchange, increment 4). fkasad (Nexus, 25/09/2026): "one of my allies who, hating this prisoner, would like to buy him from me. Or a father wishing to buy back his daughter (a lady who changed clans upon marriage)." Neither could ever come before: only the captive's clan leader, else his realm's leader. This order decides whose envoy the player meets, and whether the captive goes home or into another's cells._

- GIVEN a daughter married into another house, WHEN buyers are ordered, THEN her father comes after her house and before her realm
- GIVEN lords who bitterly hate the captive, WHEN buyers are ordered, THEN only the most hateful is one
- GIVEN a lord who only dislikes the captive, WHEN buyers are ordered, THEN he is not one
- GIVEN a lord who cannot treat, WHEN buyers are ordered, THEN he is left out
- GIVEN a lord of the players own clan, WHEN buyers are ordered, THEN he sends no envoy
- GIVEN a lord who is both house and realm, WHEN buyers are ordered, THEN he is listed once as the house
- GIVEN each kind of buyer, WHEN asked what he wants, THEN only an enemy wants the captive kept
- GIVEN a father, WHEN the stake is worded, THEN it names her as his daughter

### PrisonerDeedTargetTests.cs

_Guards the deliver-a-prisoner deed's matching: the right captive satisfies it, a named hero wins over a faction demand, and "do I already hold one" (case a vs b) is read correctly — a drift hands over or credits the wrong prisoner._

- GIVEN a named hero demand, WHEN matching, THEN only that hero satisfies
- GIVEN a faction demand, WHEN matching, THEN any captive of that faction satisfies
- GIVEN both a hero and a faction, WHEN matching, THEN the named hero takes precedence
- GIVEN a targetless demand, WHEN asked, THEN it is invalid and matches nothing
- GIVEN the player holds a matching captive, WHEN classifying, THEN it is case a
- GIVEN the player holds no matching captive, WHEN classifying, THEN it is case b

### PrisonerExchangePolicyTests.cs

_Documents the rules of a prisoner exchange. fkasad (Nexus, 25/09/2026) holds captive lords rather than selling them and asked for exchanges; Gabriel ruled the same day that only heroes change hands (lords, vassals, companions, never troops) and that the offer always comes through an envoy. These rules decide what an envoy may propose and what the swap costs, so a wrong answer here is a bargain the player can see is absurd, or a soldier traded like a lord._

- GIVEN a captive lord or companion, WHEN asked, THEN they may be exchanged
- GIVEN a captive soldier, WHEN asked, THEN he is never exchanged
- GIVEN a notable a child or the dead, WHEN asked, THEN none of them is exchanged
- GIVEN two captives of near equal worth, WHEN the swap is priced, THEN no gold changes hands
- GIVEN the player gives the dearer captive, WHEN the swap is priced, THEN the other side pays the difference
- GIVEN the player receives the dearer captive, WHEN the swap is priced, THEN the player pays the difference
- GIVEN a captive whose worth cannot be read, WHEN the swap is priced, THEN it counts at the sale fallback
- GIVEN several captives the other side holds, WHEN it chooses what to offer, THEN it is the closest in worth
- GIVEN the other side holds only troops, WHEN it chooses what to offer, THEN it offers nobody
- GIVEN the other side holds one of the players people, WHEN it chooses what to offer, THEN that one comes first
- GIVEN the wanted captive among their hold, WHEN it chooses what to offer, THEN it never offers him for himself
- GIVEN someone of the players own clan, WHEN asked whether captives can be exchanged with them, THEN they cannot
- GIVEN a lords envoy came about a captive recently, WHEN that lord would send another, THEN he waits
- GIVEN no envoy ever came about a captive, WHEN one would come, THEN he may
- GIVEN another lords envoy came about the captive days ago, WHEN a new lord would send one, THEN he waits out the captives spacing
- GIVEN envoys already came about a captive he still holds, WHEN another would come, THEN nobody comes any more
- GIVEN an envoy came about another prisoner days ago, WHEN one would come about this one, THEN he waits
- GIVEN fewer envoys than the limit came, WHEN the spacing has passed, THEN another may come
- GIVEN a captive just taken, WHEN an envoy would come, THEN he waits for the news to travel
- GIVEN a captive held for some days, WHEN an envoy would come, THEN he may
- GIVEN a captive handed to his own side, WHEN the exchange is done, THEN he goes free
- GIVEN a captive handed to a side not his own, WHEN the exchange is done, THEN he stays a prisoner in their hands

### PrisonerOfferAvailabilityPolicyTests.cs

_whether a lord's written offer to buy one of your prisoners can still be honoured when you open the letter, and what the player is told when it cannot._

- GIVEN the captive is still held, WHEN judged, THEN the offer can be taken
- GIVEN the captive has left your prison, WHEN judged, THEN it says so by name
- GIVEN each way an offer can lapse, WHEN judged, THEN they are told apart
- GIVEN no terms at all, WHEN judged, THEN that is the reason given rather than a missing captive
- GIVEN any blocked offer, WHEN described, THEN it reads as a sentence even with no name

### PrisonerOfferPolicyTests.cs

_PrisonerOffer letters name a price for a captive the player holds, and the read window's Accept settles the ransom at that figure. This policy is the ONLY place that figure is decided, so these tests pin it: a lord with a personal stake opens higher than an opportunist, and every figure stays inside the honest ransom band the face-to-face trade uses (PrisonerTradePolicy). If the premium escaped the band, a letter could name an absurd sum the Accept would then pay out; if it never rose, a kinsman would offer no more than a stranger._

- GIVEN a personal stake, WHEN pricing the offer, THEN it exceeds an opportunist at the same anchor
- GIVEN extreme anchors, WHEN pricing, THEN the offer stays inside the ransom band
- GIVEN an envoy already turned away, WHEN the lord sends another, THEN he offers more
- GIVEN many envoys turned away, WHEN pricing, THEN the raise stays inside the band
- GIVEN a nonpositive anchor, WHEN pricing, THEN it falls back rather than zero

### PrisonerRecruitmentPolicyTests.cs

_v1.34.3 recruit_prisoner: the pure gate deciding whether a captured HERO the player holds prisoner can be persuaded to switch sides and swear into the player's clan. This closes a specific player report (Nexus): a player convinced a captured noble to join them, the LLM narrated the switch, and nothing happened, because no executor existed (join_clan excludes prisoners; turn_nemesis is reserved for a tracked bandit nemesis)._

- GIVEN a held non leader prisoner at the regard floor, WHEN evaluating, THEN they can be recruited
- GIVEN regard exactly at the floor, WHEN evaluating, THEN it passes
- GIVEN regard just below the floor, WHEN evaluating, THEN regard is too low
- GIVEN a held prisoner who leads their own clan, WHEN evaluating, THEN they cannot be recruited even at full regard
- GIVEN a hero the player does not hold, WHEN evaluating, THEN they are not held by the player
- GIVEN a prisoner not held who also leads a clan with low regard, WHEN evaluating, THEN custody is reported first

### PrisonerReleasePolicyTests.cs

_Guards the freedom-bargain branching: a held lord is freed on the spot, another's captive is a rescue deed, a non-prisoner gets nothing, and only a lord may bargain at all._

- GIVEN a captive lord, WHEN asked, THEN they may bargain for freedom
- GIVEN a commoner or a free lord, WHEN asked, THEN they may not
- GIVEN the players own captive, WHEN classifying, THEN it is an immediate release
- GIVEN a captive held by another lord, WHEN classifying, THEN it is a rescue deed
- GIVEN someone who is not a prisoner, WHEN classifying, THEN there is no bargain

### PrisonerTradePaymentTests.cs

_What a captive's buyer may SAY and what is PAID must be the same figure, and a ransom is paid once._

- GIVEN a rich buyer, WHEN asking the most they can pay, THEN it is the captives ceiling
- GIVEN a buyer short of the ceiling, WHEN asking the most they can pay, THEN it is their purse
- GIVEN an empty purse, WHEN asking the most they can pay, THEN it is nothing
- GIVEN the named figure was paid, WHEN noting the payment, THEN there is nothing to add
- GIVEN less was paid because the purse ran out, WHEN noting the payment, THEN it says so
- GIVEN less was paid because of the ceiling, WHEN noting the payment, THEN it says so
- GIVEN more was paid because of the floor, WHEN noting the payment, THEN it says so
- GIVEN no figure was named, WHEN noting the payment, THEN there is nothing to add
- GIVEN a captive traded this turn, WHEN a separate payment is also emitted, THEN it is the same payment
- GIVEN another deal moving its own coin, WHEN gold is also emitted, THEN it is the same payment
- GIVEN no captive traded this turn, WHEN gold is given, THEN it stands
- GIVEN a captive traded this turn, WHEN reading the trade itself, THEN it is not a separate payment

### PrisonerTradePolicyTests.cs

_Prisoner trade (ratified 2026-07-08): the LLM proposes a price in-fiction, but the BRIDGE IS LAW here._

- GIVEN a price within the honest band, WHEN clamping, THEN the negotiated figure stands
- GIVEN a lowball below the floor, WHEN clamping, THEN it rises to the floor
- GIVEN an absurd premium, WHEN clamping, THEN it falls to the ceiling
- GIVEN no price emitted, WHEN clamping, THEN the sale lands at the floor not zero
- GIVEN an unreadable ransom anchor, WHEN clamping, THEN the fallback anchor rules
- GIVEN a worthless captive, WHEN clamping, THEN the absolute minimum price holds
- GIVEN a valuable captive, WHEN reading the minimum acceptable payment, THEN it is the forty percent floor
- GIVEN a worthless captive, WHEN reading the minimum acceptable payment, THEN the absolute minimum holds
- GIVEN an unreadable ransom anchor, WHEN reading the minimum acceptable payment, THEN the fallback rules
- GIVEN the captive stands in the npcs own cage, WHEN reading the trade, THEN the npc can only be selling
- GIVEN the player holds the captive, WHEN reading the trade, THEN the npc can only be buying
- GIVEN nobody here holds that captive, WHEN reading the trade, THEN there is no trade to run
- GIVEN both sides appear to hold them, WHEN reading the trade, THEN the players own roster decides
- GIVEN a captive held by another house, WHEN the wrong lord agrees to sell, THEN the player is told who holds them
- GIVEN a captive of the players own house, WHEN a lord agrees to sell, THEN the player is told why not
- GIVEN no captive by that name, WHEN a lord agrees to sell, THEN the refusal says he holds none

### PromptProfilesTests.cs

_The Prompt Composer, increment 2 (docs/design/prompt-composer/DESIGN.md; Gabriel, 11/10/2026: "Un param MCM pourrait designer l'ordonnanceur a utiliser, car il pourrait y avoir plusieurs mods qui proposent un ordonnanceur", "Mise en page par type"). Decides which prompt profiles exist, what a profile's files say, which layout a character gets, and which profile stays chosen in MCM. If it is wrong: a mod's profile is never offered, a player's is wiped by an update, a broken file breaks every conversation instead of falling back to stock, a lord is given a spouse's layout, or adding a mod silently switches the player to another mod's prompt._

- GIVEN the players spouse who is also a lord, WHEN the kind is decided, THEN it is spouse
- GIVEN a companion in a captive scene, WHEN the kind is decided, THEN it is companion
- GIVEN each station, WHEN the kind is decided, THEN it follows the station
- GIVEN custom and two mods with profiles, WHEN listed, THEN stock then the players then each mods
- GIVEN two profiles with one id, WHEN listed, THEN the first keeps it and the other is reported
- GIVEN a profile with a lord layout, WHEN read, THEN its order and off list are taken
- GIVEN a layout file that is not json, WHEN read, THEN it is left out and reported
- GIVEN a layout named after no kind, WHEN read, THEN it is reported
- GIVEN a section file, WHEN read, THEN its words replace the stock text and notes are dropped
- GIVEN append and remove directives, WHEN read, THEN they are taken
- GIVEN a profile and its parent both rewording, WHEN merged, THEN the nearest wins and the rest comes from the parent
- GIVEN a profile and its parent, WHEN a layout is chosen, THEN kind then default then the parents then stock
- GIVEN a missing parent and a loop, WHEN the chain is built, THEN it stops and reports
- GIVEN the same list as last time, WHEN the player picked a profile, THEN that pick is used
- GIVEN a mod added a profile since last time, WHEN resolved, THEN the profile chosen last time stays chosen
- GIVEN the chosen profile is gone, WHEN resolved, THEN it falls back to stock with a notice

### ProvideGoodsPolicyTests.cs

_This is the pure core of the ProvideGoods supply quest (referential increment 2): the LLM writes a free-text category word for what a lord wants, and this policy normalizes it to one of three canonical keys that SupplyItemMatcher then maps to real game items at runtime. Get normalization wrong and the runtime matcher (which switches on these exact string constants) silently never counts a delivered item, stalling a quest the player has genuinely fulfilled. An unrecognized category makes QuestFactory refuse the quest outright (a giver never asks for goods the game cannot verify), so Normalize's null path is a hard gate, not a detail._

- GIVEN a known category word, WHEN normalizing, THEN it maps to the canonical key
- GIVEN an unsupported or empty category, WHEN normalizing, THEN it returns null
- GIVEN a count below or above the band, WHEN clamping, THEN it is held inside
- GIVEN a tally at or over the required count, WHEN testing, THEN it is satisfied
- GIVEN a tally short of the count or a zero requirement, WHEN testing, THEN it is not satisfied

### ProviderModelPolicyTests.cs

_Written from the report, not from the code._

- GIVEN a local server and a model field never touched, WHEN resolving, THEN nothing is sent rather than an id it cannot know
- GIVEN openrouter and the shipped default, WHEN resolving, THEN it is a real choice and is sent
- GIVEN a local server and a model the player actually named, WHEN resolving, THEN it is sent verbatim
- GIVEN a field the player cleared, WHEN resolving, THEN it says nothing on every provider
- GIVEN the shipped default pasted with stray spaces, WHEN resolving, THEN it still counts as untouched
- GIVEN a real choice pasted with stray spaces, WHEN resolving, THEN the id sent is trimmed
- GIVEN nothing at all, WHEN resolving, THEN it says nothing instead of throwing
- GIVEN each provider, WHEN asking whose namespace the ids belong to, THEN only openrouter shares ours

### ProviderPinFailurePolicyTests.cs

_Windmolen (Nexus, 29/09/2026): "Since about one day I am unable to talk to characters... I have tested my connection in game and there appears nothing wrong with it." His log: OpenRouter 404, "No endpoints found for deepseek/deepseek-v3.2._

- GIVEN a pin with no fallbacks and no endpoint left, WHEN explained, THEN the player is told to clear the pin and which model
- GIVEN no pin or fallbacks allowed, WHEN explained, THEN the pin is not blamed
- GIVEN another failure, WHEN explained, THEN it is left to its own message
- GIVEN pinned providers, WHEN the test request is built, THEN it carries the same routing as the chat

### ProviderRolePolicyTests.cs

_Documents the per-role provider routing rules: each model role (Roleplay/Prose, ConsensualIntimate, Cnc, and the ActionInterpreter) may be sent to its OWN provider; a role left on "(default)" (or blank, or an unrecognised value) inherits the global provider; a concrete selection resolves to its own kind; and each ModelMode maps to the role whose provider a turn of that mode should use._

- GIVEN the default sentinel, WHEN resolving a role kind, THEN it inherits the global provider
- GIVEN a blank or null selection, WHEN resolving a role kind, THEN it inherits the global provider
- GIVEN an unrecognised selection, WHEN resolving a role kind, THEN it inherits the global provider
- GIVEN a concrete selection, WHEN resolving a role kind, THEN it uses that kind over the default
- GIVEN each concrete provider string, WHEN parsing, THEN it maps to the matching kind
- GIVEN the default sentinel, WHEN parsing a kind, THEN it returns the fallback
- GIVEN blank or unknown input, WHEN parsing a kind, THEN it returns the fallback

### PublicAffrontPolicyTests.cs

_Protects the "insults travel" tuning: what becomes a public-disgrace rumour, and how far it carries. It breaks in silence — too sensitive and every cross word spreads, too coarse and grave affronts evaporate the moment the conversation closes._

- GIVEN a grave drop before witnesses, WHEN judging, THEN it is a public affront
- GIVEN a drop exactly at the threshold, WHEN judging, THEN it still counts
- GIVEN a drop just short of the threshold, WHEN judging, THEN it does not spread
- GIVEN a grave drop with no one watching, WHEN judging, THEN it does not spread
- GIVEN a positive or neutral delta, WHEN judging, THEN it is never an affront
- GIVEN a small audience, WHEN computing magnitude, THEN it grows with the witnesses
- GIVEN a large audience, WHEN computing magnitude, THEN it caps
- GIVEN the audience size that lands exactly on the cap, WHEN computing magnitude, THEN it is the cap

### QuestEditorParityTests.cs

_The tale editor and the game must agree (docs/design/quest-editor/goal.md, "the one real risk: two validators drifting apart"). The game checks a quest file in C# (AuthoredQuestLoader); the editor checks it in JavaScript, inside Docs/TaleEditor.html, so an author sees the problems before the game does. If the two disagree, the editor says "all good" and the game refuses the tale, or the editor refuses a tale the game would play._

- GIVEN each fixture, WHEN the game reads it, THEN it reports what the fixture expects
- GIVEN each fixture, WHEN the editor checks it, THEN it reports exactly what the game reports
- GIVEN every tale in the repository, WHEN checked by the game and by the editor, THEN they agree
- GIVEN each twine story, WHEN imported, THEN it becomes its expected tale with its notes
- GIVEN each twine story, WHEN imported, THEN the game loads the tale with nothing to report
- GIVEN macros and tags the import cannot map, WHEN imported, THEN each is named to the author
- GIVEN every tale and fixture, WHEN the editor opens and saves it, THEN the game reports the same problems as before
- GIVEN every field the game reads, WHEN the editor lists its forms, THEN none is missing or unknown

### QuestFailureLeniencyPolicyTests.cs

_Guards the no-fault forgiveness for a failed or abandoned BANDIT quest (player report: after searching for a hideout the marker never pointed to, or open-field bandits that were not there, the player returned to say they could not find the base and the giver turned disgusted and hostile). That hostility was a magnitude-25 FailedQuest grudge + a "broken word" memory + a stance/trust hit + full regard loss, all fired for honestly reporting an unfindable target. QuestFailureLeniencyPolicy softens ONLY the hard-to-find bandit class (no grudge, no broken-word memory, no trust spent, a token regard nick), and keeps the full consequence for every other quest type WITHOUT the policy owning those penalty constants (the caller passes its own value in). If this rule drifts, either the hostility returns for bandit failures, or a genuine broken promise (a war deed, a letter, a capture) stops costing the player anything._

- GIVEN a bandit quest, WHEN it fails, THEN it is forgiven
- GIVEN a bandit quest, WHEN it fails, THEN no promise trust is spent
- GIVEN a non bandit quest, WHEN it fails, THEN the full consequence applies
- GIVEN a non bandit quest, WHEN resolving, THEN the callers own penalty is echoed
- GIVEN a quest type, WHEN asked if hard to find, THEN only the bandit deeds qualify

### QuestKillCreditPolicyTests.cs

_Guards the v1.34.2 fault/no-fault hardening (audit Pattern 2, finding 4) for kill-target quests._

- GIVEN an execute enemy quest, WHEN a third party kills the target, THEN it is satisfied
- GIVEN an execute enemy quest, WHEN the player kills the target, THEN it is satisfied
- GIVEN a nemesis bounty, WHEN a third party kills the target, THEN it is NOT satisfied
- GIVEN a nemesis bounty, WHEN the player kills the target, THEN it is satisfied
- GIVEN a non kill target kind, WHEN the target dies by any hand, THEN it is NOT satisfied

### QuestMenuComposerTests.cs

_The prompt-facing text for the viable-quest referential (ratified 2026-07-08): the fix for "the NPC offered a task but no quest was registered" is to stop teaching the LLM 17 quest types in the abstract and instead teach ONLY what a giver can truly hand out right now, real targets named.
