This formatter is the last mile of that promise: get the wording wrong (a missing target, a bad line count) and the LLM either invents a target the factory cannot ground, or the prompt bloats past its budget. MaxEntries caps the line count so a giver with many viable tasks never blows out the prompt._

- GIVEN a grounded entry, WHEN composing, THEN the line names the type the target field and the real target
- GIVEN a targetless entry, WHEN composing, THEN no target parenthesis appears
- GIVEN no viable entries, WHEN composing, THEN the menu is empty so nothing is taught
- GIVEN more entries than the cap, WHEN composing, THEN the menu is truncated to the cap
- GIVEN a null or empty entry, WHEN composing, THEN it is skipped without wasting a cap slot

### QuestMenuTokensTests.cs

_The grounded quest menu (ViableQuestScanner) tells a lord, by type token, which tasks exist for him to give; he copies the token into his [QUEST] block, and the SDK parser turns it into a quest type. A token the parser does not know is a task the lord offers and the game never records: the broken promise the menu exists to prevent._

- GIVEN every task the quest menu can offer, WHEN a lord copies its token, THEN the parser records that task

### QuestReconcilePolicyTests.cs

_The pure decision behind QuestJournalBridge.ReconcileOnSessionLaunch, which runs on every save load and can close a player's active quest-journal entries. The rule exists because of a repeatedly-reported bug: a false cancel on load destroyed a valid, often already-completed quest ("my quest auto-failed on load"). So Decide only ever closes a mirror on POSITIVE proof (a confirmed-dead giver, or a matching quest found terminal); any ambiguity (unresolved reference, unloaded profile, id drift after an update) resolves to Keep, because a lingering entry is a far smaller harm than a destroyed completed quest. ShouldRestoreMirror runs the healing direction: it re-creates a mirror the old bug destroyed while the quest itself survived in the store, without ever duplicating an entry that is already open._

- GIVEN a matching live quest, WHEN deciding, THEN the mirror is kept
- GIVEN an awaiting reward quest that fails the strict match, WHEN deciding, THEN it is still kept not cancelled
- GIVEN the giver is confirmed dead, WHEN deciding, THEN the mirror is closed as giver gone
- GIVEN a dead giver takes precedence even over a live match, WHEN deciding, THEN it closes
- GIVEN a matching terminal quest, WHEN deciding, THEN the mirror is closed as terminal
- GIVEN both a live and a terminal match, WHEN deciding, THEN the live one wins and it is kept
- GIVEN a live quest with a living giver and no open mirror, WHEN checking, THEN the mirror is restored
- GIVEN an open mirror already shows the quest, WHEN checking, THEN nothing is restored
- GIVEN a terminal quest, WHEN checking, THEN no mirror is restored
- GIVEN a dead or unresolved giver, WHEN checking, THEN no mirror is restored

### QuestRecoveryPolicyTests.cs

_QuestRecoveryPolicy turns the number a player types after cr.quests_all into the quest cr.quest_void cancels._

- GIVEN the first and last numbers, WHEN resolving, THEN they map to the first and last index
- GIVEN a number above the count, WHEN resolving, THEN it is refused with the count
- GIVEN a number below one, WHEN resolving, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN a non numeric argument, WHEN resolving, THEN it is refused with a hint
- GIVEN no argument, WHEN resolving, THEN it asks for the number
- GIVEN nothing outstanding, WHEN resolving, THEN it is refused whatever the number

### QuestRescueCreditPolicyTests.cs

_Guards Gabriel's ruling on the "his success cancelled his quest" report (v2.5.3, 2026-09-07): a player who gets a quest target out of a settlement dungeon must have the rescue credited, whatever way the captivity actually ended, except a ransom or a release bundled with a peace treaty (and death, which frees nobody)._

- GIVEN a captive freed by choice, WHEN judging credit, THEN it credits
- GIVEN a captive freed by battle, WHEN judging credit, THEN it credits
- GIVEN a captive who escaped, WHEN judging credit, THEN it credits
- GIVEN a captive freed by compensation, WHEN judging credit, THEN it credits
- GIVEN a ransom or peace release, WHEN judging credit, THEN it does NOT credit
- GIVEN a captive who died, WHEN judging credit, THEN it does NOT credit
- GIVEN an unrecognised future reason, WHEN judging credit, THEN it credits

### QuestRewardGatePolicyTests.cs

_COUNCIL_ACTIONS.md Partie 5 (the "Caladog" case): a king promised the player a fief and marriage to himself as a quest reward, and both evaporated on completion because no executor existed. QuestRewardGatePolicy is the pure gate behind the fix: SAFETY-CRITICAL, since with ModSettings.AllowFiefAndMarriageQuestRewards left at its default (false), NOTHING about today's behavior may change. Every test group below pins one of the three moments the policy guards: emission (never promise what cannot be paid), the toggle-off floor (the feature is fully inert unless explicitly opted into), and payout re-validation (the world may have moved between the promise and the completion, so nothing is ever delivered on stale facts)._

- GIVEN the toggle is off, WHEN a fief promise would otherwise qualify, THEN it is still refused
- GIVEN the toggle is off, WHEN a marriage reward would otherwise qualify, THEN it is still refused
- GIVEN the toggle is off, WHEN a fief payout would otherwise succeed, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the toggle is off, WHEN a marriage reward payout would otherwise succeed, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the giver truly leads their own clan and owns the fief, WHEN the toggle is on, THEN the promise is allowed
- GIVEN the giver does not lead their own clan, WHEN promising a fief, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the named fief is not owned by the givers clan, WHEN promising it, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN every marriage condition holds, WHEN the toggle is on, THEN the promise is allowed
- GIVEN the player already reigns over a realm, WHEN promising a marriage reward, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN the giver has no authority over the named spouse, WHEN promising a marriage reward, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN every fief precondition still holds, WHEN deciding the payout, THEN it delivers
- GIVEN the fief no longer belongs to the givers clan, WHEN deciding the payout, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the giver died before completion, WHEN deciding the fief payout, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the player is now at war with the givers clan, WHEN deciding the fief payout, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN every marriage precondition still holds, WHEN deciding the payout, THEN it delivers
- GIVEN the promised spouse is no longer marriageable, WHEN deciding the payout, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the player became a reigning sovereign since the promise, WHEN deciding the marriage payout, THEN it refuses
- GIVEN the llm proposed no deadline, WHEN clamping a heavy reward, THEN the maximum is used instead of open ended
- GIVEN the llm proposed a deadline beyond the cap, WHEN clamping, THEN it is pulled down to the maximum
- GIVEN the llm proposed a sane deadline, WHEN clamping, THEN its own value is kept

### QuestSaveBasePolicyTests.cs

_This policy decides the save-type BASE ID of the CR quest definer. It exists because Europe1100 / EOE ships a definer at the same base(300000000)+id 1 as ours, so both claim save-id 300000001 and the second mod to load hard-crashes the game at boot. The rule: keep the shipped 300M base when no collider is present (so existing CR-quest saves are byte-for-byte untouched, zero quest loss), and only shift to a distinct base when a collider is loaded (so CR and EOE coexist). What breaks if this is wrong: pick the wrong base with no collider and every existing CR save loses its in-progress quests; fail to detect the collider and the EOE boot crash returns._

- GIVEN no collider, WHEN choosing the base, THEN it is the shipped 300M
- GIVEN a collider, WHEN choosing the base, THEN it shifts to the coexistence base
- GIVEN the coexistence base, WHEN offsetting our three ids, THEN they stay valid positive ints
- GIVEN an assembly name, WHEN asking if it is a known collider, THEN only the EOE ones match

### QuestTargetVoidPolicyTests.cs

_Guards the v1.34.2 fault/no-fault hardening (audit Pattern 2) for hero-bound quests. The daily quest sweep (QuestService.RunDailyMaintenance) only ever handled the GIVER dying and the war premise ending; a capture/attack/execute/rescue/deliver quest whose TARGET hero died or vanished went untouched and later expired with a relation penalty PLUS a FailedQuest grudge, punishing the player for an act of the world._

- GIVEN a hero bound quest, WHEN its target is dead, THEN it voids
- GIVEN a hero bound quest, WHEN its target has vanished, THEN it voids
- GIVEN a hero bound quest, WHEN its target is alive and present, THEN it does NOT void
- GIVEN a rescue, WHEN the target is alive but no longer a prisoner, THEN it voids
- GIVEN a rescue, WHEN the target is alive and still held, THEN it does NOT void
- GIVEN a quest with no named target hero, WHEN judging, THEN it does NOT void
- GIVEN a non hero bound kind, WHEN judging, THEN it does NOT void
- GIVEN an envoys man already free, WHEN swept, THEN the task is voided as freed
- GIVEN a match whose lady or suitor wed another, WHEN swept, THEN it is voided and otherwise stands
- GIVEN a quest type, WHEN asked if hero bound, THEN only the target hero kinds qualify
- GIVEN a war hostile hero quest, WHEN peace is made, THEN it voids
- GIVEN a war hostile hero quest, WHEN the war is still on, THEN it does NOT void
- GIVEN a non hostile hero quest, WHEN peace is made, THEN it does NOT void
- GIVEN a war quest with no named target hero, WHEN peace is made, THEN this rule does NOT void
- GIVEN a quest type, WHEN asked if war hostile, THEN only the strike an enemy kinds qualify
- GIVEN a vanished target, WHEN asked the void reason, THEN it is TargetVanished
- GIVEN a dead target, WHEN asked the void reason, THEN it is TargetDead
- GIVEN a rescue target alive but freed, WHEN asked the void reason, THEN it is RescueTargetFreed
- GIVEN a live reachable target, WHEN asked the void reason, THEN it is None
- GIVEN any case, WHEN comparing ShouldVoid and ReasonToVoid, THEN they agree

### RansomEnvoyPolicyTests.cs

_fkasad (Nexus, 01/10/2026): "He hires you as envoy to negotiate the release of one of his guys... Here is 5000 denars. Bring my lad alive. You can keep the change." Gabriel's ruling (option A): the purse is advanced; if the man goes free some other way first, nobody is at fault and the advance goes back. If these rules are wrong, a lord sends an envoy where the player is at war (that is a rescue), the player keeps a purse for a task that vanished under him, a ransom someone else paid completes the player's errand, or the refund takes more than the player has._

- GIVEN his own man held by a realm the player is at peace with, WHEN asked, THEN he may send the player
- GIVEN the player at war with the captor, WHEN asked, THEN it is a rescue not an envoys errand
- GIVEN a stranger a free man one the player holds or an empty purse, WHEN asked, THEN no envoy is sent
- GIVEN the mans ransom value, WHEN the purse is set, THEN it is half again as much
- GIVEN the player freed a man he held or broke him out, WHEN judged, THEN it is the players deed
- GIVEN a freeing the player had no hand in, WHEN judged, THEN it is not his deed
- GIVEN the task voided through no fault, WHEN the advance is returned, THEN it is the advance up to the purse

### RealmFacesTests.cs

_Queens with a chosen face (Gabriel, 12/10/2026: "une reine par faction", made in the character editor; "une reine par Empire, et on habille la reine du roi"). A faces file says which face the queen of each realm wears; CR dresses the ruler's wife in it and gives a former queen her own face back. If this is wrong: a broken line crashes the face generator or dresses nobody in silence, a mod's face overrides the player's own choice, two women wear one face, or a widowed king's new wife never gets the face while the old queen keeps it forever._

- GIVEN a faces file, WHEN read, THEN each realm has the line the editor copied
- GIVEN a line that is no face or a file that is no json, WHEN read, THEN it is reported and left out
- GIVEN the same realm in several files, WHEN merged, THEN the first read keeps it
- GIVEN a realm with a face and a portrait, WHEN read, THEN both are kept and a face alone still reads
- GIVEN a portrait outside the faces folder or no picture, WHEN read, THEN it is reported and the face alone is kept
- GIVEN a queen not yet dressed, WHEN planned, THEN she is dressed and a dressed one is left alone
- GIVEN a new queen, WHEN planned, THEN she is dressed and the former queen gets her face back
- GIVEN no queen or no face any more, WHEN planned, THEN nobody is dressed and the face is given back

### RealmServiceWordingTests.cs

_Documents how the world records the player's clan entering a realm's service. tashmetu, 25/09/2026: hiring on as a mercenary was recorded as an oath of fealty, the chronicle said the player "bent the knee", and a king told the hired sellsword "I've heard word you swore fealty". What characters hear here is what they will say back, so a wrong word is a wrong thing said to the player's face._

- GIVEN the player hires on as a mercenary, WHEN the world records it, THEN no oath is spoken of
- GIVEN the player swears to a realm, WHEN the world records it, THEN it is fealty
- GIVEN a mercenary contract, WHEN its reach is set, THEN it carries less than an oath and no less than its end

### RealmTidingsPolicyTests.cs

_Guards the subject-selection at the heart of the realm-tidings letter: a friend writes unbidden to RELAY a real world event, never to invent one. Three promises rest on this pure pick and all break in silence if it drifts: the per-NPC non-repeat (an NPC must never relay the same event twice, or the player gets the same news on loop from one friend), the no-fake-news floor (the policy may only ever hand back a real known event, never conjure one, so returning null when nothing qualifies is a feature not a gap), and the no-trivial-spam floor (gossip below a salience worth a friend's ink must be left unsaid). The tie-break must stay deterministic or the same inputs could relay different events run to run, which no test could then pin._

- GIVEN several worth telling events, WHEN selecting, THEN the most salient is relayed
- GIVEN the most salient event already sent, WHEN selecting, THEN the next fresh event is relayed
- GIVEN every candidate already sent, WHEN selecting, THEN null is returned
- GIVEN all candidates below the salience floor, WHEN selecting, THEN null is returned
- GIVEN a candidate exactly at the salience floor, WHEN selecting, THEN it qualifies
- GIVEN two candidates tied on salience, WHEN selecting, THEN the ordinal lowest event id wins
- GIVEN null or empty inputs, WHEN selecting, THEN null is returned and it never throws

### RealmTidingsSalienceTests.cs

_Guards the salience score that ranks which known world event a friend relays first. It folds an event's magnitude (how far the news carries) with how surely the writer knows it (RumorConfidence) into one int that RealmTidingsPolicy.SelectSubject sorts on. Two promises rest on it: magnitude must still carry the news (a looming war outranks an idle rumour), and confidence must genuinely bite (a confirmed report is worth more ink than a doubtful whisper of the same size, and a faint whisper of a trivial thing must sink below the policy's no-trivial-spam floor so it is left unsaid). It also runs on live, sometimes-malformed data every weekly tick, so a zero or negative magnitude must degrade quietly, never throw._

- GIVEN same confidence, WHEN magnitude is higher, THEN salience is higher
- GIVEN same magnitude, WHEN confidence rises, THEN salience rises
- GIVEN well known confidence, WHEN scoring, THEN the full magnitude counts
- GIVEN a faint whisper of a trivial event, WHEN scoring, THEN it falls below the spam floor
- GIVEN a confirmed sizable event, WHEN scoring, THEN it clears the spam floor
- GIVEN a confirmed mid event and a faint bigger one, WHEN scoring, THEN confidence can win
- GIVEN zero or negative magnitude, WHEN scoring, THEN it returns zero and never throws

### ReasoningWirePolicyTests.cs

_Documents which reasoning-effort wire shape each provider kind expects: OpenRouter's nested "reasoning" object, or the top-level "reasoning_effort" string. LlmServices reads this at three sites (the main chat client, the interpreter client, and the verbose diagnostic log) - a wrong answer here silently drifts one of those sites from the other two, or sends a provider a shape it ignores._

- GIVEN openrouter, WHEN asking the wire shape, THEN it does not use the effort string
- GIVEN openai compatible, WHEN asking the wire shape, THEN it uses the effort string
- GIVEN nanogpt, WHEN asking the wire shape, THEN it uses the effort string

### RebellionWordingTests.cs

_FloatingMammal (Nexus, 02/10/2026): a lady of a house that rose against the Southern Empire, once received back, claimed she had fought on the Empire's side; she was not lying, she had never been told. Her house's members now remember the rising and the return. If these words are wrong, a former rebel again speaks as a loyalist, or the memory names the wrong realm, or the return reads as though the rising never happened._

- GIVEN a house that rose, WHEN its member remembers, THEN she knows she was a rebel and against whom
- GIVEN a rising with no banner, WHEN remembered, THEN it is still a rebellion
- GIVEN a house received back, WHEN its member remembers, THEN the rising is not erased

### RecentMatterWordingTests.cs

_How the matter a character comes to raise is quoted in the "THIS MEETING" block (you sought the player out). fkasad (Nexus, 05/10/2026): Dorin sought Bardin out about being freed by him, and from his first line said HE had freed Bardin._

- GIVEN a lived memory written to the player, WHEN the character comes to raise it, THEN whose you is whose is said
- GIVEN a rumour about the player, WHEN raised, THEN it is given plainly
- GIVEN no player name, WHEN a lived memory is raised, THEN you is still said to be the player

### RecruitIntentDetectorTests.cs

_Documents the gate that decides when the NPC is asked where to FIND a companion, so the host only then injects the real "wanderers for hire" facts. The flagship case is the reported "Oros" bug: the player asked where to find a companion and the NPC invented one because nothing grounded the answer._

- GIVEN the reported line, WHEN detecting, THEN it reads as a recruiting question
- GIVEN various recruiting phrasings, WHEN detecting, THEN each is caught
- GIVEN an ordinary message, WHEN detecting, THEN it is not a recruiting question
- GIVEN empty or whitespace, WHEN detecting, THEN it is never a match
- GIVEN mixed casing, WHEN detecting, THEN the match is case insensitive

### RecruitTroopsPolicyTests.cs

_Lordfadooboo (Nexus, 01/10/2026): "recruitment quests". A lord asks for fresh recruits of his own people. If these rules are wrong, the player hands over veterans he trained (or his companions) and calls them recruits, a Battanian lord takes Imperial levies as his own people, or a lord asks for more men than his party can take._

- GIVEN a raw soldier of the culture asked, WHEN counted, THEN he is a recruit
- GIVEN a veteran a hero or another people, WHEN counted, THEN he is not a recruit
- GIVEN no culture asked, WHEN counted, THEN any raw soldier counts
- GIVEN a proposed number, WHEN the ask is set, THEN it is the garrison band against the room in his party

### RefusalFallbackPolicyTests.cs

_Documents when a REFUSED beat is redone on the NSFW model. Gabriel (04/10/2026), playing the werewolf tale's scene in Enhanced mode: the narrator answered an explicit turn with the model's own safety refusal ("I cannot fulfill this request...") and the player had to switch the in-chat model to NSFW by hand. The MCM hint promises the NSFW model is "the fallback in Enhanced"; until then that fallback only caught a TRUNCATED beat, never a refused one, and the scene narrator had no fallback at all._

- GIVEN enhanced mode and a distinct nsfw model, WHEN the beat is a refusal, THEN it is redone on the nsfw model
- GIVEN enhanced mode, WHEN the beat is ordinary prose that finished cleanly, THEN it is not redone
- GIVEN enhanced mode, WHEN the beat was filtered cut off or empty, THEN it is redone
- GIVEN a full reply with no finish reason, WHEN checked, THEN it is not redone
- GIVEN the player chose roleplay or nsfw, WHEN the beat is a refusal, THEN it is not redone
- GIVEN no distinct nsfw model, WHEN the beat is a refusal, THEN it is not redone

### RelationGateTests.cs

_This is CalradiaRemembers.Logic's core anti-exploit rule: the LLM proposes a relation delta after every exchange, and RelationGate is the only thing standing between that proposal and the number BannerlordGameStateBridge actually applies to Hero relation. Get it wrong and players can talk their way to max relation, the exact failure this mod exists to avoid._

- GIVEN a generous positive proposal on a fresh npc, WHEN resolving, THEN a routine gain is capped to one and the tier rises
- GIVEN repeated warm moments in one conversation, WHEN resolving, THEN each tier costs one more moment
- GIVEN a tier, WHEN asking the moments required, THEN it is that tier plus one
- GIVEN a single moment a few hours after the tier rose, WHEN resolving, THEN it is still gated
- GIVEN two full decay windows of absence, WHEN resolving, THEN the resistance has cooled and a moment lands
- GIVEN almost but not quite a decay window, WHEN resolving, THEN the tier has not relaxed yet
- GIVEN the cooldown turned off, WHEN resolving, THEN warmth never becomes dearer
- GIVEN the cooldown turned off, WHEN resolving at once, THEN a deep bond still deepens
- GIVEN the default cooldown, WHEN a deep bond resolves, THEN it still resists as it always did
- GIVEN the cooldown turned off, WHEN a single exchange resolves, THEN it is still only one point
- GIVEN a moderate insult, WHEN resolving, THEN the full negative applies immediately
- GIVEN a null profile, WHEN resolving, THEN nothing moves
- GIVEN a zero delta, WHEN resolving, THEN nothing moves and no moment accrues
- GIVEN a savage insult in ordinary conversation, WHEN resolving, THEN it stops at the conversational floor
- GIVEN the same cruelty toward a captive, WHEN resolving, THEN the deeper captive floor is unlocked
- GIVEN an extreme proposal against a captive, WHEN resolving, THEN it still stops at the captive floor
- GIVEN an allowed intimacy moment, WHEN resolving, THEN it grants three and leaves the routine tier untouched
- GIVEN a disallowed intimacy tag on a fresh npc, WHEN resolving, THEN it lands only the routine point
- GIVEN a disallowed intimacy tag at a raised tier, WHEN resolving, THEN it only banks a moment
- GIVEN a second allowed intimacy within its own cooldown, WHEN resolving, THEN it is demoted to a routine moment
- GIVEN an allowed intimacy after its own cooldown, WHEN resolving, THEN it grants three again

### RelationSummaryTextTests.cs

_The chat header's line of standings. Fakade (Nexus, 06/10/2026), with True Relations (each noble has a relation of their own with the player): "I'm talking to a person that is +10 relation with me, but the conversation window has them at -61, because that's my relation with the clan leader." Since 2.7.0 the character is TOLD their own relation apart from the clan's; the header still showed only the clan and CR's regard, so the player could not see the number the character was working from. It is shown when it differs, and only then._

- GIVEN a character whose own relation differs from their clans, WHEN the header is written, THEN it shows their own too
- GIVEN no relation of their own, WHEN the header is written, THEN it is as before

### ReplyCourierGatePolicyTests.cs

_Documents the one gate deciding whether a rider can be on the road to the player (courier robustness batch, ROADMAP 2026-07-20). Three world states block the ride, and every blocked case resolves the same safe way: the letter stays on date-based delivery, so only the visible ride is skipped, never the letter itself._

- GIVEN an ashore free player with a road, WHEN gated, THEN the courier may ride
- GIVEN a player at sea, WHEN gated, THEN the ride is blocked as at sea
- GIVEN a captive player, WHEN gated, THEN the ride is blocked as prisoner
- GIVEN no land route to the player, WHEN gated, THEN the ride is blocked as no road
- GIVEN several blocks at once, WHEN gated, THEN sea outranks prisoner which outranks the road

### RescueEvidenceTests.cs

_A completed rescue task records what happened, and the giver reads it back. If this is wrong, a lord whose kinsman the player broke out of a town's dungeon thanks him for "freeing him from captivity", as if a battle had done it._

- GIVEN the player broke him out of a named prison, WHEN recorded, THEN the prison is named
- GIVEN any other freeing, WHEN recorded, THEN the plain line stands

### RescueFromFoePolicyTests.cs

_zhenja1 (Nexus, 10/10/2026): "I liberated two from Garios, and both are saying they were never prisoner." A lord the player takes from an enemy who held them, and lets go, was rescued (Gabriel), and remembers who held them. If these rules are wrong, the freed lords deny their captivity, a prisoner of the player's own is thanked for a pardon, or the memory blames a realm and never names the lord who held them._

- GIVEN a lord taken from a beaten foe, WHEN the player lets them go, THEN it is a rescue
- GIVEN a prisoner the player captured themselves, WHEN let go, THEN it is no rescue
- GIVEN a lord kept past the window, WHEN let go, THEN it is no longer a rescue
- GIVEN the lord who held them, WHEN the rescue is remembered, THEN that lord is named
- GIVEN no lord to name, WHEN the rescue is remembered, THEN the place or realm stands in

### RetainerLeavePolicyTests.cs

_RetainerSpikeBehavior calls RetainerLeavePolicy.MustLeave from BOTH its WarDeclared hook and its daily safety check to decide whether a foreign lord riding in the player's party must break off and return to their own clan. These tests pin the STAKE the spike itself was set: the author's flagship rule is that a WAR between the retainer's faction and the player forces them to leave, and a dead retainer must never linger as a stale tracked id. If this gate is wrong, an enemy lord keeps riding inside the very army they now field against, or a corpse stays "in the party" forever - the two exact failures the rule exists to prevent._

- GIVEN a tracked retainer alive and at peace, WHEN checked, THEN they stay
- GIVEN the retainers faction is now at war with the player, WHEN checked, THEN they must leave
- GIVEN a tracked retainer has died, WHEN checked, THEN they must leave even at peace
- GIVEN the hero is not tracked as a retainer, WHEN checked, THEN nothing must happen
- GIVEN a dead hero who is not tracked, WHEN checked, THEN still nothing must happen

### RideWithMeEligibilityPolicyTests.cs

_when an idle lord or lady will ride inside the player's own party for a time, keeping their own clan, and the many ways they will not._

- GIVEN an idle lord who esteems the player, WHEN asked, THEN he rides along
- GIVEN he leads his own party, WHEN asked, THEN that is follow me s business not this one
- GIVEN the hero is no lord at all, WHEN asked, THEN the offer does not apply to them
- GIVEN he is dead or in a cell, WHEN asked, THEN he rides nowhere
- GIVEN he is already of the player s own clan, WHEN asked, THEN there is nothing to arrange
- GIVEN their two realms are at war, WHEN asked, THEN he will not ride inside that army
- GIVEN he already rides as a retainer, WHEN asked, THEN he will not promise it twice
- GIVEN the bond is one short of what he asks, WHEN asked, THEN he declines until it reaches it
- GIVEN his own realm is at war, WHEN asked, THEN he stays available for the muster
- GIVEN nothing could be read about him, WHEN asked, THEN nobody rides

### RoadAudienceSafetyPolicyTests.cs

_when a travelling companion may interrupt the player to ask for a private word, and, far more often, when they may not._

- GIVEN free travel on the open road, WHEN gating the road audience, THEN the offer may fire
- GIVEN a scene is loaded, WHEN gating the road audience, THEN nobody interrupts it
- GIVEN a conversation is already under way, WHEN gating, THEN nobody talks over it
- GIVEN the player is somebody s prisoner, WHEN gating, THEN no private word is possible
- GIVEN the player is inside a settlement, WHEN gating, THEN the road path leaves it alone
- GIVEN the player is committed to something on the map, WHEN gating, THEN the offer waits
- GIVEN a campaign menu is open, WHEN gating, THEN nothing is raised over it
- GIVEN the mod is already running a conversation, WHEN gating, THEN it does not start another
- GIVEN the player is sailing, WHEN gating, THEN no companion asks for a private word
- GIVEN nothing could be read about the moment, WHEN gating, THEN the offer is withheld

### RoleplayBenchChecksTests.cs

- GIVEN all distinct npc lines, WHEN counting repeats, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN a line repeated with different case and spacing, WHEN counting repeats, THEN it counts as one
- GIVEN the same line three times, WHEN counting repeats, THEN it counts as two
- GIVEN blank lines among the npc lines, WHEN counting repeats, THEN blanks are not repeats
- GIVEN a null line list, WHEN counting repeats, THEN it is zero
- GIVEN an out of fiction refusal, WHEN scanning, THEN it is flagged
- GIVEN a refusal in mixed case, WHEN scanning, THEN it is still flagged
- GIVEN an in character reply, WHEN scanning, THEN it is not flagged
- GIVEN the real false positive lines from the log, WHEN scanning, THEN they are not flagged
- GIVEN a provider moderation block, WHEN scanning, THEN it is flagged
- GIVEN high risk used in character, WHEN scanning, THEN it is not flagged
- GIVEN empty text, WHEN scanning for refusal, THEN it is not flagged
- GIVEN each published refusal phrase, WHEN scanning, THEN every one is flagged
- GIVEN the real fade to black line from the log, WHEN scanning, THEN it is flagged
- GIVEN an explicit present tense depiction, WHEN scanning, THEN it is not flagged
- GIVEN empty text, WHEN scanning for fade to black, THEN it is not flagged
- GIVEN each published fade marker, WHEN scanning, THEN every one is flagged
- GIVEN the calradian plural gods, WHEN scanning, THEN it is not flagged
- GIVEN the word romantic, WHEN scanning, THEN roman does not match inside it
- GIVEN a real world place as a whole word, WHEN scanning, THEN it is flagged
- GIVEN the phrase oh my god, WHEN scanning, THEN it is flagged
- GIVEN each published earth term, WHEN scanning, THEN every one is flagged

### RomanticPreferenceHonourDecouplingTests.cs

_which romantic preferences a character's traits and CULTURE make plausible, the pool a new NPC profile rolls from once and then keeps for the rest of the campaign._

- GIVEN a culture that tolerates shared partners, WHEN the character is honourable, THEN polyamory is still plausible
- GIVEN a strictly monogamous culture, WHEN the character is dishonourable, THEN polyamory is not offered
- GIVEN a tolerant culture, WHEN the character is below its elite tier, THEN polyamory is not offered
- GIVEN no culture is known, WHEN building the pool, THEN it falls back to monogamy
- GIVEN a dishonourable character, WHEN they are otherwise giving, THEN casual is not offered
- GIVEN a character who keeps their distance, WHEN they are honourable, THEN casual is still plausible
- GIVEN honour, WHEN building the pool, THEN it still decides how exclusive the character is

### RomanticProfilePolicyTests.cs

- GIVEN a count larger than the pool, WHEN picking distinct entries, THEN it is clamped to the pool size
- GIVEN a pool larger than the count, WHEN picking distinct entries, THEN no duplicates are returned
- GIVEN a roll of 65, WHEN determining orientation, THEN it is bi curious
- GIVEN a roll of 89, WHEN determining orientation, THEN it is still bi curious
- GIVEN a roll of 90, WHEN determining orientation, THEN it is bisexual
- GIVEN a roll of 96, WHEN determining kinks, THEN two kinks are rolled
- GIVEN a roll of 97, WHEN determining kinks, THEN three kinks are rolled
- GIVEN a roll under 60, WHEN determining kinks, THEN no kink is rolled
- GIVEN a roll under 65, WHEN determining orientation, THEN it is heterosexual
- GIVEN high calculating, WHEN building the kink pool, THEN it gains control and ownership kinks
- GIVEN high honor, WHEN building the kink pool, THEN it gains breeding and praise
- GIVEN high mercy, WHEN building the kink pool, THEN it gains submission leaning kinks
- GIVEN low honor, WHEN building the kink pool, THEN it gains power imbalance and corruption
- GIVEN low mercy, WHEN building the kink pool, THEN it gains dominance and sadism leaning kinks
- GIVEN neutral traits, WHEN building the kink pool, THEN only the base pool is present
- GIVEN the same seed, WHEN building a full profile twice, THEN every roll matches

### RoundTableControllerTests.cs

_Covers the round-table controller's pure decision state (RoundTableGate), which the mod's RoundTableController composes. RoundTableController itself and its RunAsync lap live in the net472 mod project (engine-bound: ChatSpeaker/Hero, NpcMemoryCampaignBehavior, EncounterContextBuilder, ChatManager), which this net10 test project cannot reference, so the lap sequencing is exercised in game rather than here. The gates below use the real RoundTableIntentDetector._

- GIVEN a group present and a live conversation, WHEN computing visibility, THEN the button shows
- GIVEN no witness or a captive scene or an ended talk, WHEN computing visibility, THEN it is hidden
- GIVEN a group opener, WHEN the main npc replies, THEN the floor opens exactly once
- GIVEN a group opener, WHEN the speaker is not the main npc, THEN the floor does not open
- GIVEN a group opener, WHEN the scene is captive or the talk ended, THEN the floor does not open
- GIVEN an ordinary message, WHEN the main npc replies, THEN the floor stays closed
- GIVEN a group opener but no witnesses present, WHEN the main npc replies, THEN the floor stays closed
- GIVEN a group opener inside a captive scene, WHEN the main npc replies, THEN the floor stays closed
- GIVEN a later ordinary message, WHEN it follows a group opener, THEN it disarms the pending request
- GIVEN the rotation start, WHEN set, THEN it round trips

### RoundTableIntentDetectorTests.cs

_Documents the free-text trigger for opening the floor to the whole group, as opposed to addressing one person (which routes through AddressedSpeakerDetector instead). A false positive here would derail an ordinary one-on-one line (or one naming a witness) into a group burst nobody asked for; a false negative means the player's explicit "what do you all think?" silently does nothing, since RoundTableGate.OnPlayerSend only arms on a Detects() hit._

- GIVEN a message opening the floor to the group, WHEN detecting, THEN it is a round table
- GIVEN a message addressing one person or lacking a cue, WHEN detecting, THEN it is not a round table
- GIVEN a cue buried inside a longer word, WHEN detecting, THEN it is not a round table
- GIVEN the same cues as whole words, WHEN detecting, THEN the floor still opens
- GIVEN a null empty or whitespace message, WHEN detecting, THEN it is not a round table

### RoundTablePolicyTests.cs

_Documents the pure round-robin arithmetic a round-table burst runs on: which participant indices get a turn this burst (SpeakerOrder), and where the next burst should resume (NextStartIndex) so that, over repeated triggers, everyone in a group larger than the per-burst cap eventually speaks instead of the same few voices answering every time. Consumed by the mod's RoundTableController.RunAsync, which spawns one LLM turn per returned index. Get the wrap/advance math wrong and either a turn indexes past the participant list, or the rotation stalls and never reaches participants beyond the cap._

- GIVEN three participants from the start, WHEN ordering a full cap, THEN each speaks once in turn
- GIVEN two participants and a larger cap, WHEN ordering, THEN it wraps into cross talk
- GIVEN four participants from the start, WHEN ordering a cap of three, THEN only the first three speak
- GIVEN four participants from the third, WHEN ordering, THEN it round robins and wraps to zero
- GIVEN no participants, WHEN ordering, THEN the order is empty
- GIVEN a non positive cap, WHEN ordering, THEN the order is empty
- GIVEN a burst, WHEN computing the next start, THEN it advances so everyone eventually speaks
- GIVEN a council roster, WHEN capping the lap, THEN small tables speak in full and large ones are bounded

### RumorAwarenessBreakdownTests.cs

_Documents the account cr.heard prints for "has this character heard this event". A bridge author (tashmetu, 23/09/2026) asked for a way to check it "short of just going and talking to them"; the answer is read by a modder deciding whether their bridge is broken, so a wrong verdict or a wrong lever sends them debugging the wrong thing. The arithmetic itself is pinned in RumorAwarenessTests; this pins what is SAID about it._

- GIVEN a heard event, WHEN described, THEN it says heard and how many days remain
- GIVEN an event heard once but now too old, WHEN described, THEN it says faded and when
- GIVEN an event this observer could never hear, WHEN described, THEN it names every unused lever
- GIVEN a never heard event already tagged with their realm, WHEN described, THEN it does not advise tagging it

### RumorAwarenessTests.cs

_Protects the awareness model — who plausibly knows what, and how surely (distortion with distance) — the logic behind rumour propagation and the "WHAT YOU'VE HEARD" hedging._

- GIVEN a fresh nearby in faction event, WHEN scored, THEN it is well known
- GIVEN a faint distant out of faction rumour, WHEN scored, THEN it is not known
- GIVEN a mid event, WHEN it touches the observers faction, THEN it crosses into known
- GIVEN a major event far away, WHEN scored fresh, THEN its magnitude carries it
- GIVEN an event older than the recency window, WHEN scored, THEN the score is zero
- GIVEN an event halfway through the recency window, WHEN scored, THEN reach is halved
- GIVEN a high score, WHEN rated, THEN it is well known
- GIVEN a middling score, WHEN rated, THEN it is secondhand
- GIVEN a score just over the threshold, WHEN rated, THEN it is a distant rumour
- GIVEN a score at the threshold, WHEN asked, THEN the observer knows it
- GIVEN an unnamed observer, WHEN explained, THEN the score is the plain awareness score
- GIVEN a faint fresh event, WHEN the observer is named in it, THEN the flat bonus carries it over the threshold
- GIVEN news past the recency window, WHEN the observer is named, THEN the bonus alone cannot carry it
- GIVEN a realm tagged magnitude 60 event, WHEN its fade day is asked, THEN its own people hold it sixteen days
- GIVEN a faint event naming the observer, WHEN its fade day is asked, THEN they hold it six days
- GIVEN an untagged magnitude 30 event, WHEN its fade day is asked, THEN nobody ever hears it

### RumorDiscretionTests.cs

_IdentityCrysys (Nexus, 03/10/2026): "is there a way to remove a world event from public memory and/or a specific NPC's awareness? For example, preventing a certain character from being aware of a marriage to a different character? Or preventing a certain event from coming up in conversation with anyone (except who it involves directly, maybe)?" A world event is heard by score (magnitude, realm, place, age), never by a stored list, so the player had no say. Now an event can be kept to those it names, or hidden from one character. If these rules are wrong, a secret wedding is gossiped in every hall, or one of the newlyweds forgets her own wedding._

- GIVEN an ordinary event, WHEN anyone asks, THEN nothing is withheld
- GIVEN a secret event, WHEN a stranger or one of those named asks, THEN only the named know
- GIVEN a secret event, WHEN a town is asked, THEN it is withheld
- GIVEN an event hidden from one character, WHEN she and another ask, THEN only she is kept unaware
- GIVEN a secret event, WHEN its line is tagged, THEN it asks for discretion
- GIVEN a secret with a confidant, WHEN the confidant and a stranger ask, THEN only the confidant knows
- GIVEN an ordinary event, WHEN its line is tagged, THEN nothing is added

### ScatteredLootPolicyTests.cs

_Scattered-loot recovery (ratified 2026-07-24, AUDIT-NEMESIS M1): a fled bandit escape scatters the player's gear by culture, and defeating a band of that culture has a CHANCE to return the OLDEST-lost matching piece._

- GIVEN a roll below the chance, WHEN checking recovery, THEN it recovers and the boundary holds
- GIVEN a negative roll, WHEN checking recovery, THEN it never recovers
- GIVEN a defeated culture, WHEN selecting, THEN only that cultures scattered item is returned
- GIVEN several matching pieces, WHEN selecting, THEN the oldest loss comes back first
- GIVEN a culture cased differently, WHEN selecting, THEN it still matches
- GIVEN no matching culture, WHEN selecting, THEN nothing is returned

### ScenarioArgsTests.cs

_Documents the target:delta payload grammar used by consequence keywords like CHANGE_RELATION:Rhagaea:+5, and the target:rest grammar used by INJECT_MEMORY:Ref:text. These strings come straight out of a modder's hand-typed ModuleData/scenarios/*.json (or the composer app's export); ScenarioConsequenceExecutor calls straight into these parsers before ever touching game state. A wrong split silently no-ops an authored consequence (logged "bad payload", never applied); a parser that throws instead of returning false would turn a modder's typo into a campaign-breaking exception. So the grammar's edge cases are pinned here._

- GIVEN a target and a positive delta, WHEN parsing, THEN both are returned
- GIVEN a negative delta, WHEN parsing, THEN the sign is kept
- GIVEN a zero delta, WHEN parsing, THEN it parses
- GIVEN no colon, WHEN parsing, THEN it fails
- GIVEN a trailing colon or a non integer, WHEN parsing, THEN it fails
- GIVEN a target and free text, WHEN parsing target rest, THEN the first colon splits
- GIVEN free text with further colons, WHEN parsing target rest, THEN only the first colon splits
- GIVEN no colon or empty rest, WHEN parsing target rest, THEN it fails
- GIVEN signed integers, WHEN parsing, THEN a leading plus is accepted

### ScenarioPackSourcesTests.cs

_Decides where scenario packs are read from and which rule keeps an id when two packs share one. Gabriel (11/10/2026) asked that mods can ship scenarios, as they ship tales. If the folders are wrong, a player's pack is wiped by an update or a mod's pack is never read; if two rules keep the same id, firing one seals the other in the save (seals are kept by id alone), so a mod's rule could silently switch off one of CR's._

- GIVEN cr the custom folder and two mods, WHEN folders are listed, THEN cr then custom then each mods cr scenarios in launcher order
- GIVEN calradia remembers among the enabled mods, WHEN folders are listed, THEN its packs are read once
- GIVEN a repeated or blank module, WHEN folders are listed, THEN each mod is read once and blanks are skipped
- GIVEN a mod, WHEN its folder is listed, THEN it is cr scenarios
- GIVEN two packs sharing a rule id, WHEN merged, THEN the first keeps it and the later one is reported
- GIVEN a missing rule, WHEN merged, THEN it is skipped

### ScenarioPassTests.cs

_Decides which rules a fired event plays. Gabriel (11/10/2026): "Si plusieurs regles existent pour la mort de Rhagaea, tous devraient etre joues", e.g. one that starts a war and another where a bastard heir claims the throne; and guards must protect the game by checking that its state allows the scenario. Before, ONE rule was drawn among all eligible ones, so a mod's rule could take the place of CR's. Now every eligible rule plays, in reading order, each checked at its own turn (it sees what the rules before it changed); rules sharing a GROUP are alternatives (the four outcomes of marrying Rhagaea), one drawn by weight; and a rule plays only when ALL its consequences can apply, so the realm is never told of a war that was not declared. If this is wrong, scenarios vanish, contradict each other, or lie._

- GIVEN two rules for the same death, WHEN she dies, THEN both are played in reading order
- GIVEN alternatives in a group and a rule outside it, WHEN the event fires, THEN one alternative and the other rule play
- GIVEN weighted alternatives, WHEN the roll passes the first weight, THEN the second is played
- GIVEN an alternative whose guard fails, WHEN the group draws, THEN only the eligible ones are drawn
- GIVEN a rule one of whose consequences cannot apply, WHEN the event fires, THEN it does not play and is not sealed
- GIVEN a rule with a message and no consequence, WHEN the event fires, THEN it plays
- GIVEN a rule whose guard the previous rule breaks, WHEN the event fires, THEN the second sees the change and does not play
- GIVEN a sealed one shot and a repeatable rule, WHEN the event fires, THEN only the repeatable one plays
- GIVEN a group with no eligible alternative, WHEN the event fires, THEN it plays nothing and the next rule still plays
- GIVEN a rule on another trigger, WHEN a death fires, THEN it does not play
- GIVEN rules that play, WHEN the event fires, THEN the played rules are returned
- GIVEN blank groups, WHEN rules are read, THEN they are not grouped together

### ScenarioReferenceMatchTests.cs

_Decides which hero or kingdom a scenario rule means when it names one ("empire_s", "lord_1_14", "Rhagaea")._

- GIVEN an id that another name contains, WHEN the name comes first, THEN the exact id wins
- GIVEN an exact name and a longer name containing it, WHEN matching, THEN the exact name wins
- GIVEN a short name, WHEN only a longer name contains it, THEN that candidate is named
- GIVEN two equally good matches, WHEN matching, THEN the first listed wins
- GIVEN a reference no candidate matches, WHEN matching, THEN none is named
- GIVEN a blank reference, WHEN matching, THEN none is named
- GIVEN an id in another case, WHEN ranking, THEN it is an exact id

### ScenarioRolesTests.cs

_The tale-scenario bridge (docs/design/tale-scenario-bridge; Gabriel, 12/10/2026): a scenario rule answering a tale names the tale's people by role ({husband}, {giver}, {player}), because they are chosen when the tale begins, never known when the pack is written. And a husband who does not rule "presses his king toward war" ("rancune et pousse son roi vers la guerre"), the king deciding as the leader always does. If these are wrong, a rule names a stranger, the realm's news reads "{husband}" to the player, or a king goes to war for any vassal's grievance, or never._

- GIVEN a reference in braces, WHEN read, THEN it names a tale role and anything else does not
- GIVEN a rule message naming roles, WHEN told, THEN the names are filled in
- GIVEN the husband rules, WHEN he presses for war, THEN war is declared
- GIVEN a king who holds the husband dear and can fight, WHEN pressed, THEN he declares
- GIVEN what a leader weighs, WHEN it goes against war, THEN the king declines

### ScenarioRuleTests.cs

_Documents the rule's four fields (+ fire-once, weight) and that ScenarioRule.Parse turns the raw DSL strings a JSON pack carries into tokens. The weight clamp is a small but load-bearing guarantee for the weighted draw, so it is pinned here._

- GIVEN raw dsl strings, WHEN parsing a rule, THEN trigger guards and consequences become tokens
- GIVEN no guards or consequences, WHEN parsing, THEN they default to empty not null
- GIVEN blank lines among consequences, WHEN parsing, THEN they are skipped
- GIVEN a non positive weight, WHEN building a rule, THEN it is clamped to one
- GIVEN defaults, WHEN building a rule, THEN it is repeatable with weight one

### ScenarioSelectorTests.cs

_The keystone: trigger matching (incl. wildcard), one-shot exclusion, guard filtering, and the weighted random pool that makes "several rules on one trigger → one fires" — replayability with no branching. The RNG and the guard/fired facts are injected, so each path is deterministic here. The final test documents the flagship Rhagaea marriage as four rules on a single trigger._

- GIVEN same verb and payload, WHEN matching, THEN it matches case insensitively
- GIVEN a wildcard rule payload, WHEN matching any payload of the same verb, THEN it matches
- GIVEN a different payload, WHEN matching, THEN it does not match
- GIVEN a rule naming a tale, WHEN one of its steps fires, THEN it answers and a rule naming the step answers only it
- GIVEN no rule matches the event, WHEN selecting, THEN it returns null
- GIVEN a spent one shot, WHEN selecting, THEN it is excluded
- GIVEN a guard that fails, WHEN selecting, THEN the rule is excluded
- GIVEN a single eligible rule, WHEN selecting, THEN it is returned without consulting the rng
- GIVEN three equal weight rules, WHEN the rng points at each slot, THEN that rule is drawn
- GIVEN weighted rules, WHEN drawing, THEN the weight widens a rules slot range
- GIVEN the four rhagaea marriage rules, WHEN the player does not rule, THEN the two crowns rule is filtered out
- GIVEN the four rhagaea marriage rules, WHEN the player rules, THEN all four are eligible and one is drawn

### SceneChoiceMenuTests.cs

_Branching tales: the numbered list of a scene's ways out (Gabriel, 04/10/2026: "like an LLM does in the CLI: Choice 1, Choice 2, Chat about that"). This decides what the chat lists under the narration, and what a typed number means; its answer feeds the list in both chat skins and the line sent to the narrator. If it is wrong, the player is offered a way that does not exist for them, a typed "1" takes a path they never saw listed first, a number meant as words is swallowed, or a click moves the story without the narrator ever telling it._

- GIVEN open ways under a fresh narration, WHEN listed, THEN they are numbered in order and end with something else
- GIVEN the player answered the narrator is writing or the scene is over, WHEN listed, THEN nothing is shown
- GIVEN a scene with no open way, WHEN listed, THEN nothing is shown
- GIVEN a way closed by its flag, WHEN listed, THEN it is not offered
- GIVEN the first declared way closed, WHEN 1 is typed, THEN it sends the first open way
- GIVEN a number with no way behind it or among words, WHEN typed, THEN it goes as typed
- GIVEN no list shown, WHEN a number is typed, THEN it goes as typed
- GIVEN a way clicked, WHEN its words are sent, THEN the story moves only on the narrators marker

### SceneDecisionMergeTests.cs

_Lufaine (Nexus, 30/09/2026): "the speak privately function doesn't work most of the time. Whether through requesting privacy through dialogue, or the button at the top of the chat, the npc will agree, emote a private departure, but the audience is still there listening to every word." Maifailun: the same, several times._

- GIVEN the character accepted privacy and the interpreter recorded none, WHEN merged, THEN the acceptance stands
- GIVEN both recorded the privacy decision, WHEN merged, THEN it appears once as the interpreter read it
- GIVEN the scene model tagged a deed, WHEN merged, THEN only the interpreters deeds apply
- GIVEN no interpreter reading at all, WHEN merged, THEN the privacy decision still stands

### SceneFocusSelectorTests.cs

_Pins the pure avoid-repeat rule behind a captive scene's driving "focus": pick a random index into the flavour-text pool, but never the one the previous round already used._

- GIVEN a pool larger than one, WHEN picking an index, THEN it never returns the last index
- GIVEN a pool larger than one, WHEN scanning every candidate roll, THEN every index but the last one is reachable
- GIVEN a pool of one entry, WHEN picking an index, THEN it is always zero
- GIVEN an out of range last index, WHEN picking an index, THEN it is treated as no prior round
- GIVEN no prior round, WHEN picking an index, THEN any draw from the full pool is allowed

### ScheduledLetterPolicyTests.cs

_This policy is the bounded retry loop that guarantees a committed letter (today only the child-support pension due after a birth) actually gets written, without the three failure modes that make scheduled generation hard: losing it to a single ill-timed LLM outage, queuing a duplicate while one is already being written, and re-queuing a hopeless one every day forever. Each test pins one of those boundaries. The order of precedence matters as much as each rule: a letter already written must be recognised as done even past its due day, and an in-flight generation must suppress a second attempt even when attempts remain._

- GIVEN the due day has not arrived, WHEN deciding, THEN it waits
- GIVEN it is due and nothing is in flight, WHEN deciding, THEN it generates
- GIVEN today is exactly the due day, WHEN deciding, THEN it generates
- GIVEN a generation is already in flight, WHEN deciding, THEN it waits
- GIVEN the letter has already been written, WHEN deciding, THEN it is fulfilled
- GIVEN it is fulfilled at the attempt limit, WHEN deciding, THEN fulfilled wins over give up
- GIVEN the attempt limit is reached and nothing written, WHEN deciding, THEN it gives up
- GIVEN one attempt below the limit, WHEN deciding, THEN it still generates

### SchemeAdvancerTests.cs

_SchemeAdvancer decides the ONE roll that resolves a feud plot's daily tick: backfire (Failed), discovery (secret plot only, -> Discovered), or quiet progress toward Succeeded at 100. It is entirely mechanical, no LLM involved (CHANGELOG: "entirely mechanical, no AI cost"), and its state changes are what SchemeStore turns into rumours (WorldEventStore) and, on success, real standing damage (clan influence hit + relation hit)._

- GIVEN a bold and hateful plotter, WHEN reading momentum, THEN both add to the base
- GIVEN a cunning plotter against a careless target, WHEN reading discovery chance, THEN it floors at zero
- GIVEN a freshly plotted scheme, WHEN advancing, THEN it simply gets under way
- GIVEN a null scheme or null random, WHEN advancing, THEN it is a safe no op
- GIVEN a progressing scheme and a high roll, WHEN advancing, THEN it quietly gains ground
- GIVEN a progressing scheme and a low roll, WHEN advancing, THEN it backfires
- GIVEN a scheme reaching full progress, WHEN advancing, THEN it succeeds and caps at a hundred
- GIVEN a secret scheme and a roll above the discovery band, WHEN advancing, THEN it progresses instead
- GIVEN a secret scheme and a roll in the discovery band, WHEN advancing, THEN it is discovered without progress
- GIVEN a terminal scheme, WHEN advancing, THEN nothing changes
- GIVEN a watchful target and a dim plotter, WHEN reading discovery chance, THEN vigilance raises it cunning lowers it
- GIVEN an already discovered scheme, WHEN advancing, THEN it is not discovered again and races on
- GIVEN an exposed plot, WHEN reading backfire chance, THEN it is far likelier to collapse
- GIVEN an exposed scheme and a low roll, WHEN advancing, THEN counterplay collapses it
- GIVEN each lifecycle state, WHEN asking if terminal, THEN only succeeded and failed are
- GIVEN no factors, WHEN reading momentum, THEN only the base applies
- GIVEN null factors, WHEN advancing a progressing scheme, THEN it still moves on the base momentum
- GIVEN plotters of varying guile, WHEN reading backfire chance, THEN guile lowers it within bounds

### SchemeAgentPolicyTests.cs

_SchemeAgentPolicy decides the two player-facing levers on an otherwise fully mechanical, AI-free feud system (CHANGELOG: "entirely mechanical, no AI cost"): whether a plotter would loop the player in as a secret agent (CanRecruitPlayer) and what that help does (ApplyPlayerAid), and whether/how the player can warn the target (CanWarnTarget/ExposeToTarget). SchemeStore.PlayerAssists and TargetHeeds "re-validate the gate" against a live scheme before applying standing damage, planting grudges, and firing rumours, so a wrong answer here either lets a plotter confide treason in a stranger or an enemy ally, or silently denies the player the scheme_assist/scheme_heed prompt teaching they had actually earned._

- GIVEN a freshly plotted scheme, WHEN checking, THEN the player can still be recruited
- GIVEN a freshly plotted scheme, WHEN the player aids, THEN it gets under way and leaps forward
- GIVEN a null scheme, WHEN the player aids, THEN it is a safe no op
- GIVEN a null scheme, WHEN warning, THEN it is a safe no op
- GIVEN a plotter who barely knows the player, WHEN checking, THEN they will not confide the plot
- GIVEN a progressing scheme, WHEN the player aids, THEN it advances by the boost
- GIVEN a scheme near fruition, WHEN the player aids, THEN it succeeds outright and caps
- GIVEN a secret plot and a trusting plotter, WHEN checking, THEN the player can be recruited
- GIVEN a secret plot, WHEN asking if the target can be warned, THEN yes
- GIVEN a secret plot, WHEN the player warns the target, THEN it is dragged into the light
- GIVEN a terminal plot, WHEN checking, THEN there is nothing to recruit into
- GIVEN a terminal scheme, WHEN the player aids, THEN nothing changes
- GIVEN an already exposed or terminal plot, WHEN warning, THEN nothing changes
- GIVEN an already exposed plot, WHEN checking, THEN the player is not recruited into it
- GIVEN an exposed or finished plot, WHEN asking if the target can be warned, THEN no
- GIVEN regard exactly at the floor, WHEN checking, THEN it passes
- GIVEN the player is allied with the target, WHEN checking, THEN no recruitment however warm the plotter

### SchemeBacklashTests.cs

_Protects the price of meddling in a scheme: when an abetted plot's exposure turns the wronged party against the player. The rule breaks in silence — too eager and helping is suicidal, too lax and there is no risk to weigh._

- GIVEN a caught plot against a friend of another realm, WHEN pricing, THEN it is a grave betrayal
- GIVEN a caught plot against a stranger, WHEN pricing, THEN the cost is moderate
- GIVEN a caught plot against an existing enemy, WHEN pricing, THEN it stings least
- GIVEN relations exactly at the friend and enemy thresholds, WHEN pricing, THEN the tiers are inclusive
- GIVEN an abetted plot that fizzled unseen, WHEN pricing, THEN there is no cost whoever the target
- GIVEN a plot still in motion, WHEN pricing, THEN nothing is owed yet
- GIVEN a neutral plotter, WHEN computing resentment, THEN the base resentment applies
- GIVEN a merciful plotter, WHEN computing resentment, THEN they bear the betrayal with more grace
- GIVEN a vindictive calculating plotter, WHEN computing resentment, THEN the hate is at the ceiling
- GIVEN traits that would underflow, WHEN clamping, THEN the floor is twenty
- GIVEN traits that would overflow, WHEN clamping, THEN the ceiling is forty
- GIVEN a neutral target, WHEN computing gratitude, THEN the base gratitude applies
- GIVEN an honorable target, WHEN computing gratitude, THEN they feel a genuine debt
- GIVEN a calculating target, WHEN computing gratitude, THEN they discount the motive
- GIVEN a dishonorable calculating target, WHEN computing gratitude, THEN the floor holds
- GIVEN a noble and generous target, WHEN computing gratitude, THEN the ceiling holds

### ScoutTownPolicyTests.cs

_fkasad (Nexus, 01/10/2026): "Go scout a city (actually entering the city not like the vanilla where you just have to spend some time nearby). Make contact with local gang leader, negotiate with him for intel." If these rules are wrong, a lord sends the player to scout a friend's town or a castle with no gang to talk to (a task that can never complete), or the report he reads back says something the town is not._

- GIVEN an enemy town with a gang leader, WHEN named, THEN it may be scouted
- GIVEN a friendly town a castle or no gang leader, WHEN named, THEN no such task exists
- GIVEN the towns true state, WHEN reported, THEN it names the informant and says each band
- GIVEN bands the game could not read, WHEN reported, THEN they are left out

### SecretBondEligibilityTests.cs

_the ONE rule deciding whether a companion and the player may take up a hidden bond, and the reason it gives when they may not._

- GIVEN a willing companion at the bar, WHEN judged, THEN the bond may be taken up
- GIVEN adult content is off, WHEN judged, THEN the whole arc is closed
- GIVEN a romance that is over, WHEN judged, THEN it cannot be taken up again
- GIVEN they are bound to the player already, WHEN judged, THEN it says so however that bond is named
- GIVEN the bond is one short of the bar, WHEN judged, THEN it is too shallow until it reaches it
- GIVEN several doors are shut, WHEN judged, THEN the campaign wide one is named first
- GIVEN no facts at all, WHEN judged, THEN it refuses rather than assuming
- GIVEN each closed door, WHEN described, THEN the player is told which one
- GIVEN no name to use, WHEN described, THEN the sentence still reads

### SecretLoverAdmissionPolicyTests.cs

_SecretLoverAdmissionPolicy decides whether the player confessing a secret-lover bond to a given NPC reveals anything, and about whom. It gates the admit_secret_lover verb: the LLM is taught the confession ONLY when there is a genuine secret the NPC does not already embody, and on execution the knowledge ledger records exactly the subjects this NPC newly learns. Get it wrong and the mystery-lover pillar breaks its core promise: either the verb is offered when there is nothing to confess (the LLM invents a bond), or a confession made to the lover themselves pointlessly records them as "knowing" a secret they ARE._

- GIVEN the player has no secret lover, WHEN checking admissibility, THEN nothing is revealed
- GIVEN a secret lover who is not the listener, WHEN checking, THEN that subject is revealed
- GIVEN the only secret lover is the listener, WHEN checking, THEN nothing is revealed
- GIVEN several secret lovers one being the listener, WHEN checking, THEN only the others are revealed
- GIVEN a blank subject id, WHEN checking, THEN it is ignored
- GIVEN a null subject collection, WHEN checking, THEN nothing is revealed and it does not throw

### SecretLoverLeakPolicyTests.cs

_SecretLoverLeakPolicy gates when a secret lover's EXISTENCE may begin to leak as anonymous gossip (mystery-lover pillar). The whole point of the design is that the bond stays GENUINELY secret for a while first (Gabriel: a delay, not an instant leak): if this let a leak roll fire immediately, a companion's secret would be gossip the same day it began, and the original "kept discreet" promise would be broken outright._

- GIVEN a secret still inside its delay, WHEN checking, THEN it is not eligible to leak
- GIVEN a secret that has outlasted the delay, WHEN checking, THEN it is eligible to leak
- GIVEN a secret that already leaked, WHEN checking, THEN it is not eligible again
- GIVEN no recorded onset, WHEN checking, THEN it is not eligible

### SecretPropagationPolicyTests.cs

_SecretPropagationPolicy is the numeric contract for how a held secret-lover identity trickles from a knowing lord to his friends (mystery-lover phase 3b-2). It decides who is a friend close enough to be told. Get the floor wrong and the secret either never spreads (only bosom friends qualify, so it freezes with the one person told) or spreads to every passing acquaintance at once (a broadcast, not a trickle). The daily chance being low is what keeps the spread gradual; these pin the friendship boundary that gates who is even eligible to hear it._

- GIVEN relation at the friend floor, WHEN checking, THEN it is a friend
- GIVEN relation just below the floor, WHEN checking, THEN it is not a friend
- GIVEN a negative relation, WHEN checking, THEN it is not a friend

### SecretReactionPolicyTests.cs

_Documents what a lord does with an intercepted letter that came into their hands (courier Inc 4b). Gabriel: the content provokes an action "in proportion to the secret". The model names in one word how the letter touches the reader; these tests pin how that word is read and what each severity sets in motion, so a harmless letter never costs the player anything and a plot read by its target always does._

- GIVEN the models word, WHEN read, THEN it names the severity
- GIVEN an answer that names nothing, WHEN read, THEN it is nothing
- GIVEN an answer naming two severities, WHEN read, THEN the gravest wins
- GIVEN a letter that does not harm the reader, WHEN they react, THEN nothing more happens
- GIVEN a letter against the readers interests, WHEN they react, THEN they resent its author
- GIVEN the player plotted against the reader, WHEN they react, THEN they confront the player
- GIVEN another lord wrote the plot, WHEN the reader reacts, THEN the player pays nothing

### SeductionEligibilityPolicyTests.cs

_This policy is the first gate of the Stolen Love pillar: whether a married NPC can be seduced by the player at all, before anything about breaking the marriage begins. Increment 1's seduction entry point consumes the Seducible/Reason verdict directly; Increment 2's estrangement pacing is meant to read the Difficulty to decide how long an affair takes to surface. Get the hard regard floor wrong and the path becomes a cheat that can steal ANY spouse regardless of personality; get the honour/devotion weighting wrong and either the feature never fires for anyone whose spouse isn't already loathed, or it turns every marriage in Calradia into a lock waiting for a big enough regard number._

- GIVEN no facts, WHEN evaluating, THEN it refuses without throwing
- GIVEN regard below the floor, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is refused for regard alone
- GIVEN regard one point short of the floor, WHEN evaluating, THEN only the floor case names regard
- GIVEN a devoted and honorable spouse, WHEN evaluating at high regard, THEN it is refused for devotion or honor
- GIVEN a neutral marriage but maximum honor, WHEN evaluating, THEN it names honor as the blocker
- GIVEN an unhappy marriage and high regard and low honor, WHEN evaluating, THEN it is seducible
- GIVEN a borderline case, WHEN children are shared, THEN a seducible case becomes refused
- GIVEN a borderline case, WHEN the marriage is a decade old, THEN a seducible case becomes refused
- GIVEN a borderline case, WHEN the npc is highly calculating, THEN a refused case becomes seducible
- GIVEN a barely passing case and a wide open one, WHEN evaluating, THEN the barely passing case is harder

### SelfEsteemClimbTests.cs

_fkasad (Nexus, 01/10/2026): "I'm not sure that any little battle won like fighting half a dozen looters should grants self-esteem. Vacuum the house and take out the trash shouldn't grant so much. Almost everyone in my faction is at 100 just by doing chores during peace time... it should get harder beyond a certain score. Or perhaps the classic polynomial progression/diminishing returns/S-Curve rather than a fixed added value." Gabriel: a slider in MCM to calibrate it. If these rules are wrong, every lord who clears looters struts at the top of the scale again, a real victory against the odds counts no more than a skirmish, or a man brought low cannot climb back._

- GIVEN a handful of bandits he outnumbered, WHEN he wins, THEN his pride does not move
- GIVEN who he beat, WHEN he wins, THEN the credit follows what it was worth
- GIVEN how high he already stands, WHEN a gain lands, THEN it shrinks toward the top
- GIVEN a proud lord and a humbled one, WHEN each wins, THEN only the humbled one climbs much
- GIVEN a proud lord, WHEN he loses, THEN the loss lands whole

### SelfEsteemPolicyTests.cs

_Self-esteem, increment E1 (ROADMAP, SELF-ESTEEM, ruled 30/09/2026). Lordfadooboo (Nexus, 29/09/2026): "a lord who has been humbled regularly might have it lowered, and one who is doing well might have it higher. starting self esteem could be affected by station as well... randomized by character... vanilla personality traits might also play a part... determining whether a character is easier to make feel worse or... better." Maifailun: "regressing to traumas (too long captive wouldn't be so bold when talking)". If these rules are wrong, every lord carries himself alike whatever befell him, or one defeat breaks a man for good, or a king and a hedge knight start in the same place._

- GIVEN a ruler and a man of no house alike otherwise, WHEN their baselines are set, THEN the ruler starts higher
- GIVEN two men of one station, WHEN their personal draws differ, THEN they start apart and each is stable
- GIVEN a valorous and a timid man of one station, WHEN set, THEN the valorous starts higher
- GIVEN a lost battle and a won one, WHEN recorded, THEN the loss lowers and the win raises
- GIVEN a man captured again and again, WHEN captured once more, THEN it lands harder than the first time
- GIVEN a valorous man, WHEN he loses a battle, THEN it costs him less than a timid one
- GIVEN a calculating man, WHEN he wins or loses, THEN he swings less than a plain one
- GIVEN a shift left alone, WHEN time passes, THEN it drifts back toward nothing and never past it
- GIVEN an old shift and a new captivity, WHEN recorded, THEN the old mends the new lands and the captivity is remembered
- GIVEN a captivity long past, WHEN the next is recorded, THEN the old one no longer compounds it
- GIVEN values across the range, WHEN banded, THEN each reads as its word
- GIVEN a man of no house captured three times lately, WHEN read, THEN he is not steady

### ServiceOfferPolicyTests.cs

_A ServiceOffer letter dangles ONE enticement (gold / a castle / troops) chosen by the poaching lord's means._

- GIVEN a lord with a spare fief, WHEN choosing, THEN a castle is offered even if they also lead troops
- GIVEN no fief but a field party, WHEN choosing, THEN troops are offered
- GIVEN neither a fief nor a party, WHEN choosing, THEN gold is the fallback
- GIVEN the accept warmup, WHEN read, THEN it is a small positive nudge
- GIVEN a player who serves no realm, WHEN the premise is written, THEN it says so and asks them to leave no one
- GIVEN a player serving a realm, WHEN the premise is written, THEN it names the realm they would leave
- GIVEN the realm the player left a month ago, WHEN its ruler would write, THEN they stay silent
- GIVEN the realm the player left long ago, WHEN its ruler would write, THEN they may
- GIVEN a realm the player never left, WHEN its ruler would write, THEN nothing holds them back

### SessionHistoryComposerTests.cs

_This composer rebuilds one participant's ChatSession from the shared attributed transcript before every LLM call, so what it decides IS the conversation as the model receives it. Get it wrong and the model either loses track of who said what, or reads its own past output in a shape it is not asked to produce._

- GIVEN a plain exchange, WHEN composing, THEN each entry stays its own message
- GIVEN a line from another participant, WHEN composing, THEN it is replayed attributed to them
- GIVEN the speakers own run contains narration, WHEN composing, THEN the run becomes one tagged turn
- GIVEN narration was emitted first, WHEN composing, THEN the rebuilt turn keeps that order
- GIVEN the speakers own run has no narration, WHEN composing, THEN it is left untagged and unfused
- GIVEN narration belongs to another participant, WHEN composing, THEN it is not folded into this turn
- GIVEN entries are excluded, WHEN composing, THEN the last ones are left out
- GIVEN an excluded entry would extend a run, WHEN composing, THEN it is still left out
- GIVEN the speaker has no hero id, WHEN composing, THEN no line is claimed as their own
- GIVEN no transcript, WHEN composing, THEN the session is empty
- GIVEN an out of range exclusion, WHEN composing, THEN it is clamped

### SettingEnforcersCoverageTests.cs

_cr.testall settings_ranges_agree drives every integer MCM slider end to end and fails on one it does not know._

- GIVEN every integer slider in the menu, WHEN the testall table is read, THEN each has its line

### SettingProfileTests.cs

_Protects the setting-override resolution — each field falls back to its Calradia default when the override leaves it blank, so an unmodified install is unchanged and an overhaul pack only sets what it needs._

- GIVEN no override, WHEN resolved, THEN the full calradia defaults apply
- GIVEN only the world name overridden, WHEN resolved, THEN the rest stays default
- GIVEN a full overhaul override, WHEN resolved, THEN every field is taken
- GIVEN blank or empty fields, WHEN resolved, THEN they fall back to defaults
- GIVEN no calendar override, WHEN resolved, THEN it is Calradia
- GIVEN a world with its own calendar, WHEN resolved, THEN the override is taken
- GIVEN the game runs a shorter year and no override, WHEN resolved, THEN the games calendar is taken
- GIVEN an override and the games calendar, WHEN resolved, THEN the override wins field by field
- GIVEN the games calendar is not yet set, WHEN resolved, THEN Calradias holds
- GIVEN only one of the two, WHEN resolved, THEN the other keeps its default
- GIVEN a nonsense calendar, WHEN resolved, THEN Calradia is kept

### SettlementAudienceSafetyPolicyTests.cs

_Guards SettlementAudienceSafetyPolicy.IsSafeForPrivateWord, the entry gate CompanionAudienceTopicBehavior applies before offering a "private word" popup (and, through the same entry point, the fief-audience pass)._

- GIVEN a peaceful entry, WHEN gating the audience, THEN the offer may fire
- GIVEN a settlement under siege, WHEN gating the audience, THEN the offer is blocked
- GIVEN a raid in progress, WHEN gating the audience, THEN the offer is blocked
- GIVEN enemy territory at war, WHEN gating the audience, THEN the offer is blocked
- GIVEN a player not openly admitted at the gate, WHEN gating the audience, THEN the offer is blocked
- GIVEN any combination of hostilities, WHEN gating the audience, THEN the offer is blocked

### SharedIntimacyPolicyTests.cs

_Intimacy that happened in another mod. tashmetu (Nexus, 08/10/2026): "Affairs of Calradia's nights reach your characters through my bridge as Intimacy memories, but they don't touch your romantic status, so your consent threshold never learns they happened (Maifailun, playing a Casanova: several nights, regard still 0)". He asked to count such a night, and to read CR's verdict so his mod opens "spend the night" only when CR's chat would allow it. Gabriel (08/10/2026) ratified both, on one condition: a plain memory never unlocks the romance arc (any mod could write one), only an explicit call, held to the very bar the chat holds, and recorded as if the night had happened in the chat._

- GIVEN a night the chat would have allowed, WHEN recorded, THEN it counts
- GIVEN a night below the bar the chat holds, WHEN recorded, THEN it is refused
- GIVEN romance off or a bad call, WHEN recorded, THEN it is refused for that reason

### SharingRankFloorTests.cs

_that a partner whose own culture accepts the arrangement cannot be ground into hatred by it._

- GIVEN a partner whose people accept it, WHEN slighted again and again, THEN they cool but never hate
- GIVEN a partner still warm, WHEN slighted, THEN the slight costs exactly what it should
- GIVEN a partner nearly cooled, WHEN slighted, THEN the fall stops at the floor
- GIVEN a partner already cold, WHEN slighted again, THEN it costs nothing further
- GIVEN a partner below the floor, WHEN slighted, THEN nothing is given back
- GIVEN anything that is not a loss, WHEN measured, THEN the rule leaves it alone

### ShippedGuidelinesMoodTests.cs

_One word, one meaning in the prompt. fkasad (Nexus, 05/10/2026): the shipped guidelines carried a section "=== CONTEXT AND MOOD ===" about the conversation's TONE, and the prompt later carries "YOUR DAY", the character's own MOOD; "Is there any risk of confusion there?" There was: a short-tempered day read beside "respect the mood" made a lord who came to thank the player curt. The guidelines now speak of the conversation's tone, and "mood" is the day's._

- GIVEN the shipped guidelines, WHEN read, THEN no section is about mood

### ShippedHtmlToolsParseTests.cs

_The Tale Editor and the Scenario Composer ship in Docs/ as single HTML pages a player opens in a browser. Gabriel (11/10/2026): "lorsque je demarre TaleEditor.html, ca ne fonctionne pas". One unescaped apostrophe in a label ('Travelling in the player's party', added with spouse tales) was a syntax error that stopped the whole page, and nothing caught it: the parity tests run only the editor's validator under Node, never its forms. A browser drops a script with a syntax error silently, so every code script in each shipped page is parsed here._

- GIVEN a page shipped in Docs, WHEN a browser reads its scripts, THEN none has a syntax error

### ShippedNarrativeSizeTests.cs

_that the narrative files the host injects into EVERY prompt stay the size the SDK's Compact budget guard assumes they are._

- GIVEN world txt, WHEN measured, THEN it is the size the budget guard assumes
- GIVEN behavior guidelines, WHEN measured, THEN it is the size the budget guard assumes
- GIVEN the optional narrative files, WHEN measured, THEN they are still empty

### SiegeParticipationMarkerTests.cs

_Protects the match logic behind the fix for "the monarch notified me per letter of a successful siege after a few days when I was part of the force that took it": WorldEventFeeders stamps a marker when the player fights on the winning attacking side of a siege assault, and this policy decides whether that marker still applies to the settlement whose ownership is changing. Too loose and an unrelated later recapture of the same place could wrongly read as player participation; too narrow and the real reported case (the settlement-owner-changed event landing a tick after the assault) would be missed._

- GIVEN the same settlement the same day, WHEN matching, THEN it matches
- GIVEN the same settlement one day later, WHEN matching, THEN it still matches
- GIVEN the same settlement but the marker is stale, WHEN matching, THEN it does not match
- GIVEN a different settlement, WHEN matching, THEN it does not match
- GIVEN a null or empty marker, WHEN matching, THEN it does not match
- GIVEN a marker day after today, WHEN matching, THEN it does not match

### SilentRefusalMessagesTests.cs

_Player reports, 2026-08-03: a gifted item that never transferred, an NPC's "here's 1000 denars" that never arrived, an offered prisoner that stayed in the party. In every case a bridge executor hit a refusal branch (no parsable amount, an insolvent giver or player, no matching bargain) and returned a bare null instead of an ActionOutcome. A null reaches neither AppendSystemMessage (the player line) nor ActionRefusalNote (the model's out-of-character transcript note), so the LLM, having already narrated the deed as done, keeps replaying the phantom gift or payment into every later prompt while the player sees nothing at all. These strings are what the bridge now hands to ActionOutcome.Refused(...) on those branches instead, so both sides of the conversation learn the deed did not happen._

- GIVEN give gold had no valid sum, WHEN building the message, THEN it is a parenthetical in character note
- GIVEN the giver has no gold, WHEN building the message, THEN it names the giver and denies the gift
- GIVEN take gold had no valid sum, WHEN building the message, THEN it is a parenthetical in character note
- GIVEN the player has no coin, WHEN building the message, THEN it is a parenthetical in character note
